| `CSI X` | ECH erase chars | done — under ERM reset, a guarded position among the `n` is spared, as ED and EL spare one. Under ERM set it is erased and stays guarded (the `ESC V` row). **The erase's colour is DECECM's** (the `?117` row): the current background, every other attribute cleared, at power-on, the screen background once set, which is `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s empty rendition (ED, EL, pp. 5-154 and 5-156; ECH's "attributes set to normal", p. 5-152). Under DECECM reset an erased position keeps its background and loses every other attribute, reverse and the foreground included: `[[source: vt520-rm]]`'s ED, EL and ECH "clear all character attributes", as ghostty, foot and alacritty keep the background alone (docs/record.md, "An erase clears every attribute but the background"). **Since 2026-10-21 it clears the Sixel and ReGIS pixels in the positions it erases** (the Sixel row; `each_erase_clears_the_sixel_in_its_positions`, `each_erase_clears_the_page_in_its_positions`). |
| `CSI Z` | CBT backward tabulation | done |
| `CSI Pn Y` | CVT cursor line tabulation | done — since 2026-10-19, to the line of the n-th following line tabulation stop, the column kept (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.23; `cvt_counts_its_stops`). When the stops run out first, the bottom margin inside the scrolling region and the last line outside it, as HT stops at the right margin (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` HT note 1), and it never scrolls (Jev, BOTTOM, 0.96/0.90; `cvt_past_the_last_stop_goes_to_the_bottom_margin`) |
| `CSI Ps W` | CTC cursor tabulation control | done — since 2026-10-19, every parameter in order (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.17, "Notation: (Ps...)"): 0 and 1 set a character stop at the cursor and a line stop on its line, 2 to 6 clear what TBC 0, 1, 2, 3 and 4 clear, any other value counted (`ctc_sets_and_clears_both_kinds`, `ctc_walks_every_parameter`) |
| `CSI ? 5 W` | DECST8C tab stops every eight columns | done — since 2026-10-19, every stop cleared and one set at column 9 and every eighth after (`[[source: vt510-decst8c]]`; `decst8c_sets_a_stop_every_eight_columns`). Only the form the texts give: `CSI ? W` and any other value are counted, where xterm also takes a bare or 0 parameter (Jev's lean, FIVE_ONLY, 0.74/0.90; `decst8c_takes_only_five`) |
| ``CSI ` `` | HPA char position absolute | done |
| `CSI a` | HPR char position relative | done — to "the last position on the line" (`[[source: vt510-hpr]]`) whatever the side margins (`hpr_passes_the_right_margin`), as xterm's `CursorSet` and ghostty; under DECOM confined (the `?6` row). No DEC algorithm has an HPR: open for Jev |
| `CSI j` | HPB char position backward | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can move the cursor back a number of columns with ECMA-48's own control**: "moved by n character positions ... in the direction opposite to that of the character progression" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.58, `CSI Pn 06/10`, default 1; `hpb_moves_back_n_columns`). No DEC text and no ctlseqs entry has it, so it mirrors HPR: to the line's first position whatever the side margins (`hpb_passes_the_left_margin_as_hpr_passes_the_right`), confined under DECOM (`under_decom_hpb_and_vpb_stop_at_the_margins`), no reverse wrap (`hpb_does_not_reverse_wrap`). From a pending wrap it counts from the last column, where the cursor stands, and the wrap is gone; CUB counts the wrap as its first step and lands a column further right (`from_a_pending_wrap_hpb_counts_from_the_last_column`). Counted and dropped until then |
| `CSI b` | REP repeat preceding char | done — **after every writer**: a run of UTF-8 text the bulk writer took and a character the cell table read now leave REP what they wrote, where sixteen UTF-8 `Ä` and `CSI 2 b` wrote nothing until 2026-10-19 (`rep_repeats_the_last_character_of_every_writer`, `rep_repeats_a_character_the_table_writes`, the property atom `rep`). **A copy made after GL's set changed is the character as drawn, counted as the code the original was received as**: German `[` drawn `Ä`, `ESC ( B`, `CSI 2 b` draws two more `Ä` that count 0x5B, ECMA-48 8.3.103 repeating "the preceding character", a graphic character being one "that has a coded representation" (4.2.43) (Jev's lean, AS_SHOWN, 0.67 and 0.48, then 0.35 and 0.64 after research, then 0.71 and 0.57 asked with renders, log 878-881, 886/887; foot's line drawing (`[[source: foot-csi]]`, `[[source: foot-term-reset]]`) and kitty (`[[source: kitty-screen-c]]`) keep the character, ghostty re-maps its code (`[[source: ghostty-terminal-9bbb9b2]]`), and xterm repeats nothing after any control (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, `doparsing`); `rep_after_a_set_change_copies_the_character_and_its_code`). After a cluster each copy is its first character (Jev's lean, BASE, 0.98 and 0.97, then 0.88 and 0.76 asked with renders; `rep_after_a_cluster_repeats_its_first_character`) |
| `CSI c` | DA device attributes | done — identifies as VT102, and adds Sixel (attribute `4`) only when the embedder opts in (`Term::set_sixel_claim`, the app's `[images] advertise_sixel`, **off by default**) **and** has provisioned image memory. Off by default because an application that reads the claim stops drawing its text fallback (for yazi, chafa's grid of block elements, box-drawing strokes and coloured spaces), and the owner held the claim back until that fallback was proven to render right -- which `baud-core/tests/image_preview.rs` now does, cell for cell, on two real chafa captures. A claim is only read by an application that decides from the DA: yazi takes a terminal brand from `TERM_PROGRAM` first, which baud sets to `baud` so an outer terminal's name does not decide it; an unprovisioned baud refuses every Sixel, so it answers `?6c` even when opted in. `the_primary_da_advertises_sixel_only_when_opted_in_and_provisioned` pins all three. **Only `Ps` 0 or omitted is answered**, since 2026-10-18: `[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.24, "With a parameter value not equal to 0, DA is used to identify the device which sends the DA", and xterm answers only `GetParam(0) <= 0`; any other first parameter gets no reply and is counted in `unsupported` (`a_da_with_a_nonzero_parameter_is_not_answered_and_is_counted`). Until then `CSI 1 c` was answered as a request. **Open departure, on the owner's veto list**: ReGIS is built and DA lists no attribute `3`, against 070-4's DA, "The rest of the parameters indicate the architectural extensions supported at the current operating level" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]`, PDF 221), its Table 1 registering "3 1-4 ReGIS Display" (PDF 223); baud lists Sixel's `4` there under its claim, and xterm sends `3` while its ReGIS is on (`[[source: xterm-reset]]` 4165-4168). `3` stays out by the owner's pick of 2026-10-15, "Keep ?6c (Recommended)" (the ReGIS row; note AW, section 7) |
| `CSI > c` | DA2 secondary device attributes | **out** — answered with silence and counted in `unsupported`. `[[source: ctlseqs]]` line 817 shapes the reply as `CSI > Pp ; Pv ; Pc c`, where `Pp` is a terminal *type* and `Pv` a firmware version. baud has neither honestly: it is not a VT220 and has no firmware, and an application parses whatever arrives as the report it asked for. That is the same refusal primary DA makes by under-claiming as a VT102, one step further. Recorded here because real capture proved it is asked for — vim sends it once in `tests/recordings/vim-unicode.raw`, and until that landed the table did not mention DA2 at all. `every_fixture_owes_the_same_reply` pins the silence *and* the count |
| `CSI > 0 q` | XTVERSION report the terminal's name and version | done — `Ps` 0, omitted included, answers `DCS > | Pt ST`, the one report baud sends that is not a CSI, as `[[source: ctlseqs]]` ("CSI > Ps q, Ps = 0 -> Report xterm name and version (XTVERSION)") shapes it; `Pt` is `baud(<version>)`, the name and the workspace version in xterm's own form, `XTerm(411)` (`[[source: xterm-version]]`), since 2026-10-16 (until then `baud`, a name with no version), and `TERM_PROGRAM_VERSION` is the same version (`xtversion_names_baud_and_its_version`, `the_environment_names_what_xtversion_reports`); any other `Ps` is unanswered (`xtversion_answers_with_a_dcs`, `xtversion_defaults_to_zero`, `an_unknown_secondary_query_is_not_answered`, `xtversion_does_not_collide_with_other_q_finals`, `baud-core/tests/xterm/keypad_version.rs`). The table had no row for it until 2026-10-08 |
| `CSI d` | VPA line position absolute | done |
| `CSI k` | VPB line position backward | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can move the cursor up a number of lines with ECMA-48's own control**: "moved by n line positions ... in a direction opposite to that of the line progression" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.159, `CSI Pn 06/11`, default 1; `vpb_moves_up_n_lines`), the column kept. It mirrors VPR: past the top margin to line 1 (`vpb_passes_the_top_margin_as_vpr_passes_the_bottom`), confined under DECOM, and a pending wrap left as VPR and CUU leave it. Counted and dropped until then |
| `CSI e` | VPR line position relative | done |
| `CSI g` | TBC tab clear | done — all six of `[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.154's values since 2026-10-19: 0 the character stop at the cursor, 1 the line stop on its line, 3 every character stop, 4 every line stop, 5 both kinds; **2, the character stops "in the active line", clears every character stop**, because baud's are the same in every line (TSM MULTIPLE; the `CSI 18` row). Any other value is counted and clears nothing (`tbc_1_clears_the_line_stop_at_the_cursor_and_nothing_else`, `tbc_2_clears_every_character_stop`, `tbc_5_clears_both_kinds`, `tbc_with_a_value_ecma48_does_not_define_is_counted`). `[[source: vt510-tbc]]` and xterm keep 0 and 3, "There are only two values for Ps"; ECMA-48's set was decided by Jev (BUILD_ECMA, 0.98/0.94) |
| `CSI h` | SM set mode | done — LNM and IRM, and ERM, KAM and SRM (their rows, below). **Every mode in the list is set, in order**: `CSI 20 ; 4 h` sets LNM and IRM, as `[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.125 gives the notation `(Ps...)` and xterm's `ansi_modes` walks every parameter; a mode baud does not act on, a hole and a bare `CSI h` are each counted in `unsupported_param`. Until 2026-10-19 only the first parameter was read (`sm_sets_every_mode_in_its_list`, `an_unrecognised_mode_in_the_list_is_counted_and_the_rest_apply`, `a_hole_in_the_list_is_counted_and_the_rest_apply`; the `sm-list` property atom) |
| `CSI 2 h` / `CSI 2 l` | KAM keyboard action mode | done — since 2026-10-19, **a program can lock the keyboard**: while set, a key sends nothing and draws nothing (`[[source: vt510-kam]]`, "The terminal ignores all keystrokes that send characters to the host"; `[[source: ecma48]]` 7.2.11). Only the keys: a focus or mouse report, the wheel's cursor keys under `?1007` (`Term::wheel_key`) and a paste go on, the "part of the manual input facilities" ECMA-48 allows (Jev, KEYS_ONLY, 1.00/1.00; `the_wheels_keys_are_neither_locked_nor_echoed`). The app's own menu shortcuts never reach `Term::key`, as VT510 exempts Set-Up, so the window can still be closed (BUILD_NOW, 0.98/0.96). Unlocked at power-on and by `RIS` and `DECSTR` ("Keyboard action KAM: Unlocked", `[[source: vt510-decstr]]`); `DECRQM` answers it (`kam_locks_the_keyboard_and_a_key_sends_nothing`, `kam_is_reported_and_unlocked_by_decstr_and_ris`). **And it is shown** since 2026-10-20: "Wait" on the indicator status line while the keyboard is locked, "the Wait indicator appears on the keyboard indicator line at the bottom of the screen" (`[[source: vt420-rm]]`, Unlocking the Keyboard; `[[source: vt510-kam]]`; `the_indicator_shows_wait_while_the_keyboard_is_locked`), a program showing the line with DECSSDT 1 (that row). With no status line, which baud powers up with, the lock is the embedder's to draw from `Term::keyboard_locked` (EXPOSE_STATE, 1.00/1.00) |
| `CSI 12 h` / `CSI 12 l` | SRM send/receive mode | done — since 2026-10-19, **reset, a typed key appears on the screen as well as going to the program**: `[[source: vt510-srm]]`, "the terminal sends keyboard characters to the host and to the screen", and the key's bytes do there what they would from the program, an arrow's `CSI A` moving the cursor (`srm_reset_draws_a_typed_key_as_well_as_sending_it`, `the_echo_of_a_sequence_is_performed`). Only the keys (Jev, KEYS_ONLY, 1.00/1.00), through a parser of the echo's own, so a key typed between the halves of a program's sequence lands on its own (OWN_STATE, 1.00/1.00; `the_echo_does_not_land_inside_a_host_sequence`), and in the protocol the terminal is in, VT52's `ESC A` for Up in VT52 (`the_echo_follows_the_terminal_into_vt52`). **Set at power-on and by `RIS`**, VT510's "Default: No local echo" and xterm's, where `[[source: ecma48]]` 7.1 recommends the reset state: every pty echoes a key itself (Jev's lean, SET_NO_ECHO, 0.80/0.72; `ris_sets_srm_and_decstr_keeps_it`); kept by `DECSTR`. Both app drivers draw the echo's frame at the key. **Open gap**: a control string the echo opens (Alt-`]` sends `ESC ]`, Alt-Shift-P `ESC P`) shares the host's string buffer |
| `CSI 18 $ p` | TSM tabulation stop mode, reported | done — since 2026-10-19, `DECRQM` answers `4`, permanently reset: a character stop is in every line, MULTIPLE, the reset state (`[[source: ecma48]]` 7.2.17), and cannot be made per-line, the one-state mode 7.1 allows ("some or all of the modes may have one state only"). `CSI 18 h` and `l` are counted (Jev, PERMANENTLY_RESET, 0.98/0.98; `tsm_is_reported_permanently_reset_and_cannot_be_set`) |
| `CSI 6 h` / `CSI 6 l` | ERM erasure mode | done — since 2026-10-19: reset (PROTECT) at power-on and after `RIS`, as `[[source: ecma48]]` 7.1 recommends, and 8.3.105 lists "set the modes into the reset state" among what RIS "may imply, if applicable": ED, EL and ECH spare a guarded position (the `ESC V` row); set (ALL), "The contents of protected as well as of unprotected areas are affected by an erasure control function" (7.2.4). DECSTR keeps it, as neither DEC's soft-reset table nor xterm's names it. DECRQM answers `1` or `2` for mode 6 (`erm_is_set_reset_and_reported`). xterm has no ERM; built by Jev's lean, 0.72/0.92, and the owner's standing rule to build a protocol whole. EA and EF, the other two functions 7.2.4 names, follow it as ED does (the `CSI O` and `CSI N` rows). |
| `CSI l` | RM reset mode | done — as SM, every mode in the list reset (8.3.106; `rm_resets_every_mode_in_its_list`) |
| `CSI m` | SGR character attributes | done — see SGR below |
| `CSI n` | DSR device status report | done — 5 and 6; others unanswered, not guessed. 6's report is from the origin under DECOM, the column from the left margin since 2026-10-13 (`cpr_under_origin_mode_counts_from_the_left_margin`) |
| `CSI x` / `CSI 1 x` | DECREQTPARM request terminal parameters | done — since 2026-10-19. A program written for a VT100 that asks for the line settings is told them: `CSI 0 x` or `CSI x` and `CSI 1 x` are answered with DECREPTPARM, `CSI sol ; 1 ; 1 ; 120 ; 120 ; 1 ; 0 x` (`[[source: vt100-ug-ch3]]`): the request plus two, no parity, eight bits, 19200 both ways, the clock multiplier 1 and no STP flags. A pty has no line speed; 120 is the highest code the VT100 table defines (Jev's lean, 0.86 and 0.80, taken after a second ask), where xterm sends 128, a code the table does not have. A `<sol>` of 2 or 3 is a report, sent to a host and not to a terminal: counted, not answered (`decreqtparm_reports_the_line_the_vt100_table_can_name`, `a_decreptparm_sent_to_baud_is_not_answered_and_is_counted`). xterm answers only while it is a VT102 or below; baud identifies as a VT102 |
| `CSI ? 996 n` | colour preference query | done — since 2026-09-29, answered `CSI ? 997 ; 1 n` (dark) or `; 2 n` (light), `[[source: contour-palette-notifications]]`'s two replies and nothing else: the class of what `OSC 10`/`11 ; ?` would answer -- the override, else the embedder's default, of the colours shown, so under `?5` a dark theme answers light (since 2026-10-11, `the_996_answer_follows_the_colours_shown`; note Y § 7) -- by WCAG relative luminance of the background against the text (note Y, decision 5; `baud_domain::ColorScheme`, its linearisation a table held to `f64` in `baud-domain/tests/color_scheme.rs`). Silent where `OSC 11` would be, on a core told no colours. The DEC private `n`s are answered in their own row (Tier 2); any other stays unsupported and counted (`other_private_dsrs_stay_unsupported`). `the_query_answers_the_class_of_the_theme`, `the_query_follows_an_applications_override`, `tmux_starting_is_answered_in_order` (a tmux capture, `tests/scheme/`) |
| `CSI r` | DECSTBM scroll region | done — an `LF`, `IND` or wrap at a region's bottom scrolls the region whatever row that is, a region ending on the last row included (`lf_at_the_bottom_of_a_region_ending_on_the_last_row_scrolls_only_the_region`: it rotated the whole screen and retired the header rows above it), and on the last row *below* a region it neither scrolls nor moves (`lf_on_the_last_row_below_a_region_does_not_scroll`), both as `[[source: foot-linefeed]]`'s `term_linefeed` and alacritty do; `baud-difftest` carries both cases. On the primary a full-width region anchored at the top sends its scrolled line to the history whatever its height (`a_top_anchored_region_retires_its_line_whatever_its_height`), as `[[source: xterm-scroll]]`, kitty and wezterm do; a region in the middle, one narrower than the screen, and the alternate screen keep nothing. **It homes to the page origin**, line 1 column 1 with DECOM reset and the margins' corner with it set (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` DECSTBM note 1, p. 5-25; `decstbm_homes_to_the_page_origin`); until 2026-10-13 it went to the region's top row either way. **An omitted or 0 bottom margin is the last line**: "default Pb: last line", and the algorithm reads a 0 as omitted (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` DECSTBM, pp. 5-25 and 5-26); "Default: Pb = current number of lines per screen" (`[[source: vt510-decstbm]]`); a default is "assumed when no explicit value, or a value of 0, is specified" (`[[source: vt100-ug-ch3]]`); "default = full size of window" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`); as xterm and ghostty read it, since 2026-10-13 (`a_zero_bottom_margin_is_the_last_line`, `a_zero_zero_region_is_the_whole_screen`, with `an_omitted_bottom_margin_is_the_last_line` the control); until then a 0 read as line 1, so `CSI 2;0 r` was parsed and dropped. **A bottom past the screen leaves the region alone**: STD 070's note 3 (p. 5-25), "this control will be ignored (not executed)", since 2026-10-13 (`a_bottom_margin_past_the_screen_leaves_the_region_alone`); until then it was clamped to the last line, as xterm and ghostty still do (pinned in `bench-others/ghostty-oracle`) |
| `CSI s` / `CSI u` | SCOSC / SCORC | done — one slot with `ESC 7` / `ESC 8`, the shown screen's, as xterm has it with DECLRMM reset (`scosc_and_decsc_share_a_slot`). **`SCORC` with nothing saved does nothing** (`scorc_without_a_save_is_inert`): its own text, ANSI.SYS's RCP (`[[source: msdos2-ansi-doc]]`), restores "the value it had when the console driver received the SCP sequence" and says nothing of a restore before one, and xterm sends `CSI u` through DECRC's `CursorRestore` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, `CASE_ANSI_RC`) onto a slot it saved at start-up. Which to follow is note AE's Q13, open. ANSI.SYS's RCP restores the position alone, where xterm's and baud's restore everything DECSC saves. **Neither takes a parameter** (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 1592-1593 and 1734; SCP `ESC [ s` and RCP `ESC [ u` in `[[source: msdos2-ansi-doc]]` 84 and 91, `[[source: mslearn-ansi-sys]]` 794-797 and `[[source: sco-osr507-screen-hw]]` 2595 and 2623): since 2026-10-14 one that is not a default makes the sequence `Unsupported`, counted, the cursor neither saved nor restored -- for `s` it is DECSLRM's shape, `CSI Pl ; Pr s`, "available only when DECLRMM is enabled" (ctlseqs 1595-1597; `[[source: dec-std-070]]` DECSLRM note 4, p. 5-27), and `CSI 5 u` is no form at all (`scosc_with_a_parameter_saves_nothing_and_is_counted`, `scorc_with_a_parameter_restores_nothing_and_is_counted`, every read size and split); until then each saved or restored. **A defaulted parameter is the bare form**: ANSI.DOC's note 1, "The default value is used when no explicit value is given or a value of zero is given", and STD 070 § 3.5.3.1 (p. 3-24), so `CSI 0 s`, `CSI ; s` and `CSI 0;0 s` save (`scosc_with_every_parameter_defaulted_saves`, `scorc_with_every_parameter_defaulted_restores`), where xterm's `only_default` (charproc.c 2220, 4899 and 4913, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`) takes only a bare or single empty one, so `CSI 0 s` and `CSI ; s` save nothing there: xterm's departure. A colon makes it `Unsupported` too, § 3.5.3.1's "the entire sequence ... shall be ignored". Whether a non-default parameter is instead ignored and the SCOSC or SCORC run, § 3.5.1.3's "ignored as if that parameter were not received", is open (`docs/record.md`, "`CSI s` and `CSI u` with a parameter save and restore nothing") |
| `CSI ? h` / `CSI ? l` | DECSET / DECRST | done — every parameter of a multi-parameter form is applied, in order (`decset_applies_its_list_in_order`) |

Private modes, one row each. A mode is **done** only when something acts on it;
for the input-side and renderer-side ones "acting" means an accessor an embedder
can read, which is the only channel a `no_std` core with no keyboard and no
renderer has.

| mode | name | status |
|---|---|---|
| `?1` | DECCKM cursor keys | done — `Term::cursor_keys_application()` |
| `?2` | DECANM — **reset enters VT52** | done — from every conformance level, the power-up Level 4 included, read against the scope of 070-0A's "only recognized in Level 1 and Level 2" (Jev's lean, ANY_LEVEL; `vt52_is_entered_from_every_level`); see [protocols/vt52.md](protocols/vt52.md) |
| `?3` | DECCOLM 132 columns | done — since 2026-10-14, **behind xterm's `?40`**, the owner's call of 2026-10-13 (`docs/record.md`, "`?3` resizes behind `?40`, and DECSCPP asks for a width"): while `?40` is reset it is ignored, as xterm's `srm_DECCOLM` is without `c132`, and counted (`Term::column_mode_refusals`; `deccolm_is_ignored_while_40_is_reset`, `a_deccolm_at_power_on_leaves_the_screen`). Allowed, it is `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s `set_column_mode` and `reset_column_mode` (p. 5-72): the screen cleared unless `?95` is set, and with it every line single-width (`decaln_ris_and_deccolm_reset_every_line`), the top, bottom, left and right margins reset, the cursor at 1;1 whatever DECOM says, "even if the terminal was already in the selected state" (p. 5-71), and the width asked for, 132 or 80 (`Term::requested_width`; `deccolm_under_40_clears_resets_both_margin_pairs_homes_and_asks`, `deccolm_acts_again_in_the_state_it_selects`, `deccolm_under_origin_mode_homes_to_one_one`, `the_homes_under_origin_mode_go_to_the_origin`). `DECRQM` answers the bit; XTSAVE keeps it whatever `?40` says and XTRESTORE acts only under it, as DECSET does (`xtrestore_of_3_acts_only_under_40`). **The resize is the embedder's**: under `WidthHold::On` a read stops at the request, so the bytes after it land at the width asked for (`a_held_read_lands_the_rest_at_the_width_asked_for`, every read size and split), and they go on in the protocol the request's sequence left: a DECANM beside it, `CSI ? 2 ; 3 l` or `CSI ? 3 ; 2 l`, takes the rest to VT52 (`decanm_beside_a_held_deccolm_resumes_in_vt52`), and XTRESTORE of `?2;3`, which cannot replay `?2`, to ANSI (`xtrestore_of_2_and_3_holds_and_resumes_in_ansi`); the macOS app takes the columns, keeps its rows and then sizes the window to them (`baud-app`'s `width.rs`; `honour_resizes_inside_the_read_and_keeps_the_rows`, `a_program_width_lays_out_to_its_columns`), and refuses under `BAUD_GRID`; the Linux driver keeps its window and says so once (`a_request_is_decided_once_and_a_refusal_warns_once`). **And DECLRMM reset**, the VT510's "DECCOLM resets vertical split screen mode (DECLRMM) to unavailable" (`[[source: vt510-deccolm]]`), so `CSI s` is SCOSC again; STD 070's algorithm sets `left_right_margins_mode = SETABLE` instead, and xterm leaves the mode and calls that line an error -- the texts disagree, an open question in the record. **partial** until 2026-10-14: the side margins stayed, and nothing asked for the width. Its clear, on the primary narrower than its rows hold, clears what the rows held past the edge as `ED 2` does (`deccolm_while_narrow_clears_what_its_rows_held_past_the_edge`, since 2026-10-14). **Since 2026-10-21 its clear takes the pictures too**: a Sixel and the ReGIS page lose their pixels on every row, as under `ED 2`, and the kitty placements on the screen go, as kitty's own DECCOLM set does, and on reset too, since DECCOLM clears the screen either way (Jev's lean, 0.84 and 0.86; `deccolm_s_clear_frees_a_sixel`, `deccolm_s_clear_clears_the_regis_page`, `deccolm_s_clear_deletes_a_kitty_placement`) |
| `?4` | DECSCLM smooth scroll | done — `Term::smooth_scroll()` |
| `?5` | DECSCNM reverse video | done — recorded by the core (`Term::reverse_video()`) and **drawn** since 2026-09-25. **Every cell draws with its foreground and background exchanged, an explicit `SGR` colour included**, since 2026-10-14, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-13 between that and xterm's palette swap: the mode "will affect which attribute bits from display memory are used to render the text foreground and background color" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` § 5.6.3.2, page 5-107, PDF 362). Bold brightens the foreground's index before the exchange, a cell's own `SGR 7` exchanges again, so it draws as on a normal screen, and faint dims the drawn text after both; no palette entry moves, and an `SGR 58` underline colour is drawn as set (`baud_domain::Video::drawn`). The contrast floor, `app_backgrounds = "opaque"` and the picture finder read the pair as drawn; under `?5` the finder, on by default, finds btop and cava as pictures (`under_reverse_video_only_btop_and_cava_draw_a_picture`), and whether it should read the drawn background is open for the owner (the record, "`?5` exchanges every cell's colours"). Until then baud drew xterm's `ReverseVideo` (util.c 3269-3345, `[[source: xterm-scroll]]`'s file), which swaps the defaults and palette entries 0 and 7 and 8 and 15 and leaves every other colour. The margin, scrollbar, cursor trail and screen fade take the default pair as a default cell shows it, on the frame that flips. Every background, the reversed default one included, keeps the window's alpha -- the owner's call. `baud-render/tests/reverse_video.rs` (`every_cell_exchanges_its_colours_explicit_ones_included`, `the_canvas_paints_each_cell_exchanged`, `a_picture_is_found_by_the_background_drawn`), and the pixel oracle's `?5` scenes, cells and picture. **A `?5h` and `?5l` in one read are drawn too**, since 2026-09-25: on macOS `tput flash` sleeps its `$<100/>` and writes both in one burst (measured through a pty), so the mode reads as it did and no frame would show the flip. The core counts the changes -- the ones made where a frame could show them (`Term::reverse_flips`) apart from the ones made inside `?2026` (`Term::reverse_flips_held`), which a driver shows only once it stops honouring an update left open -- a frame that finds a count moved with the mode unchanged shows the other video, and the flash ends on the loop's own clock after the entry's padding: the `$<100/>` of the pinned `xterm-256color.ti`, parsed at build time by `baud_domain::terminfo` into a bounded `Delay` (`baud-core/tests/reverse_flips.rs`, `baud-app/src/bell.rs`, `baud-render/tests/video_flash.rs`, the pixel oracle's flash scene). Under the config's `bell = "flash"` a `BEL` shows the same flash on macOS, counted apart so a frame planned before it still sees it (`Bell::ring`, `a_ring_flashes_from_the_next_frame`, `a_ring_between_plan_and_commit_is_not_lost`; the `BEL` row). Each driver times an update from its own opening (`UpdateClock`, restarted by `Term::updates_opened`). The drivers' wiring -- the plan, the commit and the refusal, the hold, the due check and the Linux `poll` timeout -- is established by reading, since no test drives the loop, and a flash was not watched on screen. vim's `set vb` and bash's `bell-style visible` write the two halves 102 ms and 110 ms apart, in two reads, and frames draw each -- by reading the frame loop; the app was not watched doing either. **An `OSC 10`/`11` set, query or reset *while* reversed acts on the colour shown** since 2026-10-11 (note V, V17): a default cell drawn exchanged shows the stored background as its text and the stored foreground as its page, so baud maps the role to the stored slot through `baud_domain::Video`, the one table the renderer draws by -- `OSC 10` names the stored background and `OSC 11` the foreground, where xterm's handlers land on its swapped tables (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3568-3750) -- and a reply keeps the code asked (`baud-core/tests/reverse_osc.rs`, `a_dynamic_colour_set_under_reverse_draws_in_the_role_it_named`). Under the exchange the colour an `OSC 10` sets while reversed is also the text of every cell without `SGR 7` whose background is the default, an `SGR 31` cell's included (`osc_10_and_11_under_reverse_reach_an_exchanged_cell`). Before 2026-10-11, an `OSC 11` under `?5` moved the foreground drawn and its query answered the text colour. `CSI ? 996 n` follows, and `?5` sends no `997` (note Y § 7). **An `OSC 4` or `OSC 104 ; c` sent while reversed names the entry drawn**, since no entry moves under the exchange (`a_palette_override_is_drawn_where_its_entry_is_named`). **An `OSC 12` cursor colour equal to the text moves to the background on each change of `?5`** since 2026-10-11, as xterm's `ReverseVideo` does before its swap (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]` util.c 3293-3295) and `ReverseOldColors` to the copy `OSC 12 ; ?` answers (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 6418-6431): every flip goes through `flip_reverse_video` -- `DECSCNM`, XTRESTORE and `RIS`, as each of xterm's calls `ReverseVideo` -- and the rule is `baud_domain::Video::cursor_across`. An unset cursor stays unset, being drawn in the text already, and so does one whose page a core without embedder colours cannot name. A flash draws the colour the flip would leave (`Term::cursor_color_in`, read by the renderer into `Renderer::block_paint`, which the pixel oracle's cell arm and the app's macOS cell path draw from; the oracle's `?5` scene flashes with an `OSC 12` equal to the text). `baud-core/tests/reverse_cursor.rs`, `a_flash_draws_the_cursor_colour_a_flip_would_leave`, `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws` |
| `?6` | DECOM origin | done — since 2026-10-13 the origin is "(Top Margin, Left Margin)" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` DECOM, p. 5-31): `?6h` homes there and `?6l` to line 1, column 1 (`setting_origin_mode_homes_to_the_top_and_left_margins`), `DECSTBM` and `DECSLRM` home to it (`decstbm_homes_to_the_page_origin`, `decslrm_homes_to_the_page_origin`), `CUP`/`HVP` count from it and stop at the right margin (CUP, p. 5-50; `cup_under_origin_mode_addresses_from_the_left_margin`), and `CPR` reports from it (p. 5-54; `cpr_under_origin_mode_counts_from_the_left_margin`). **And the cursor "cannot be moved above the Top Margin or left of the Left Margin"** (p. 5-31; `[[source: vt510-decom]]`, "cannot move outside of the margins"), since the same day for columns as for rows: `CR`, `NEL`, `CNL`, `CPL` and `LF` under `LNM` go to the left margin, `CUF`, `HPR`, `CUB`, `HT`, `CHT`, `CBT`, `CHA` and `HPA` stop at the margins, `DECRC`, `SCORC` and `?1049 l` clamp what they restore when the restored mode is displaced (`restore_cursor`, p. 5-190), a column on the right margin that a narrower `DECSLRM` leaves level with the new right margin + 1 included (`a_right_margin_column_restored_under_a_narrower_margin_is_clamped`), `DECCOLM` homes to the origin, which is line 1, column 1 once it has reset the margins, and `DECALN` resets the mode and the margins and homes there (`?1049 h` did until 2026-10-13, and leaves the cursor since: the `?1049` row), and VT52's moves keep it, each followed by a `CPR` (`tests/vt420/origin.rs`); until then `CPR` answered column 1 for a cursor left of the origin. `CHA`/`HPA` count from the screen's column 1 and are clamped, option (a'), where xterm counts from the left margin: no text settles which, listed for Jev (`docs/record.md`, "DECOM's origin is the left margin too"). Held against ghostty by the oracle's `decom:` fixtures, with ghostty's `CUB`, `DECRC` and `CHA`/`HPA` pinned |
| `?7` | DECAWM autowrap | done — the last column is the right margin from at or inside it and the screen's from right of the fence, `[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-139, "will not advance beyond the Right Margin or last column of the Active Line" (`without_autowrap_right_of_the_fence_writes_to_the_last_column`; the `CSI s` row) |
| `?8` | DECARM auto-repeat | done — set at power-on, as `[[source: vt510-decarm]]` gives it ("Default: Repeat (set)"); `Term::key` drops a repeat while it is reset, on every layer. xterm ignores the mode (its `srm_DECARM` case: "ignore autorepeat"). `DECSTR` leaves it, as DEC's DECSTR table and xterm's soft reset both do. `a_repeat_is_dropped_while_autorepeat_is_reset` |
| `?12` | cursor blink | done — since 2026-09-27 **a second input to the blink, *or*-ed with the style's own**, foot's rule (`[[source: foot-term-reset]]`'s file): `?12h` blinks a steady style and `?12l` cannot stop one that blinks, so the `?12h ?12l` vim sends at start and every `cnorm` leave a configured blink blinking. DECRQM answers the `?12` bit, reset at power-on whatever the style draws, as foot and xterm answer it; `DECSCUSR 0` and `7` leave it and both resets clear it. It is saved with the style at `?1049 h` and restored at `?1049 l` (`baud-core/tests/cursor_blink_rule.rs`, which replays vim's and helix's captured exits from `tests/cursor/`) |
| `?13` | xterm's `cursorBlink`, read-only | done — since 2026-10-19, **a program can ask whether the user configured a blinking cursor**: `DECRQM` answers `1` or `2` from the style the embedder configured (`Term::set_initial_cursor_style`), as xterm answers its resource while it can still change (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`, `srm_CURSOR_BLINK_OPS`; Jev's lean, ONE_OR_TWO, 0.80/0.78; note X recommends this read-only answer for the owner's sub-call of 2026-09-27). DECSET and DECRST are counted, "set only via resource or menu" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`; `the_read_only_and_permanent_modes_do_not_move`) |
| `?14` | xterm's `cursorBlinkXOR`, read-only | done — since 2026-10-19: `DECRQM` answers `4`, permanently reset, because baud ORs `?12` with the style's blink (rule F) and never XORs; DECSET and DECRST are counted (`each_mode_answers_what_baud_does_at_power_on`) |
| `?18` | DECPFF print form feed | done — since 2026-10-20: "When Print Form Feed Mode is set, a Form Feed (FF) control character is transmitted to the printer port at the conclusion of each Print Page or Print Screen operation" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-7 p. 7-37), and not after a print line ("DECPFF does not affect the print cursor line function", `[[source: vt420-rm]]` p. 211); the MC row. Off at power-on and **reset by `RIS`**, "Default: No form feed", **kept by `DECSTR`**, whose list names it nowhere; set and reset with no printer, as a mode needs no printer port. DECRQM answers `1` or `2`, as xterm's `MdBool(PrinterOf(screen).printer_formfeed)` (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 5544-5545); XTSAVE and XTRESTORE carry it, as xterm's `savemodes` does. Counted as unsupported before (`decpff_and_decpex_answer_decrqm_and_reset_with_ris`, `print_screen_ignores_decpex_and_decpff_adds_a_form_feed`) |
| `?19` | DECPEX print extent | done — since 2026-10-20: set, print page prints the whole page; reset, "only the area of the display within the scrolling region (Top Margin to Bottom Margin inclusive)" (070-7 p. 7-38), and print line only DECSLRM's margins (PRINT LINE); the MC row. **Reset at power-on and by `RIS`**, a source conflict (below), kept by `DECSTR`; DECRQM, XTSAVE and XTRESTORE as `?18`. Counted as unsupported before (`print_page_prints_the_scrolling_region_unless_decpex_is_set`, `print_line_prints_the_cursor_line_and_decpex_picks_the_margins`) |
| `?25` | DECTCEM cursor visible | done |
| `?30` | show scrollbar (rxvt) | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can hide baud's scrollbar and show it again**: "Show scrollbar (rxvt)" and "Don't show scrollbar (rxvt)" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`). Hidden, the macOS app draws none and nothing takes a press there, a drag in flight dropped; the overlay style's strip is cells, and the legacy style's gutter goes, the window narrowing by its width so the grid keeps its columns, as xterm's `ScrollBarOff` resizes its window through `DoResizeScreen` (`[[source: xterm-scrollbar-c]]` 101, 563-585), or, in full screen or with `[window] operations` off, the grid taking the columns (`a_scrollbar_change_resizes_the_window_where_it_may`, `a_hidden_scrollbar_keeps_no_gutter`; Jev's lean, AS_XTERM, 0.74/0.78 and 0.74/0.74 after a second ask). Shown at power-on, since baud has a scrollbar, where xterm's default has none, and `DECRQM` answers it as xterm's answers whether one is shown (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 5553-5554; `the_scrollbar_is_shown_at_power_on`; Jev's lean, SET, 0.36/0.64 and 0.50/0.70). Kept by both resets, as xterm's, the scrollbar being the window's; XTSAVE keeps it (`resets_keep_it_and_xtsave_restores_it`; Jev's lean, KEPT, 0.90/0.74 and 0.92/0.58). The wheel still scrolls the history. **The Linux app draws a thumb too** since 2026-10-21, AppKit's overlay style drawn at the grid's edge, where macOS draws it at the window's edge, there being no system scroller setting to follow, and hidden by `?30` it draws none and takes no press (`a_jump_needs_a_drawn_thumb_and_a_shown_scrollbar`). Counted and dropped until 2026-10-20 |
| `?40` | allow 80 ⇄ 132 columns (xterm's `c132`) | done — since 2026-10-14: "Allow 80 -> 132 mode, xterm" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), the gate `?3` reads, reset at power-on as `Bres(XtNc132, ..., False)` gives it (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file). `DECRQM` answers it, as xterm's `MdBool(screen->c132)` does (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`); XTSAVE carries it; `RIS` resets it, ECMA-48 § 8.3.105's "set the modes into the reset state", where xterm's reset keeps the resource; `DECSTR` keeps it (`deccolm_is_ignored_while_40_is_reset`, `ris_in_an_80_column_window_asks_for_80_and_resets_the_modes`, `xtsave_and_xtrestore_carry_40_and_95`). It does not gate DECSCPP, which neither xterm nor STD 070 gates (the `CSI $ \|` row) |

CHT takes the wrap once. Nothing changes with no wrap pending or `?7` reset (`without_autowrap_the_tab_does_not_wrap`). Reset at power-on, the resource's default (`[[source: xterm-man]]`, `-cu`); `DECRQM` answers it (`decrqm_answers_the_more_fix`), XTSAVE keeps it, `RIS` resets it and `DECSTR` keeps it, as the xterm resource modes go (`xtsave_keeps_it_ris_resets_it_and_decstr_keeps_it`). Counted and dropped until then |
| `?43` | DECGEPM graphics expanded print | done — since 2026-10-21, **a program can print its pictures large**: set, a ReGIS hard copy is sent expanded, reset compressed, "Default: Compressed" (`[[source: vt3xx-gp-002]]` ch. 16), the Level 2 dump's grid 9 or 6 decipoints a pixel (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-7 § 7.8.2.4). **The texts differ on a terminal with no printer**: the VT330/VT340 manual, "If you do not have a printer connected to the terminal, the terminal ignores these functions" (ch. 16, p. 255), and 070-7 § 7.8.2.2, which sets no such condition; baud records DECGEPM either way, following 070-7, as it records DECPEX, and the VT340's ignoring is not followed. DECRQM answers `1` or `2`; compressed at power-on and after `RIS`, kept by `DECSTR`, which the VT330/VT340's Table 13-1 does not name; XTSAVE and XTRESTORE carry it. The VT340's other four graphics print modes, `?44` to `?47` (colour, colour syntax, background, rotation), are xterm's modes here (those rows), so the four are the user's `[screen] print_colour`, `print_rgb`, `print_background` and `print_rotated`, each off by default, as 070-7 § 7.8.2.1-7.8.2.3 lets the user select them (Jev's lean, ESCAPE_43_SETTINGS_REST, 0.38/0.43 then 0.31/0.61; colour off by default decided by Jev, DEFAULT_MONO, 0.98/0.98). Counted as unsupported before (`offset_size_rotation_syntax_and_background`, `decgepm_resets_on_ris_and_survives_decstr`) |
| `?44` | margin bell (xterm's `marginBell`) | done — since 2026-10-20: "Turn on margin bell, xterm" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), "whether or not the bell should be rung when the user types near the right margin. The default is "false"" (`[[source: xterm-man]]`). One of the margin bell's two switches, DECSMBV's volume the other (that row; Jev's lean, TWO_SWITCHES, 0.37/0.27 then 0.27/0.14): set alone it rings nothing until a DECSMBV 2 to 8 arrives, and reset it disarms the bell, as xterm's `bellArmed = -1`. DEC assigns `?44` to the VT340's DECGPCM, a graphics print mode (`[[source: vt3xx-gp-002]]` ch. 16), which xterm takes only while its terminal identity is a VT125, VT240, VT241, VT330 or VT340 (`optRegisGraphics`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file); baud's is a VT102, the owner's pick, so `?44` is xterm's, and colour printing is the user's `[screen] print_colour`, which 070-7 § 7.8.2.1 lets "the user ... select" (Jev's lean, ESCAPE_43_SETTINGS_REST, 0.38/0.43 then 0.31/0.61; the `?43` row). `Term::margin_bell()`. Off at power-on, **reset by `RIS`**, ECMA-48 § 8.3.105's "set the modes into the reset state", where xterm's reset keeps the resource, and kept by `DECSTR`; DECRQM answers `1` or `2`, as xterm's `MdBool(screen->marginbell)` (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 5579-5580); XTSAVE and XTRESTORE carry it. Before, it was counted as unsupported and DECRQM answered `0`. `decrqm_answers_44_and_xtsave_carries_it`, `turning_it_off_disarms_it` |
| `?45` | reverse wraparound | done — since 2026-10-20 to xterm's `CursorBack`, which defines this xterm mode (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` names it and no more; `[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]` 121-191): **backspace at the left margin goes to the end of the line above only when that line wrapped into this one**, and otherwise stays, so it walks back over a wrapped command line and not into unrelated output; **only while `?7` is set**, `REVERSEWRAP` with `WRAPAROUND`; **`CUB` walks the same way, its count included**, `CASE_CUB` calling `CursorBack` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file). No top margin bounds it while `DECOM` is reset, only an unwrapped line and the screen's first line, where xterm would read a line of its history; **under `DECOM` the walk stops at the top margin**, as origin mode confines all cursor movement to the region (`[[source: vt510-decom]]`), where xterm's `rev` path reads no `ORIGIN` and leaves it, not followed (`under_origin_mode_reverse_wrap_stops_at_the_top_margin`). A failed step from below the scrolling region leaves the cursor on the line above, xterm's `if (row < bottom) ++row` as written (Jev's lean, XTERM_UP, 0.86/0.80 then 0.76/0.78). From a pending wrap `CUB n` moves n-1 columns, counting the pending wrap as one step as X11R5's `CursorBack` did; today's xterm moves n-2 for n of 2 or more. This follows the lean taken for `?1045` (SAME_AS_CUB) and was not asked separately for `?45`; `BS` (n = 1) agrees with xterm. Until 2026-10-20 it acted without `?7`, went into any line above but not past the top margin, and left `CUB` alone (`backspace_reverse_wraps_only_into_a_wrapped_line`, `reverse_wrap_needs_autowrap`, `cub_reverse_wraps_a_count_of_columns`, `reverse_wrap_crosses_the_top_margin_into_a_wrapped_line`, `a_failed_reverse_wrap_below_the_region_goes_up_a_line`) |
| `?46` | XTLOGGING start and stop logging | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can log everything it sends to a file**, the owner's call of 2026-10-20 ("3 46 and 50 all yes", note AV): "Start logging (XTLOGGING), xterm", reset "Stop logging" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), to the file `OSC 46` names. The log is the host's bytes from the one after `?46 h` up to and including `?46 l`, as xterm's `StartLog` takes `logstart = VTbuffer->next` and `CloseLog` flushes to it (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`): the core stops a read after each change (`Node::Held`, as at a page-width request), so each part the embedder feeds was parsed under one mode, and the app writes each part fed while started (`reads::feed`, `hostlog.rs`). The app opens the file on a writer thread, never the read loop: appended, created `0644`, opened non-blocking so a named pipe with no reader fails at once, and refused unless the real user owns it and neither group nor others may write it, xterm's `open_userfile` (Jev, XTERM_CHECKS, 1.00/1.00); `-` is the app's standard output; a name beginning `|`, which xterm runs as a command only when built with `ALLOWLOGFILEEXEC`, is refused and counted (Jev, XTERM_DEFAULT_BUILD, 1.00/1.00); with no name the log is `baud.log.<host>.<yyyy.mm.dd.hh.mm.ss>.<pid>` after xterm's `Xterm.log` form, in local time as xterm's `localtime` gives it (Jev, LOCAL, 0.92/0.90; `a_generated_name_is_in_local_time`), in the directory the shell started in, where a relative name is read too (Jev's leans, GENERATED_START_DIR 0.85/0.72, START_DIR 0.86/0.58), and that name is kept for the next start with none, as xterm's `GenerateLogPath` keeps one name per process ("once opened we just reuse the same log name"), so a stop and a second unnamed start append to the first file. An open or write that fails stops the core's log (`Term::stop_log`) and is warned (Jev's lean, REQUESTED_THEN_CLEARED, 0.98/0.82); the queue to the writer holds 4 MiB; past it a part is dropped and warned once. **When the shell's exit ends the app, or on Linux the window's close does, the log is finished first**: the app ends once the writer has written every part queued before and closed the file, as xterm's `FlushLog` writes each read before the next, and gives up on a sink that stalls at the loop's first turn two seconds on, warned; on macOS, Quit in the app's menu ends the process without waiting for it. `DECRQM ?46` answers `1` or `2`, and `4` while `[screen] logging = "off"` or in a core no embedder turned it on, when `?46` and `OSC 46` are refused and counted in `Term::logs_refused` (Jev, PERMANENTLY_RESET, 0.96/1.00). XTSAVE keeps it and XTRESTORE starts or stops through it, as xterm's `restoremodes` calls `StartLog`; `RIS` and `DECSTR` leave it, as xterm's resets do (Jev, RIS_KEEPS, 0.98/0.96). VT340's Graphic Print Background Mode shares the number; baud prints no graphics (`the_log_runs_from_after_the_start_to_the_stop`, `decrqm_answers_the_mode`, `off_both_are_refused_and_counted`, `a_command_is_never_started`, `xtsave_and_xtrestore_keep_the_mode`, `a_reset_leaves_the_log`; the app's `the_log_holds_what_the_host_sent_while_started`, `a_file_xterm_would_refuse_stops_the_log`, `a_full_queue_drops_and_counts`, `a_quit_finishes_once_the_last_parts_are_written`, `a_stalled_writer_is_given_up_on_at_the_bound`, `a_generated_name_is_kept_for_the_next_start`, `the_logging_setting_reaches_the_core`). Counted as unsupported before |
| `?1045` | extended reverse wraparound (XTREVWRAP2) | done — since 2026-10-19, **backspace at the left margin wraps up, and from the top margin round to the bottom margin's line**, each time to the line's end, while `?7` is set, as xterm's `REVERSEWRAP2` acts only with `WRAPAROUND` (`[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]`, `CursorBack`; Jev's lean, WITH_DECAWM, at confidences 0.04/0.10, the first ask having leaned the other way: effectively unresolved). Above the top margin `CUB` walks up to the first line and stops at its left margin, not off the screen (`cub_under_extended_reverse_wrap_wraps_a_count_of_columns`; backspace: `extended_reverse_wrap_goes_up_and_round_from_the_top`). **`CUB` wraps the same way, its count included**, as xterm's `CursorBack` serves both: each step past the left margin goes to the end of the line above and from the top margin round to the bottom margin's line, and from left of the left margin the walk turns at the screen's first column (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`, `CASE_CUB`; `cub_under_extended_reverse_wrap_wraps_a_count_of_columns`, `cub_under_extended_reverse_wrap_from_a_pending_wrap_and_in_margins`). Within a line it moves as `CUB` does without the mode, a pending wrap included, as the original `CursorBack` that `?1045` imitates counts one (`[[source: xterm-x11r5-cursor-c]]` 81-84); today's loop takes a pending wrap off the count twice, so for a count of two or more it lands a column right, not followed (Jev's lean, SAME_AS_CUB, 0.84/0.72, after research). `DECRQM`, XTSAVE (slot 47); `DECSTR` keeps it and `RIS` resets it |
| `?47` | alternate screen, pre-1049 | done — switches buffers and does nothing else: no cursor save or restore and no clear, so the alternate screen shows what it held when last left, and the cursor stays where it is (xterm's `srm_ALTBUF`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file). `DECRQM` answers from which buffer is shown, as for `?1049` -- baud's choice, since the pinned file records `whichBuf` only for XTSAVE and xterm's `DECRQM` code is not pinned. It was marked done while routed nowhere, and its one test passed on a no-op; every test in `baud-core/tests/legacy_alt_screen.rs` fails on a no-op. `RIS` erases the parked alternate surface, which xterm does not, because the reset frees the cluster and link slots its cells name. A resize while `?47` is up anchors the primary on the one shared cursor, which `?47 l` hands back. Each screen has its own saved cursor, as xterm keeps one per buffer, `sc[screen->whichBuf]` (`[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]`), since 2026-10-20 |
| `?1046` | allow switching to and from the alternate screen | done — since 2026-10-19, set at power-on. **Reset, a program cannot switch screens**: resetting it on the alternate screen goes back to the primary as `?47 l` does, and `?47` and `?1049` then do nothing, as xterm's do under titeInhibit (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`; `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, `srm_ALLOW_ALTBUF`; `alt_screen_switching_can_be_turned_off`), and so do `?1047` and `?1048` (`modes_1047_and_1048_wait_on_1046`). `DECRQM` answers the mode's own sense, `1` while switching is allowed; xterm answers its titeInhibit bit, the inverse, a departure not followed (Jev's lean, TRUTHFUL, 0.50/0.50). XTSAVE slot 48; `DECSTR` keeps it, `RIS` sets it |
| `?1047` | alternate screen, cleared on the way out | done — since 2026-10-19. **A program that uses `?1047` gets the alternate screen, and the screen it leaves is wiped**: set switches as `?47` does, showing what the alternate screen held, and reset clears it first, "Clear the screen first if in the Alternate Screen Buffer" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), xterm's `ToAlternate(xw, False)` and `ClearScreen` before `FromAlternate(xw, False)` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, `srm_OPT_ALTBUF`). The cursor is neither saved nor restored. `DECRQM` answers which buffer is shown, as for `?47` and `?1049`; XTSAVE keeps that, slot 52, and a restore goes through DECSET's path, as `?1049`'s does (XT-6), so **an XTRESTORE that leaves the alternate screen clears it, where xterm's does not**: its `restoremodes` calls `FromAlternate(xw, False)` with no `ClearScreen` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, 8590-8600), a departure from xterm's practice. It was counted and dropped (`mode_1047_clears_the_alternate_screen_on_the_way_out`, `mode_1047_shows_the_alternate_screen_as_it_was_left`, `mode_1047_reset_on_the_primary_clears_nothing`) |
| `?1048` | save and restore the cursor | done — since 2026-10-19. Set is DECSC and reset DECRC, "Save cursor as in DECSC" and "Restore cursor as in DECRC" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), through the shown screen's slot, the one `ESC 7` and `ESC 8` use, as xterm's `CursorSave` and `CursorRestore` (`srm_SAVE_CURSOR`), so rxvt's `ESC 7` and `?47` pair brings the shell's cursor back. `DECRQM` answers whether a cursor is saved on the screen shown, xterm's `MdBool(screen->sc[screen->whichBuf].saved)` (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 5716-5717; `decrqm_1048_reads_the_shown_screens_slot`). **XTSAVE of it saves the cursor and XTRESTORE restores it**, xterm's `savemodes` and `restoremodes` (Jev, XTERM_ACTIONS, 0.80/0.88, then 0.91/0.94 with foot's and ghostty's code). Since 2026-10-20 `?1049` saves into the primary's slot, so `?1049 h`, `?47 l`, `?1048 l` restores what `?1049` saved, as xterm's one `CursorSave` serves both (`the_1049_save_is_the_primarys_decsc`). It was counted and dropped (`mode_1048_saves_and_restores_the_cursor_as_decsc_and_decrc`, `decrqm_reads_1047_and_1048`, `xtsave_and_xtrestore_of_1047_and_1048`). At power-on and after `RIS`, `DECRQM ?1048` answers `2` (reset); xterm answers `1`, because `VTRealize` saves both slots at home and its `RIS` (`ReallyReset`) saves the primary's again, an open difference |
| `?66` | DECNKM numeric keypad mode | done — since 2026-10-19, DECKPAM and DECKPNM as a mode, one state with them, so an application can ask with `DECRQM` which keypad it has (`[[source: vt510-decnkm]]`, "provided mainly for use with the request and report mode"; `decnkm_is_the_keypad_mode_and_decrqm_reads_it`). Numeric at power-on and after `DECSTR` and `RIS`; XTSAVE keeps it |
| `?67` | DECBKM backarrow key mode | done — since 2026-10-19, **set, the Backspace key sends BS; reset, the default, DEL** (`[[source: vt510-decbkm]]`), and Control inverts it, xterm's documented `backarrowKey` and `[[source: kitty-keyboard]]`'s legacy table (Jev's lean, XTERM_TOGGLE, 0.62/0.70): Control-Backspace sends BS under the default where it sent DEL. `modifyOtherKeys` reports the byte the key sends, and VT52 follows it (`decbkm_chooses_what_the_backarrow_key_sends`, `decbkm_names_the_byte_modify_other_keys_reports`). Kept by `DECSTR`, whose table names it not, reset by `RIS` (`decbkm_is_reported_kept_by_decstr_and_reset_by_ris`) |
| `?69` | DECLRMM left/right margins | done — and set, every line of both screens single-width and DECDWL and DECDHL refused until it is reset (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-29; `declrmm_resets_every_line_and_refuses_the_double_controls`) |
| `?95` | DECNCSM no clearing screen on column change | **partial** — since 2026-10-14: while set, `?3` does not clear the screen ("When enabled, a column mode change ... does not clear the screen", `[[source: vt510-decncsm]]`) and does everything else it does (`ncsm_withholds_the_clear_and_nothing_else`); DECSCPP never clears, as "This sequence does not affect the column mode change caused by the sequence, DECSCPP". Reset at power-on; `DECRQM` answers it, XTSAVE carries it, `RIS` resets it and `DECSTR` keeps it, as `[[source: vt510-decstr]]`'s table does not name it. No level gate, where xterm answers it only at its VT510 level: note AF's Q-Level. **The gap**: what it keeps is re-cut by the embedder's `Term::resize`, which reflows the primary, where a VT510 "can lose data from page memory. Columns no longer present in page memory are lost" (`[[source: vt510-decscpp]]`) -- the DECSCPP row's gap |
| `?117` | DECECM erase colour | done — since 2026-10-19. **A program can choose whether an erase paints the colour it set or the screen's own background.** "Controls the background color used when text is erased or new text is scrolled on to the screen ... Set: erase to screen background (VT) ... Reset: erase to text background (PC)" (`[[source: vt520-rm]]` p. 5-48). Reset, the factory default (Set-Up 2.9.7, p. 2-32) and the state after `RIS`, ED, EL, ECH and every line a scroll, IL, DL, ICH or DCH brings onto the screen take the current background, every other attribute cleared, the `bce` that `TERM=xterm-256color` promises (`[[source: terminfo-xterm-256color]]`); set, the default rendition. `DECSTR` keeps it (table 5-6 does not name it), `DECRQM` answers `1` or `2` and XTSAVE carries it; xterm answers `4` and never acts on it. DECSED, DECSEL and DECSERA keep each character's own rendition under either (`set_erases_to_the_screen_background`, `reset_erases_in_the_current_rendition`, `it_is_reported_kept_by_decstr_and_reset_by_ris`). Built by Jev's lean, DECECM, 0.93/0.87 and 0.91/0.85 on a re-ask (log 870-873) |
| `?1034` | eightBitInput — Alt/Meta add 128 | done — `Term::eight_bit_input()`, applied by `Term::key` under ISO 8859-1 only. What terminfo's `smm`/`rmm` send |
| `?1036` | metaSendsEscape — Meta prefixes `ESC` | done — `Term::meta_sends_escape()`, **set** at power-on |
| `?1039` | altSendsEscape — Alt prefixes `ESC` | done — `Term::alt_sends_escape()`, **set** at power-on |
| `?1035` | NumLock overrides the application keypad, xterm's `numLock` | done — since 2026-10-19, **set at power-on**, as the resource is (Jev's lean, SET, 0.66/0.58): with NumLock on a keypad key sends its digit under DECKPAM too, ctlseqs' "Use the NumLock key to override the application mode", in both ANSI and VT52 (`mode_1035_lets_num_lock_override_the_application_keypad`). **On Linux the whole keypad leaves DECKPAM**, digits, decimal point, operators and Enter: the Wayland driver reports NumLock on every keypad key, the digits too, whose keymap type consumes it, as xterm tests NumLock against the event's whole state (`num_lock_takes_every_keypad_key_out_of_the_application_keypad`); a navigation key, which produces no text, keeps DECKPAM, as xterm overrides only a key with one byte of text (`a_navigation_key_with_num_lock_keeps_the_application_keypad`). **On macOS the mode has no effect**: a Mac keypad has no NumLock key (`0x47` is `kVK_ANSI_KeypadClear`), and the driver reports none. Its Alt half is nothing here: baud's embedder reports Alt itself, and xterm 411 finds the Alt modifier whatever the resource says (Jev, NO_ALT_EFFECT, 1.00/0.94). A modifier with no ctlseqs code (a lock, Super, Hyper) adds no modifier parameter to any base-layer key (`a_lock_alone_adds_no_modifier_parameter`). Kept by `DECSTR`, set again by `RIS` |
| `?1037` | Delete key sends DEL, xterm's `deleteIsDEL` | done — since 2026-10-19: set, the editing keypad's Delete sends DEL; reset, the default, VT220 Remove, `CSI 3 ~` (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`). A modified Delete stays `CSI 3 ; m ~` and the numeric keypad's Delete is not the editing keypad's, as in xterm's input.c (`[[source: xterm-input-c]]`; `mode_1037_makes_the_editing_delete_send_del`). Kept by `DECSTR`, reset by `RIS` (`ris_returns_the_key_modes_to_power_on`) |
| `?1010` | scroll to the bottom on output (rxvt) | done — since 2026-10-19, reset at power-on, which is baud's window over the history as it was: output keeps the reader where they scrolled (the owner's V2). **Set, a read from the program sends the primary's window live**, once a read, at the read's end: xterm's scrollTtyOutput does it as each read arrives, before parsing (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file), a practice not followed for the cost the record gives, so the read that sets `?1010` itself sends the window live and a read that ends on the alternate screen leaves the primary's window where it was (`scroll_on_output_sends_the_window_live_when_set`). xterm's resource is true by default; Jev's lean kept baud's, RESET_AS_TODAY, at confidences 0.46/0.66. XTSAVE slot 44 |
| `?1011` | scroll to the bottom on a key press (rxvt) | done — since 2026-10-19, set at power-on, as a key has always snapped the window to the live screen here, as ghostty's `keystroke` and foot's view reset do (`[[source: ghostty-d9840f3-config]]`, `[[source: foot-input]]`); xterm's scrollKey defaults false (Jev's lean, SET_AS_TODAY, at confidences 0.12/0.20, little better than none). Reset, a key leaves the window where it is; a paste still snaps. Read by the macOS app through `Term::resource`; XTSAVE slot 45 |
| `?1014` | xterm's `fastScroll` | done — since 2026-10-19, `DECRQM` answers `3`, permanently set: baud draws at most one frame a display refresh and never one output already shifted off, which is what the resource selects ("suppressing screen refreshes for the special case when output to the screen has completely shifted the contents off-screen", `[[source: xterm-man]]`), and no switch could draw every intermediate scroll. DECSET and DECRST are counted (Jev's lean, PERMANENT_SET, 0.88/0.88) |
| `?1041` | use the CLIPBOARD selection (selectToClipboard) | done — since 2026-10-19, reset at power-on. **Set, a selection is copied to the clipboard as it is made**, as xterm's SELECT names CLIPBOARD rather than PRIMARY (`[[source: xterm-man]]`); reset, the macOS app copies a finished selection only under `[clipboard] copy_on_select = "on"`, since 2026-10-21 the default (the owner's V13), the pasteboard being the one selection macOS has; `"off"` leaves the copy to `?1041` alone (Jev's lean, MODE_WINS, 0.42/0.36; `a_finished_selection_goes_to_the_pasteboard_unless_the_key_is_off`). Read by the macOS app (Jev's lean, BUILD_COPY_ON_SELECT, 0.52/0.56); XTSAVE slot 46. Since 2026-10-20 the Linux app reads it for every use of SELECT, as xterm does: reset, a selection made goes to the primary selection and the middle button and Shift+Insert paste the primary; set, both use the clipboard instead, and the primary is not set; an `OSC 52` `s`, or an empty `Pc`, lands on the same one (`select_is_the_primary_until_1041`, `on_wayland_each_selection_named_is_its_own`) |
| `?1044` | reuse the data copied to CLIPBOARD (keepClipboard) | done — since 2026-10-19, `DECRQM` answers `3`, permanently set: xterm serves CLIPBOARD lazily and the resource makes it serve the copy it saved rather than a later selection (`[[source: xterm-button]]`, `ConvertSelection`); the macOS pasteboard holds the text baud wrote at the copy, so the clipboard always keeps the data copied. DECSET and DECRST are counted (Jev's lean, PERMANENT_SET, 0.89/0.86, re-asked after the first two asks described the mode wrongly) |
| `?2001`, `?2005`, `?2006` | readline mouse button-1, character-quoting, newline pasting | done — since 2026-10-19, reset at power-on: a click on the cursor's line moves the shell's point there, a paste is quoted byte by byte with the tty's literal-next character, a paste keeps its line breaks ([input-coverage.md](input-coverage.md); Jev, BUILD, 0.98/0.98 and 0.94/0.94). XTSAVE slots 49-51. Under `?2004` a bracketed paste keeps its line breaks whatever `?2006` says. xterm's `_WriteSelectionData` turns LF into CR inside the brackets too unless `?2006` is set, and that is a departure, recorded as open. |
| `?2002`, `?2003` | readline mouse button-2 and button-3 | **out** — each changes a gesture baud does not have: a middle-button paste and a double-click of button 3 that deletes the selection, xterm's X conventions; on macOS the middle button pastes nothing and the right button is the menu's (Jev, REASON, 0.94/0.94). Not recognised: DECSET counted, `DECRQM` answers 0 |
| `?1001` | highlight tracking | done — the fifth tracking level, exclusive with `?9`, `?1000`, `?1002` and `?1003` (ctlseqs' "five modes of the mouse protocols"), where it was a flag beside the level until 2026-10-11. Normal tracking's presses and releases; button 1's press is owed the program's XTHIMOUSE answer, the region the answer starts tracks the mouse within the program's rows (`Term::highlight`) and the macOS app draws it through the selection's highlight, and its release is reported as `CSI t` or `CSI T` in the encoding in force -- refused whole and counted where the encoding cannot name a position. Nothing waits on the answer. Under `?1016` the region's four positions are cells and the mouse's two pixels, the one reading both paragraphs allow; a long-form release with no pixel is refused and counted. Questions for Jev where the text is silent or reads two ways -- the short form's test, the SGR and urxvt forms, an empty row range -- are in the record, "`?1001` highlight tracking is built to the text". `a_press_is_reported_as_normal_tracking_reports_it`, `it_is_a_level_exclusive_with_the_other_four`, `the_end_is_held_to_the_rows_the_program_gave`, `under_sgr_pixels_the_region_is_in_cells_and_the_mouse_in_pixels`, `the_program_s_region_is_drawn_through_the_selection_highlight` |

### SGR

Enumerated against `[[source: ecma48]]` § 8.3.117 and, for the extensions,
`[[source: ctlseqs]]` § "Character Attributes".

| parameters | status |
|---|---|
| 0 reset | done |
| 1, 2 bold/dim · 22 neither | done |
| 3 italic · 23 not | done |
| 4 underline, 21 double, **4:1..4:5** styled · 24 / 4:0 not | done — five styles, exactly one in force: straight, double, and `[[source: kitty-underlines]]`'s curly, dotted and dashed, each its own `Attrs` bit and drawn by the software compositor and the cell shader. A `4:n` *replaces* the family, and so do a bare 4 (the spec's backward-compatible 4:1) and 21 (`[[source: ecma48]]`'s "doubly underlined", the state 4:2 names), so 4:3 then 21 is double alone and 21 and 4:2 are one rendition (practice departs three ways and is not followed: vte reads 21 as bold off, `[[source: alacritty-vte-sgr]]`; the Linux console as a single underline, `[[source: linux-vt-sgr]]`; xterm holds 4 and 21 as two bits, `[[source: xterm-sgr]]`); 24 and 4:0 clear all five; an unknown n clears rather than aborts the walk. **4:3 is not 4;3** -- sfte flattens the colon and gets underline-plus-italic, measured -- and DECRQSS reports the three colon styles as 4:n so the reply round-trips. `each_colon_style_sets_exactly_its_own_bit`, `the_colon_form_is_not_the_semicolon_form`, `a_style_replaces_the_family_and_4_0_clears_it`, `twenty_one_replaces_the_family_in_either_order`, `twenty_one_and_4_2_are_one_rendition`, `a_colon_underline_style_round_trips`, `a_double_underline_after_another_style_reports_alone_and_round_trips` |
| 5, 6 blink · 25 steady | done — **each rate drawn at its own rate, and the two one aspect**, as the owner decided on 2026-10-13 ("Own rate, exclusive"). `SGR 5` sets `Attrs::BLINK`, which the app blinks at 75 a minute (0.4 s shown, 0.4 s hidden, `BLINK_HALF_MS` on the display link's clock), inside `[[source: ecma48]]` § 8.3.117's "slowly blinking (less then 150 per minute)"; `SGR 6` sets `Attrs::RAPID_BLINK`, a bit of its own in the twenty-byte `StyleWord`'s `Attrs` (not the eight-byte cell), which it blinks at 150 a minute (0.2 s and 0.2 s, `RAPID_HALF_MS`), the floor of "rapidly blinking (150 per minute or more)". Integer const assertions beside the two halves hold both bounds, and `each_blink_rate_flips_at_its_own_rate` counts 75 and 150 hides across a minute of the clock. Each rate shows for half its cycle, as 5 always did: the text gives a rate and no duty cycle. **5 and 6 replace each other and the later wins** (`Attrs::BLINKS`, `SgrAttr::BlinkRate`): § 8.3.117 lists them as two rates of one aspect beside "25 steady (not blinking)" and closes "The usable combinations of parameter values are determined by the implementation"; 25 clears both, and so does 0 (`rapid_blink_is_its_own_rendition`, `the_later_blink_rate_wins`, `steady_clears_both_blink_rates`, `the_blink_rates_are_the_same_at_every_read_size`, every read size and every split into two reads, and `the_blink_rates_are_masked_replaces`, which holds the `sgr-table` arm's derived fold to the grammar's). Until 2026-10-14 6 set 5's bit and blinked at its rate, a stated departure, and DECRQSS answered `5` for it; it answers `6` and the answer round-trips (`decrqss_answers_6_for_rapid_blink_and_it_round_trips`). **Each rate hides its text only in its own off phase**, in the software compositor and in the cell shader, whose `blink_hidden` is the mask of the shapes the frame hides (`BlinkPhases::hidden`; `each_blink_rate_hides_only_in_its_own_off_phase`, `hidden_blinking_text_is_the_frame_of_its_spaces`, `a_hidden_cells_records_are_not_drawn`, and the pixel oracle's mixed-phase scene in `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws`), and **a frame knows which rates are on screen**, so a rate's flip repaints only while text at that rate is drawn and a slow-only screen builds no frame at the rapid flips (`Blinking::flips`; `a_rate_flips_a_frame_only_while_its_text_is_on_screen`, `a_blinking_row_keeps_blinking_through_incremental_frames`, `a_blinking_slot_off_screen_is_not_seen`, `a_rapid_slot_alone_is_seen_on_the_cell_path`, and `the_tick_takes_the_blink_phase_under_the_motion_preference` for the tick). 2.5 cycles a second is under WCAG 2.3.1's "more than three times in any one second period" (`[[source: wcag22]]`), whose flash is "a pair of opposing changes in relative luminance": one shown-and-hidden cycle is one flash, not two. **Under macOS's Reduce Motion both are held shown** (`text_blink`, `reduce_motion_holds_blinking_text_shown`, both rates, with the tick held to it by `the_tick_takes_the_blink_phase_under_the_motion_preference`): `[[source: apple-hig-accessibility]]` puts blinking animation under that setting, and WCAG 2.2.2 (`[[source: wcag22]]`) asks for a way to stop blinking that lasts past five seconds -- a departure from "slowly blinking" and "rapidly blinking" made at the system's request, stated here so it is not silent. **Under macOS's Dim Flashing Lights each off phase is drawn faint**, the text half way to its page as `SGR 2` mixes it, at the same two rates, so § 8.3.117's rates hold and each flip changes less of the page (`dim_off_phases`, `BlinkPhases::dimmed`; `dim_flashing_lights_draws_the_off_phase_faint_at_the_same_rates`, `a_faint_off_phase_draws_the_text_half_way_to_its_page`, `a_faint_phase_flips_frames_and_dims_only_hidden_phases`, and the pixel oracle's faint scene in `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws`): Apple's article asks that "an animation sequence with flashing lights in a game or app" be adjusted (`[[source: apple-mediaaccessibility-flashing-lights]]`), and Jev's lean, below the bar, took a faint off phase over steady text, a slower rate and an off phase held under WCAG's general-flash threshold (the runner-up; the record, "Blinking text dims under Dim Flashing Lights"). With Reduce Motion on as well the text is steady. The setting is read at launch and followed when its notification arrives (`the_flashing_lights_notification_marks_its_own_setting`, `a_local_centre_post_reaches_the_default_centre`). **The Linux app blinks both rates** since 2026-10-21, its one wait woken a millisecond past each flip of a rate on screen and nothing while none is (`effects::next_flip`, `the_next_flip_is_ahead_and_reads_the_new_phase`, `only_a_rate_on_screen_bounds_the_wait`, `blinking_text_wakes_at_its_own_flips_and_repaints_whole`). It holds both shown when the desktop portal's `reduced-motion` asks (`reduced_motion_holds_text_shown_and_turns_the_default_trail_off`); the portal has no Dim Flashing Lights counterpart to follow |
| 7 reverse · 27 positive | done |
| 8 conceal · 28 reveal | done |
| 9 strike · 29 not | done |
| 30-37, 39 foreground · 40-47, 49 background | done |
| 90-97, 100-107 aixterm bright | done — palette indices 8..15 |
| 38/48 `;5;n` 256-colour, `;2;r;g;b` truecolour | done — **a cell keeps its rendition while the cells name no more renditions than the table has slots** (65,536 at the app's provisioning): `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s SGR, page 5-104, "In no way does the selection of current rendition affect the rendition of characters already entered into the display". Since 2026-10-14 the rendition table sweeps -- at 7/8 load and before an eviction, at most once a sixteenth of its writes -- freeing every slot no cell of either screen or the history names, slot 0 never, and the renderer repaints a frame whose slots were reclaimed (`live_cells_keep_their_renditions_through_five_tables_of_churn`, `the_other_screens_cells_are_marked`, `the_historys_cells_are_marked`, `the_primarys_history_is_marked_from_the_alternate_screen`, `an_erased_rows_rendition_is_marked`, `a_churned_table_sweeps_before_it_fills`, `a_full_table_sweeps_before_it_evicts`, `a_sweep_is_chunk_invariant`, `a_swept_slot_reused_in_place_is_repainted`). Until then a session that had minted more renditions than slots evicted a named slot and recoloured its cells: yazi's chafa previews left 191 of 7,110 final cells differing from alacritty, 0 of 7,093 now. **Open**: with more distinct renditions named at once than slots it still evicts and recolours, as chafa printing into the shell does (81 cells of 7,199 differ); and a full table evicts without sweeping within a sixteenth of its writes of the last sweep, which happens only where the cells name more than 15/16 of the slots (`a_full_table_evicts_without_sweeping_within_a_sixteenth_of_its_writes`; options in the record, "A full rendition table sweeps before it evicts") |
| 38/48 `:5:n` / `:2:Pi:r:g:b` sub-parameter form | done — both the six-part form with a colour-space id and the five-part form without |
| 10-19 fonts | **partial** — **a font selection changes nothing on screen**: recorded in `StyleWord`'s spare bits, answered by DECRQSS and readable through `Term::style_of`, and drawn by no path. `[[source: ecma48]]` § 8.3.117 names "primary (default) font" and nine alternatives and says nothing of what each is, so drawing one needs a configured face per slot, which baud has no setting for. The gap is that setting and its drawing |
| 58/59 underline colour | done — `StyleWord` grew to sixteen bytes; the *cell* holds a `StyleId` index and did not change |
| 20 Fraktur | **partial** — **recorded in `Attrs` and drawn as plain text**: § 8.3.117's "Fraktur (Gothic)" needs a Fraktur face, and baud has neither an embedded one nor a setting for one. 23 clears italic **and** fraktur, as "not italicized, not fraktur" says. The gap is the face |
| 26, 50 proportional spacing | **partial** — **recorded in `Attrs` and changes nothing on screen**: § 8.3.117 reserves 26 "for proportional spacing as specified in CCITT Recommendation T.61" and 50 for its cancel, and defines neither; T.61 is not a pinned source, and a fixed cell grid has no proportional advance to give. The gap is a reading of T.61 and what a cell grid would do with it |
| 51 framed, 53 overlined, 54, 55 | done — **drawn on all three paths**, the software compositor, the instance path and the cell shader, in the text's colour as the strike is: `SGR 58` is "the underline color" (`[[source: kitty-underlines]]`) and neither is an underline; ghostty draws its overline in the foreground too (`[[source: ghostty-generic-d9840f3]]`). 53 is a line across the top of the cell, one underline thickness tall, where ghostty puts it (`[[source: ghostty-metrics-d9840f3]]`). 51 is one box around each run of framed cells on a row: a line across the top and the bottom of every cell, and a side only where the run starts and where it ends. Both choices were Jev's at 1.00 in both orders, since § 8.3.117 says only "framed" and "overlined". 54 clears framed **and** encircled, 55 the overline. A framed cell whose text is hidden, concealed (`SGR 8`, "concealed characters") or in its blink's off phase, draws its background and nothing of its own, frame included, and the visible cells beside it close their box with a side at the seam: Jev leaned that way at 0.78 and 0.80, below 0.9, and the lean is taken. `an_overline_is_a_line_across_the_top_of_its_cells`, `the_overline_is_the_text_colour_and_not_the_underline_colour`, `a_framed_run_is_one_box`, `a_framed_run_closes_beside_a_hidden_cell`, `sixty_five_and_fifty_five_cancel_their_lines`, and the pixel oracle's `decoration_gate`; a glyph reaching into a framed or overlined cell takes the order 52's row gives, `reach_gate` |
| 52 encircled | done — **one enclosure around each run of encircled cells on a row, on all three paths**, in the text's colour as the frame is: the frame's top and bottom lines across the cells between the ends, and at each end a side at the run's edge between two quarter circles half a cell wide: a lone cell is a stadium; a run of two cells, a wide character among them, is a rectangle with rounded corners of radius half a cell's width. The curves keep to the corners, where the end glyph has less ink than the half ellipse crossed. A stroke at the cell's edge still touches ink at small sizes: 64 of 94 glyphs at 13 px on a lone cell. A half ellipse filling each end cell crossed most end glyphs' ink at 9-13 px, and Jev leaned to the rounded corners over it and over drawing 52 as 51's square box, as mintty does (`[[source: mintty-termout-3-7-8]]`), at 0.85 and 0.88 (decisions 804-807; below 0.9, the lean is taken). § 8.3.117 says only "52 encircled". Jev leaned to the run over an ellipse a cell and one true ellipse a run at 0.72 and 0.66, and at 0.79 and 0.76 asked again with the Unicode Standard's "no defined way of indicating the application of a combining enclosing mark to more than a single base character" (`[[source: unicode18-ch22]]`) and 51's one box a run added (decisions 782-785); below 0.9, the lean is taken. The end is an anti-aliased mask of exactly one cell (`baud_render::boxes::draw_ring`), cached in the atlas once a slot has been encircled and drawn by the software compositor and the instance path as a procedural glyph is and by the cell shader from the glyph map under keys of its own, so the three sample the same bytes. A visible encircled cell beside a hidden one closes the run, as a frame does. A glyph reaching into an encircled cell lies under its outline when it is in that row or reaches down from the row above, and over it when it reaches up from the row below, on all three paths, as for every decoration: each row's glyphs and then its decorations, rows top to bottom (`[[source: css21-zindex]]`; Jev's lean at 0.67 and 0.62, decisions 790-793); a composition lies over it as over an underline. 54 clears it. `an_encircled_run_is_one_enclosure`, `an_encircled_end_keeps_to_its_corners`, `an_encircled_run_closes_beside_a_hidden_cell`, `sixty_five_and_fifty_five_cancel_their_lines`, `the_line_shape_bits_match_the_shader`, and the pixel oracle's `decoration_gate`, `reach_gate` and `composed_decoration_gate` |
| 56, 57 | **out** — `[[source: ecma48]]` § 8.3.117 reserves 56-59 for future standardization and `[[source: ctlseqs]]` assigns 56 and 57 nothing. 58/59 are done only because ctlseqs assigns *them* the underline colour, which is an extension over ECMA-48 rather than a reading of it. `sgr_56_and_57_are_reserved_and_set_nothing` |
| 60-63, 65 ideogram lines | done — `[[source: ecma48]]` § 8.3.117 defines 60-63 as "ideogram underline or right side line", "ideogram double underline or double line on the right side", "ideogram overline or left side line" and "ideogram double overline or double line on the left side", each named for two edges because which edge is marked follows the writing direction, and 65 "cancels the effect of the rendition aspects established by parameter values 60 to 64". **baud's text is horizontal, so they are drawn as the under and over lines**, on all three paths: 60 the straight underline and 61 the double, beside whatever `SGR 4` style is in force, 62 the overline and 63 two lines at the top, one thickness and a pixel apart. 60 and 61 take `SGR 58`'s colour as an underline does, and 62 and 63 the text's as the overline does: Jev leaned to 58 for the underlines at 0.60 and 0.30, then 0.46 and 0.56 asked again, and the lean is taken. They stay a separate aspect from 4 and 21: 24 leaves them and 65 clears them. `Attrs` went to `u32` for the five bits. `tests/sgr/ideogram.rs`, `ideogram_lines_are_the_under_and_over_lines_of_horizontal_text`, `ideogram_double_lines_are_two_lines`, `sixty_five_and_fifty_five_cancel_their_lines`, and the pixel oracle's `decoration_gate` |
| 64 ideogram stress marking | done — **a filled dot over each marked character**, on all three paths, in the text's colour. § 8.3.117 names it and gives no shape or side, so baud draws what the web draws for text with no language (`[[source: css-text-decor-3]]`): `text-emphasis-position`'s "Initial: over right", over in horizontal text; a filled `dot`, "Display small circles as marks.
