The filled dot is U+2022"; `text-emphasis-color`'s "Initial: currentcolor", the text's colour, so `SGR 58` does not reach it. A mark given only as filled or open is a circle in horizontal text ("If only filled or open is specified, the shape keyword computes to circle in horizontal typographic modes"); filled is U+25CF. The marks are "drawn once for each typographic character unit", so a wide character takes one, centred across its two cells, and are not drawn for "the Unicode separator classes (Z*)", for "any characters that belong to the Unicode P* general category and do not NFKD normalize [UAX15] to any of the following symbols" (`#`, `%`, `&`, `@`, `§`, `¶`, the per mille and per ten thousand signs and a few more, and their small and fullwidth forms, keep the mark), or for "the Unicode classes for control codes and unassigned characters (Cc, Cf, Cn)": a space, a comma, an ideographic comma, a soft hyphen or an unassigned codepoint takes none. The set is built from `[[source: gc-data]]` and `[[source: unicode-data]]`'s decompositions (`[[source: unicode17-ch3]]` D65) into a two-stage bit trie by `scripts/gen-stress-table.py`, read from Rust and as the same bytes by the cell shader, so a mark costs two loads. A cluster takes the mark unless its base is one CSS leaves bare; a space with combining characters (gc M*, `[[source: unicode17-ch3]]` D52) takes it, as CSS says. The cell shader sees a cluster's slot and not its base, so the overlay carries the renderer's answer for each cluster on screen. The cell has no ruby space above the line, so the mark is a square two line thicknesses on a side, centred, a side's length below the cell's top. Jev chose it at 0.94 and 0.92 over a dot under each cell and leaving it undrawn, asked with the owner's "we need to support such protocols fully, always!" quoted. 65 clears it. `a_stress_mark_is_a_dot_over_each_character`, `sixty_five_cancels_the_stress_mark`, `the_stress_mark_skips_what_css_leaves_bare`, `a_cluster_takes_the_mark_as_its_base_does`, `the_stress_mark_table_matches_the_shader`, `the_bare_mask_bit_matches_the_shader`, and the pixel oracle's `decoration_gate` |

**Fonts moved from `out` to recorded by measuring rather than assuming.** The row
said they needed cell bits that did not exist. A font is a *rendition*, and
rendition lives in `StyleWord` -- which carried sixteen bits of explicit
padding, put there because zerocopy rejects implicit padding. `PackedCell`
never had to grow, and the eight-byte cell was never the constraint. "A bit set
and never read" resolved the way it did for charset designations: baud records
the selection and `Term::style_of` is where a renderer reads it. No renderer
does yet, and since 2026-10-18 the row says so.

Every SGR parameter above is **done** or **out** but three rows at **partial**:
10-19, 20, 26 and 50, which read `done` until 2026-10-18 while no path drew
them. 51, 53 and 60-64 read `done` over the same gap and are drawn since
that day, and 52, **partial** for part of it, since the same day. 6 was **partial** from 2026-10-10 to 2026-10-14, while it blinked at 5's slow rate where § 8.3.117 asks 150 a minute or more. `CSI m` used
to be listed as `partial` on the strength of that last row, which conflated two
different things. `out` is a scope decision with a reason; `partial` is a row
that names what it does not do yet. A font selection that no renderer reads is
that second case, and the 10-19 row says so. The four SGR rows above say what
they do not do; the 5, 6 row did until 2026-10-14.

**This was the largest `parsed` gap in the codebase and the table did not show
it.** `SgrAttr::fold` handled `Reset` and foreground only, falling through to "no
change" for `Set`, `Unset` and every `Bg` — because `StyleWord` was a bare `u32`
holding a 24-bit foreground and nothing else. Bold, italic, underline, reverse,
blink, conceal, strike and **all background colours** were parsed and discarded:
support in the parser, nothing on the screen. `StyleWord` now carries
foreground, background and attributes.

---

## Tier 2 — VT220 / VT420

In scope. Needed by real applications (vim, tmux, htop) but not by every one.

| seq | name | status |
|---|---|---|
| `CSI ! p` | DECSTR soft reset | done — resets the DEC-defined state and **not** the alternate screen, mouse tracking or its encoding. Those are xterm extensions, so a DEC list cannot mention them and xterm decides: in `ReallyReset` all three are inside the `if (full)` RIS branch, `FromAlternate` next to `ClearScreen` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`). **It empties the shown screen's saved cursor and keeps the other screen's**, as xterm's soft reset writes `sc[screen->whichBuf]` alone, so a program that soft-resets on the alternate screen still gets the shell's cursor back at `?1049 l` (`resets_and_the_two_slots`). baud reset them until 2026-08-27, so a full-screen application recovering with `CSI ! p` was dropped onto the primary screen and lost what `?1049 h` had saved. **Of the mode word it restores only what xterm's soft reset restores**: DECTCEM, DECLRMM, DECOM, DECCKM, DECAWM, reverse wraparound and IRM, and releases `?2026`, which xterm lacks; LNM, `?4`, `?5`, `?8`, `?1004`, `?1034`, `?1036`, `?1039` and `?2004` stay, as they do there. It restores the keypad to numeric and `modifyOtherKeys` to its first value, which xterm does on both resets and RIS here did not. Until 2026-09-24 it restored the whole mode word, so a program recovering with `CSI ! p` lost its bracketed paste (`decstr_restores_the_modes_xterm_restores_and_keeps_the_rest`, `decstr_and_ris_restore_the_keypad_and_modify_other_keys`). It clears every `OSC 4` override and keeps the dynamic colours, as xterm's common path does (`both_resets_clear_every_palette_override`, since 2026-09-29). **It leaves the cursor where it is**, since 2026-10-14: "DECSTR has no affect on the Active Position" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-4, DECSTR note c, p. 32), and the home in `[[source: vt510-decstr]]`'s and `[[source: vt220-rm-ch4]]`'s tables is the saved cursor state's; it homed before (`decstr_leaves_the_cursor_where_it_is`, `decstr_under_origin_mode_leaves_the_cursor`). A pending wrap at the screen's edge survives it (`decstr_keeps_a_pending_wrap_at_the_screen_edge`), and one at a narrower right margin is left one column past it, a column once the margins widen (`decstr_leaves_a_column_past_a_narrower_right_margin`): both note V's "PendingAt", the owner's. See `tests/xterm/soft_reset_scope.rs` |
| `CSI Pl ; Pc " p` | DECSCL select conformance level | done — since 2026-10-22 **a program can ask baud to behave as a VT100 (Level 1), a VT220 (2), a VT320 (3) or a VT420 (4), and baud then does only what that level defines**. Each control, mode, character set, SGR parameter and key carries the level that introduces it in one table built at compile time and indexed at dispatch (`baud_domain::LEVELS`): the lowest level its `[[source: dec-std-070]]` `Levels:` field names, an `x` an extension baud has, every higher level keeping it (Jev, LOWEST_ONLY, 0.90/0.92). A control above the level in force is not executed and is counted, DECSCL note 1's "the normal rule that Level 2 functions only work in Level 2 operation" (PDF 226): at Level 1 ICH, ECH, DECTCEM, DECSTR, DECSCA, the selective erases, LS2, LS3, the GR locking shifts, S7C1T, S8C1T and Terminal State Interrogation, below Level 3 SU, SD, DECSCPP and DECSLPP, below Level 4 the side margins, DECIC, DECDC, DECBI, DECFI and the rectangle operations (`level_one_does_not_execute_a_level_two_function`, `levels_two_three_and_four_add_their_functions`). The table is held to the standard: its fields transcribed from the pinned copy by `scripts/extract-std070-levels.py` (`baud-domain/tests/std070_levels.tsv`, its control `the_transcription_reads_the_fields_its_pages_show`), each row to its field (`every_row_is_its_fields_lowest_level`), each field to a row or a named reason it has none (`every_field_is_a_row_or_named_undispatched`), each row to the forms that perform it (`the_parsed_forms_name_their_rows`). One row departs from its own field, by its own algorithm: the announcers, at Level 3. LS2 and LS3 keep their fields' Level 2, as the VT420's "Control Functions Ignored in Level 1" lists them (`[[source: vt420-rm]]` Table 4-1), where 070-3 §3.6.1.1 names them in Level 1 operation (Jev's lean, LEVEL_2, 0.34/0.60). The VT420's table was read beside the fields: it ignores at Level 1 what the fields put at Level 2 or above but for DECTCEM, SU, SD, DECSLPP, DECXCPR, DECLRMM, DECRQUPSS and the macro space report, which it does not list, a product's departure not followed. **A control no DEC level defines acts at every level** -- xterm's, kitty's, contour's, and ECMA-48's own such as CHA, VPA, REP and SL (Jev, EVERY_LEVEL, 0.98/0.98; `an_extension_acts_at_level_one`) -- and one only the VT510's or VT520's manual defines, DECSCUSR, DECSWBV, DECSMBV, DECST8C, DECNCSM, DECECM and the VT500 character sets, is Level 4's, the VT510's DECSCL making 62, 63 and 64 one "VT Level 4 mode" (Jev, LEVEL_4, 0.94/0.91); the DEC controls only a VT220, VT340 or VT420 manual defines take the levels the VT420's Table 4-1 gives, the keyboard report Level 2's and DECBKM, DECSDM, DECGEPM and the data integrity and session reports Level 1's (Jev's lean, PRODUCT_TABLE, 0.66/0.64). **At Level 1**: SGR acts on 0, 1, 4, 5, 7 and 8 and on the parameters no DEC level defines, such as 3 and 23, and not on colour or 22, 24, 25, 27 and 28, §5.6.3's Color Text "at Level 2 or higher" and the VT520's Table 5-15 (Jev's lean, COLOUR_AND_OFFS_AT_2, 0.89/0.95; `level_one_sgr_keeps_bold_and_drops_colour`, `level_one_drops_colour_from_a_repeated_sgr`); SCS designates ASCII, line drawing, UK, the NRC sets, JIS-Roman and Katakana, into G0 and G1 only, note 1, the NRC sets as Table 1's extension 9 and DECNRCM's "1x" and the VT340 and VT420 have them (Jev's lean, LEVEL_1, 0.84/0.82) and the rest by SCS's own table (Jev's lean, TEXT_TABLE, 0.89/0.98; `level_one_designates_into_g0_and_g1_only`); DECRQSS, every string, DECRQM, DECRQPSR, DECRQTSR, DECRSPS and DECRSTS are not recognised, answer nothing and are counted, as the VT420 ignores them (Jev split, INVALID at confidence 0.16 then SILENT at 0.25 (probabilities 0.44 and 0.50), and the field's reading taken, the rule that a departure from DEC STD 070 needs 0.9 in both orders; `level_one_has_no_terminal_state_interrogation`); under ISO 8859-1 each received byte loses its eighth bit "before any processing occurs" (070-3 §3.7.5.1), from the byte after the control that starts the strip to the byte after one that ends it, in one read or across reads, and under UTF-8 nothing is stripped (Jev, LATIN1_ONLY, 0.98/0.95; `level_one_strips_the_eighth_bit_under_latin1`); and the keyboard is a VT100's (`docs/input-coverage.md`, the DECSCL row; `the_level_one_keyboard`). **Nothing changes at power-up**, Level 4, where every function but DECID executes (`level_four_executes_every_control_sequence_function`). Since 2026-10-19 **a program can select Level 1 to 4 and, above Level 1, seven- or eight-bit controls**, to `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-4 DECSCL and `[[source: vt510-decscl]]`: `Pl` 61 to 64; any other is ignored and counted, "Attempts to select conformance levels that are unsupported by the device will be ignored" (Jev, 61_TO_64, 0.98/0.98). It soft-resets, note 3's "without affecting the data contained in the display", as the VT220 manual and xterm do, where the VT320 to VT520 manuals say a hard reset (Jev's lean, SOFT, 0.68/0.94), and so unlocks the keyboard (`decscl_performs_a_soft_reset_and_keeps_the_screen`). `Pc` 0, 2 or omitted sends eight-bit C1 and 1 seven-bit, at Levels 2 to 4; Level 1 sends seven-bit whatever `Pc` says, "Level 1 operation is 7-bits only" (070-3 §3.6.4.1), and there `S8C1T` is counted and changes nothing, its algorithm's "if (conformance_level != LEVEL_1)" (Jev's lean, IGNORED, 0.88/0.94; `level_one_is_seven_bit_only`, `decscl_selects_the_level_and_the_c1_transmission`). **Level 4 at power-up and after `RIS`**, 070's `host_state_init`, "conformance level = LEVEL 4", and the VT510's factory default (Jev, LEVEL_4, 0.94/0.90; `the_terminal_powers_up_at_level_four_seven_bit`, `a_full_reset_returns_to_level_four_seven_bit`). **Level 1 and seven-bit after `ESC <`**: 070-0A EXIT VT52 EMULATION MODE (p. A-15), "turns off the VT52 emulator and turns on the ANSI parser of the terminal, causing the terminal to return to Level 1 operation", which its revision A.9.2 (p. A-45) made "always", and 070-0C Category 2 (p. C-28), "even if the terminal had been set to LEVEL 2 mode prior to entering VT52 mode"; Level 1 being seven-bit, an `S8C1T` goes with it (`leaving_vt52_returns_to_level_one_seven_bit`). xterm's `CASE_VT52_FINISH` returns to its level 1 as well, "per vttest", and keeps an `S8C1T`, a departure not followed (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file). **The primary DA answer does not follow it**: the owner picked "Keep ?6c (Recommended)" on 2026-10-15, and xterm's DA follows its identity, not its level (Jev, STAYS_6C, 0.98/1.00). DECRQSS `" p` reports it (the DECRQSS row), so **at power-up DECRQSS `" p` answers `64;1"p` while DA1 answers `?6c`**. **On the owner's veto list**, beside `RIS` returning to seven-bit C1 (070's Device Initialization): from the Level 4 power-up (Jev, LEVEL_4, 0.94/0.90, the owner's DA1 pick quoted), DECRQSS `" p` answers `64;1"p` where it was refused, and `ESC Z` gets no answer (the `ESC Z` row); since 2026-10-22, after DECSCL 61, 62 or 63 or an `ESC <`, the controls, keys and SGR parameters the level does not define do nothing, and a program that leaves VT52 with `ESC <` is at Level 1 until it sends DECSCL |
| `CSI SP q` | DECSCUSR cursor style | done — the shape and the blink an application asks for, and **drawn with its blink** since 2026-09-25: since 2026-10-15 at 333 ms a phase, or `[cursor] blink_interval_ms`, or on macOS the system caret's `NSTextInsertionPointBlinkPeriodOn` and `...Off` where the user has set them, with the hidden phase drawn by `[cursor] blink_animation` (the plasma glow by default, a fade, the fire, the ufo, or none; none under Reduce Motion), and held steady by macOS 15's non-blinking switch unless `[cursor] blink = true`, which changes the drawing and not the answer; Linux takes the same 333 ms default; only while the window has focus and restarted shown by output or a key -- foot's rules (`[[source: foot-term-reset]]`'s file) -- on the display link's clock or the Linux `poll` timeout (`baud-app/src/caret.rs`, `baud-render/tests/cursor_blink.rs`, the pixel oracle's off-phase scene). The power-on style is the embedder's (`Term::set_initial_cursor_style`, `[cursor] shape` and `blink`, a blinking block by default), and `Ps = 7`, `DECSTR` and `RIS` restore it, as xterm restores its resources. **The sources conflict on `Ps = 0`, and baud follows foot**: `[[source: ctlseqs]]` and xterm's `charproc.c` make it a blinking block, foot's `csi.c` resets it "to configured default" (`[[source: foot-csi]]`'s file). The two agree while the configured style is the default; `Ps = 1` stays the blinking block (`zero_and_seven_restore_the_configured_style_and_one_is_the_blinking_block`, `both_resets_restore_the_configured_style`). xterm's `cursorBlinkXOR`, which inverts an application's blink against the resource, is not followed. **The style is saved with the cursor at `?1049 h` and restored at `?1049 l`**, as kitty and wezterm do, and not by `DECSC`, which `[[source: vt510-decsc]]` does not list it for: helix ends its session on the alternate screen with `0 q`, the system entry's `Se` (`2 q`) and `cnorm`, and the shell gets its own cursor back. `DECRQSS` answers the style DECSCUSR set, not what `?12` draws |
| `CSI Ps SP t` | DECSWBV set warning bell volume | **partial** — since 2026-10-20, **a program can make the bell softer or silent**: `[[source: vt520-rm]]` p. 5-154, "1 Off; 2, 3, 4 Low; 5, 5, 7, 8, 0 or none High (default)", the warning bell "for errors and receipt of Ctrl G"; Table 4.7.3 and `[[source: vt510-rm-b]]` p. 5-148 agree. **0 and none are high**, as the three DEC texts give them, where `[[source: ctlseqs]]` and xterm's `CASE_DECSWBV` read them as off (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file), a departure not followed (Jev, DEC, 1.00 and 1.00); a value past 8 changes nothing and is counted. `Term::warning_bell_volume()`; DECRQSS answers `SP t` (its row). High at power-on, restored by `RIS`, which "Sets all features listed on set-up screens to their saved settings" (p. 5-184 of the VT510's), where xterm keeps it; a `BEL` sounds at the volume set when it arrived, though DECSWBV or `RIS` change it later in the same read; kept by `DECSTR`, whose Table 5-9 names it not. **On macOS off is silence and low sounds as high**: the app's `NSBeep` takes no volume, NSSound sends the system beep to it (`[[source: apple-nssound]]`), and the system sound services play "with no programmatic volume control available" (`[[source: apple-audioservicesplaysystemsound]]`), so no documented call plays the user's alert sound below its volume (Jev's lean, LOW_SAME_SOUND, 0.61/0.70 then 0.69/0.67) -- the gap this row is partial for. The volume reaches the sound alone: the flash, `?1042` and `?1043` act whatever it says, as xterm's volume reaches only its audible bell (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`), and the config's `bell = "none"` stays silent. **On Linux likewise**: `xdg-system-bell-v1`'s `ring` carries no volume (`[[source: xdg-system-bell-v1]]`), so off is silence and low sounds as high. `decswbv_reads_every_value_as_the_dec_manuals_give_it`, `a_volume_past_eight_is_refused_and_counted`, `ris_restores_the_volumes_and_44_and_decstr_keeps_them`, `each_bell_is_counted_at_the_volume_it_rang_at`, `a_bell_rings_at_the_volume_it_was_sent_at`, `the_volume_silences_the_sound_and_nothing_else` |
| `CSI Ps SP u` | DECSMBV set margin bell volume | **partial** — since 2026-10-20, **a typewriter's margin bell**: `[[source: vt520-rm]]` p. 5-138, "1, 0 or none Off (default); 2, 3, 4 Low; 5, 6, 7, 8 High", the margin bell "which sounds as the current position nears the right margin". **0 and none are off**, as DEC gives them, where ctlseqs and xterm's `CASE_DECSMBV` read 0 as high, not followed (Jev, DEC, 1.00 and 1.00); past 8, counted. The VT520's Section 2.12.7 calls high the default of all three volumes, against the page, the summary table and the Set-Up's "Margin bell volume Off"; off is built. **It rings when both switches are on**, this volume and `?44` (its row; Jev's lean, TWO_SWITCHES, 0.37/0.27 then 0.27/0.14), and then **as DEC STD 070 rings it** (070-6 § 6.6.5.2, pp. 6-45 and 6-46, `[[source: dec-std-070]]`; Jev, STD070, 0.94 and 0.95): a normal keystroke, one whose first character is not `ESC`, `CSI` or `DCS`, arms it, and a character printed from a column at or left of RIGHT MARGIN - 8 to one right of it on the same line rings it, so on 80 columns the character written in column 72 -- eight columns, as `[[source: vt100-ug-ch1]]` and `[[source: vt102-ug-ch3]]` give them (Jev, DEC_EIGHT, 0.97 and 0.97), where xterm's `nMarginBell` is 10. The right margin is the line's: a double-width line's half, or DECSLRM's. Rung once, it is disarmed until the next keystroke; the cursor moving to a new line disarms it -- an `LF`, a wrap, a scroll of the screen or of a region that holds its line, `IL`, `DL`, a change of screen -- and horizontal moves neither ring it nor arm it. A region scrolled above or below the cursor's line moves no text on it and keeps it armed, and a write that prints no character, DECDWL's or DECDHL's cleared half or a DECFRA or DECCRA rectangle, neither rings it nor disarms it. **A cursor that leaves the line and comes back before anything is printed stays armed**, where STD 070 disarms it: an open departure, since telling it needs a test on every row change (Jev, AT_RING, 0.94 and 0.94). xterm rings it on the key instead, from the cursor where the key finds it (`AdjustAfterInput`, `[[source: xterm-input-c]]`). `Term::margin_bell_volume()`, and the rings counted in `Term::margin_bells`, which the macOS app sounds as a `BEL`, at this volume -- low as high, as for DECSWBV, the gap this row is partial for; the Linux app sounds it the same way. Off at power-on and after `RIS`, kept by `DECSTR`. `decsmbv_reads_every_value_as_the_dec_manuals_give_it`, `the_character_printed_in_column_72_rings_and_no_other`, `the_margin_bell_needs_44_and_a_volume`, `a_cursor_key_does_not_arm_it`, `every_way_to_a_new_line_disarms_it`, `horizontal_moves_neither_ring_nor_disarm`, `every_writer_rings_it`, `the_right_margin_is_the_lines`, `a_write_that_prints_nothing_neither_rings_nor_disarms_it`, `a_scroll_away_from_the_cursors_line_keeps_it_armed`, `each_bell_is_counted_at_the_volume_it_rang_at`, `the_ringer_hears_each_bell_at_its_own_volume` |
| `CSI " q` | DECSCA character protection | done |
| `CSI ? J` | DECSED selective erase display | done — 0, 1 and 2; `3` is in Tier 3. **A character DECSCA left erasable becomes a space in the colours and attributes it had, and an empty position stays empty**: "changed to the Space character (2/0). The character rendition and attributes associated with each position do not change ... empty character positions, are not affected" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` pp. 5-159 and 5-162; its algorithms assign the code alone). xterm writes a space in the current colours over every unprotected position, empty ones too; until 2026-10-19 baud wrote an empty cell in the current rendition (`decsed_turns_characters_into_spaces_in_their_own_rendition`, `an_empty_position_is_not_affected`, `decsed_reaches_past_the_rows_mark`). The space keeps the ECMA-48 guard (decided 2026-10-16) and an `OSC 8` link (Jev's lean, KEEP, 0.94 then 0.54 in both orders, log 846-849), and drops the line-drawing flag and a cluster's marks (`the_space_keeps_the_guard_and_the_link_and_drops_the_rest`). A wide character the cursor's column cuts goes whole, both halves spaces, as ED snaps (Jev's lean, WHOLE_SPACE; `a_wide_character_the_edge_cuts_becomes_two_spaces`); a protected one stays whole. The line renditions stay, note 3 (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-162), where its algorithm resets the cursor's line as ED's does: Jev leaned to the note at 0.73 and 0.82, below the bar, the lean taken (`el_and_the_selective_erases_keep_the_rendition`). **Every parameter, in order**, since 2026-10-19: `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s algorithms loop `for (n=0; n<parc; n++) switch(parv[n])` (EL p. 5-155, ED p. 5-157, DECSEL p. 5-160, DECSED p. 5-163), so `CSI 1 ; 0 K` erases the whole line; a value with no case, and a `3` in a list (Jev's lean, SKIP, 0.76/0.74 then 0.72/0.70, log 874-877), is skipped and counted. `[[source: ecma48]]` gives ED and EL the notation `(Ps)` and says nothing of a second parameter (`ed_and_el_apply_every_parameter_in_order`, `a_value_with_no_case_is_skipped_and_counted`, `a_three_in_a_list_is_skipped`, `the_selective_erases_apply_every_parameter_in_order`). **Since 2026-10-21 it clears the Sixel and ReGIS pixels in the positions it erases and keeps them over a protected or unoccupied one** (the Sixel row; `a_selective_erase_clears_only_the_positions_it_erases`, `a_selective_erase_spares_the_page_over_a_protected_position`). |
| `CSI ? K` | DECSEL selective erase line | done — as DECSED, on the cursor's line (`decsel_turns_a_character_into_a_space_in_its_own_rendition`). **Every parameter, in order**, since 2026-10-19: `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s algorithms loop `for (n=0; n<parc; n++) switch(parv[n])` (EL p. 5-155, ED p. 5-157, DECSEL p. 5-160, DECSED p. 5-163), so `CSI 1 ; 0 K` erases the whole line; a value with no case, and a `3` in a list (Jev's lean, SKIP, 0.76/0.74 then 0.72/0.70, log 874-877), is skipped and counted. `[[source: ecma48]]` gives ED and EL the notation `(Ps)` and says nothing of a second parameter (`ed_and_el_apply_every_parameter_in_order`, `a_value_with_no_case_is_skipped_and_counted`, `a_three_in_a_list_is_skipped`, `the_selective_erases_apply_every_parameter_in_order`). **Since 2026-10-21 it clears the Sixel and ReGIS pixels in the positions it erases and keeps them over a protected or unoccupied one** (the Sixel row; `a_selective_erase_clears_only_the_positions_it_erases`, `a_selective_erase_spares_the_page_over_a_protected_position`). |
| `CSI $ p` | DECRQM request mode | done |
| `DCS $ q Pt ST` | DECRQSS request status string | done — an application can read back each setting baud acts on, as the control that sets it, `DCS 1 $ r <control> ST`, and gets `DCS 0 $ r ST` for any other. Of `[[source: ctlseqs]]`'s eighteen strings, fourteen answer: `m` SGR, `SP q` DECSCUSR, `" q` DECSCA, `r` DECSTBM, `s` DECSLRM, `$ \|` DECSCPP, since 2026-10-22 `t` DECSLPP, `1 $ r Ps t` with `Ps` the screen's rows and 24 on a shorter screen, by `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s "if (parv[0] <= 24) lines_per_page = 24" (p. 5-76) and because `CSI Ps t` below 24 is an XTWINOPS (Jev, FLOOR, 0.96/0.94; `decrqss_answers_decslpp_as_the_lines_per_page`), `* x` DECSACE, since 2026-10-19 `" p` DECSCL, `6n ; 0 " p` or `6n ; 1 " p` above Level 1 -- `64;1"p` at power-up, while DA1 answers `?6c`, refused until then because answering "would claim a conformance level (note AP)"; it follows from the Level 4 power-up (Jev, LEVEL_4, 0.94/0.90, the owner's DA1 pick quoted) and is on the owner's veto list (the DECSCL row) -- and invalid at Level 1, where DECRQSS is no function, xterm's shape and refusal (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`; Jev, INVALID, 0.90/0.94); since 2026-10-22 **at Level 1 no string is answered**, `" p` among them, DECRQSS being "Levels: 2x (TSI), 3-4" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-196), as the VT420 ignores it there (`[[source: vt420-rm]]` Table 4-1); Jev split, INVALID at confidence 0.16 then SILENT at 0.25 (probabilities 0.44 and 0.50), and the field's reading is taken, the rule that a departure from DEC STD 070 needs 0.9 in both orders; xterm answers the other strings, a departure not followed (`level_one_has_no_terminal_state_interrogation`, `an_unknown_setting_gets_the_invalid_reply`; the DECSCL row), and `> Pp m` and `> Pp f` for every key-modifier resource, answered `> Pp ; Pv m` and `> Pp ; Pv f`, a disabled one `-1` and modifyOtherKeys' mask `: mask`, as xterm's `do_dcs` writes them (Jev's lean, XTERM_MINUS_ONE, 0.55/0.82 then 0.23/0.27; `decrqss_answers_the_key_modifier_strings`, `every_resource_answers_as_xterm_reports_it`). xterm's `do_dcs` spells the `f` answer `>%d;%dm`, an XTMODKEYS (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`), which is not the string that sets it, and is not followed. Since 2026-10-20 `> t` and `> Ps t` answer XTSMTITLE's modes in force as the control that sets them, `> 0 ; 2 t`, and `> t` with none set, which sent back is XTSMTITLE's default (Jev's lean, SETLIST, 0.70/0.76; `decrqss_answers_the_title_modes_as_the_control_that_sets_them`); xterm's `do_dcs` answers a 0 or 1 a mode, which sent back sets the wrong modes, and is not followed. Since 2026-10-20 `$ ~` DECSSDT and `$ }` DECSASD answer the status line's type and the display selected, `1 $ r Ps $ ~` and `1 $ r Ps $ }`, "The Status Display Type (DECSSDT) and Active Status Display (DECSASD) may be queried through DECRQSS" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-14 p. 14-9; `decrqss_answers_the_type_and_the_display`). Since 2026-10-20 two the VT520's DECRPSS list names and ctlseqs' does not (`[[source: vt520-rm]]` pp. 5-103 and 5-104) answer too: `SP t` DECSWBV and `SP u` DECSMBV, `1 $ r Ps SP t` and `1 $ r Ps SP u` with `Ps` 1, 2 or 5 for off, low and high, the first of each band, on which DEC and xterm agree, never the 0 they read apart (Jev's lean, FIRST, 0.18/0.58 then 0.12/0.56; `decrqss_answers_each_volume_as_the_control_that_sets_it`). The rest answer invalid because baud does not act on them: `) {`, `, \|` and `, }` (the VT525's colours), `* \|` DECSNLS, a `> Ps t` naming no title mode, and a key-modifier string naming no resource (`decrqss_answers_invalid_for_what_baud_does_not_act_on`); each such setting's control is counted as unsupported, across ctlseqs', STD 070's and the VT520's DECRPSS lists, so one that lands with its answer left invalid fails a test (`every_setting_decrqss_refuses_is_one_baud_counts`). `1` is valid and `0` invalid, as `[[source: ctlseqs]]` records the DEC manuals' errata (`baud-core/src/dcs.rs`) |
| `DCS + q Pt ST` | XTGETTCAP request termcap/terminfo string | done — since 2026-10-21 **a program can ask which bytes a key will send**, by its terminfo or termcap name, and gets the bytes the key sends in the modes in force: `DCS 1 + r name=value ST`, both in hex, every pair in one response, or `DCS 0 + r ST` (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 545-582; Jev, R1, 0.90/0.92). The names are xterm's table, all 319 (`[[source: xterm-xtermcap-c]]` 70-244), compiled at build time into a hashed index that finds any name in one or two compares. `Co` and `colors` answer 256, `RGB` 8, and `TN` and `name` the `TERM` the app gives the child, `xterm-256color`, refused on a core no embedder told. A key answers the key as it would be encoded now -- DECCKM, DECKPAM, `modifyOtherKeys`, the kitty flags, DECBKM, `?1037` and `S8C1T` included -- the press xterm's own query simulates: `kf13` is F13, `CSI 25 ~`, as terminfo(5) and xterm's table name it, and `kcbt` at `modifyOtherKeys` 2 is `CSI 27 ; 2 ; 9 ~`, ctlseqs' own example for that level (Jev, LIVE, 0.98/0.94). A key that sends nothing in that state -- Help, Find, Select, Clear, Undo and F36-F63, which baud has no code for, F21-F35 before a kitty flag, every key under KAM -- answers the empty string and the list goes on, xterm's answer for Undo, Clear and KAM, and valid by the text's one definition of an invalid name, "one not found in xterm's tables" (Jev's lean VALID_EMPTY, 0.80/0.88). **Any other name is refused**, ending the list: the entry's output strings, numbers and booleans, `Ms`, `Tc` and the other extensions, which the text calls "intentionally not part of this interface" and which are "not found in xterm's tables" (Jev's leans XTERM_TABLE, 0.88/0.88, and REFUSE for `Ms`, 0.86/0.96, the conforming options). A list cut at the 512-byte string buffer ends before the name the cut fell in; an answer that does not fit behind the replies pending is dropped whole and counted; after a parameter, `DCS 1 + q` is another DCS, as xterm's `do_dcs` reads it (`xtgettcap_answers_a_key_as_it_would_be_sent_now`, `xtgettcap_a_key_that_sends_nothing_answers_empty`, `xtgettcap_refuses_a_name_not_in_xterms_table`, `xtgettcap_a_cut_list_ends_before_the_name_the_cut_fell_in`, `every_name_in_xterms_table_is_found_and_no_other`). Where baud differs from xterm's practice: `ka1`, `kc1`, `ka3` and `kc3` answer baud's own keypad Home, End, PageUp and PageDown, where xterm remaps them onto the editing keys before it encodes them; `kcbt` at `modifyOtherKeys` 2 answers the live Shift-Tab, where xterm answers `CSI Z`; and a refused name gets no trailing `;` or echo, which xterm writes (R1). While a ReGIS one-shot input would take the key, the answer is still the key's encoding. Before 2026-10-21 it was consumed and counted, unanswered |
| `DCS + p Pt ST` | XTSETTCAP set termcap/terminfo data | **out** — refused and counted in `Term::tcap_sets_refused`, and nothing changes. **Every retrieval takes the text's own failure path.** It has xterm "retrieve data from the terminal database" by the name it is given, and that database is the host's: xterm's `set_termcap` hands the name to `setupterm`, or `tgetent` in a termcap build. baud consults no host database, so no retrieval succeeds, and only "If successful" does the name override the one XTGETTCAP uses (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 535-542; `[[source: xterm-xtermcap-c]]` 48-51 and 624-657; note AM, sections 3 and 8.4). xterm's own code, as practice, changes no answer outside its tcap keyboard, `?1050`, which baud does not have, and never `TN`'s, which answers the `termName` resource under every keyboard (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 5225-5226). Jev leaned to counting it, COUNT_REFUSE, 0.53/0.41, under the bar (`xtsettcap_is_refused_and_counted`) |
| `DCS + Q Pt ST` | XTGETXRES request resource values | done — since 2026-10-22 **a program can ask what baud starts from by xterm's resource names**: `DCS 1 + R name=value ST`, both in hex, in xterm's `do_dcs` shape byte for byte (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 510-533; `[[source: xterm-misc]]`'s file, misc.c 5251-5306; Jev, XL_XTERM, 0.98/0.98): the first name decides the `1` or `0`, each name is echoed as sent and a known one gets `=` and its value, a boolean `true` or `false` and an integer in decimal, upper-case hex as `x_encode_hex` writes it; the list ends after the first unknown name, echoed with the `;` after it if one was sent; an empty name, an odd count of hex digits or a byte that is not hex where a name starts ends it before that name, and with no first name there is no reply, as xterm sends none, and the list is counted in `unsupported_param`, as XTQMODKEYS counts a resource it does not have (`x_decode_hex`, `[[source: xterm-xstrings-c]]`; `xtgetxres_answers_a_resource_and_echoes_an_unknown_one`, `a_list_ends_after_its_first_unknown_name`, `a_list_with_no_first_name_gets_no_reply`). **Sixty-three names answer** (Jev, X_STARTUP, 0.98/0.88 then 0.98/0.97), each as the value baud starts from, ctlseqs' "XTerm evaluates resources at startup time", so a program that sets a mode does not move its resource (`a_resource_does_not_follow_the_mode`): the seven `allow*` resources, `true` while `OSC 60` lists the category, from the same facts (`the_allow_resources_agree_with_osc_60`); the initial value of each of the thirty-six modes baud has that starts from an xterm resource -- `appcursorDefault`, `appkeypadDefault`, `jumpScroll` (DECSCLM reset), `autoWrap`, `reverseWrap`, `c132`, `reverseVideo`, `marginBell`, `curses`, `scrollBar`, `cursorBlinkXOR` (`?14`, permanently reset), `alternateScroll`, `bellIsUrgent`, `popOnBell`, `metaSendsEscape`, `altSendsEscape`, `eightBitInput` and `eightBitMeta` (both `?1034`), `deleteIsDEL`, `backarrowKey`, `numLock`, `privateColorRegisters`, `sixelScrolling` (DECSDM reset), `sixelScrollsRight`, `titeInhibit` (`?1046` reset), `scrollTtyOutput`, `scrollKey`, `fastScroll` and `keepClipboard` (permanently set), `utf8` and `privateWidth` (read-only), `keepSelection`, `selectToClipboard`, `printerFormFeed`, `printerExtent` and `logging` -- each held to a fresh terminal's DECRQM answer by a list of xterm's own resource and mode pairs, read off `dpmodes`, `xterm_resources` and ctlseqs' DECSET list rather than off baud's table, which also requires the names whose mode baud does not recognise, `shiftFonts` (`?35`) and `emojiWidth` (`?1022`), to be unknown (`every_mode_resource_is_the_fresh_terminals_mode`); `eightBitControl`, `titleModes` and the thirteen `modify*Keys` and `format*Keys` baud has, held to a fresh terminal's DECRQSS, XTQMODKEYS and XTQFMTKEYS answers (`the_setting_resources_are_the_fresh_terminals_settings`); and the five the embedder gives at start, `cursorBlink`, `cursorUnderLine` and `cursorBar` from the initial cursor style, `pointerMode` from the initial pointer mode and `cjkWidth` from the ambiguous width (`the_embedders_resources_answer_what_it_set`). **Every other name is unknown**, as xterm answers a name it has no resource for: `termName` among them, an application resource of xterm's and not the VT100 widget's, and `decTerminalID`, which would name a terminal identity, the owner's pick (DA1 `?6c`). The answer follows `S7C1T`/`S8C1T` and is sent whole or dropped and counted in `reply_overflows`; a list cut at the string limit ends before the name the cut fell in, as XTGETTCAP's does |
| `CSI ? 6 n` to `CSI ? 85 n` | DEC private device status reports | done — since 2026-10-19 an application that asks a VT420 question gets the VT420 answer, each true of baud: **`?6`** DECXCPR, `CSI ? Pl ; Pc ; 1 R`, the cursor and its page, counted from the margins under DECOM as CPR is (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-55, PDF 310; `decxcpr_reports_the_cursor_with_its_page`, `decxcpr_counts_from_the_margins_under_decom_as_cpr_does`); **`?15`** `?10n`, ready, while the embedder has a printer; `?11n`, not ready, while it has none but had one since power-up or `RIS`, its prints queued; else `?13n`, "not connected" (070-7 pp. 7-32 and 7-33, PDF 710-711): since 2026-10-20 the macOS app has one while `[screen] printing` is on and macOS lists a printer, and the Linux app never, so the answer and what a print does agree, as 070-7 § 7.3.2 ties them; **since 2026-10-21 the answer is the printer's when it is asked**: on macOS the read stops at `?15` (`PrinterHold::On`), the printer list is read again off the main thread and the answer goes before the rest of the read is parsed, so a printer added or removed while baud keeps focus is seen at once, and a list that does not come back within 100 ms is answered from the last one (Jev's lean, HOLD, 0.92/0.89; `a_held_dsr_15_is_answered_from_the_printer_the_embedder_finds`, `a_held_printer_question_waits_for_the_drivers_answer`) (`dsr_15_answers_ready_with_a_printer_and_not_connected_without`, `losing_the_printer_leaves_a_run_collecting`, `ris_forgets_a_lost_printer_and_keeps_a_present_one`; the MC row); **`?25`** `?23n`, "No User Defined Keys are present. The device does not support this extension", where xterm, which has them, answers `?20n` (070-11 p. 11-7, PDF 974: "A report will be given even if the UDK extension is not supported"), true while DECUDK stays out (its row below), and whether it is built, so that this answers `?20n` or `?21n` (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 1436-1438), is the owner's open call; **`?26`** `?27 ; 0 ; 0 n`, the keyboard language "cannot be determined" and the keyboard ready (`[[source: vt510-dsr-kbd]]`), with no keyboard type since every `Ptyp` names a DEC keyboard (Jev's lean, 0.81 and 0.93, taken after a second ask); **`?55`** `?50n`, locator ready, and **`?56`** `?57 ; 1 n`, a mouse (070-13, PDF 1030; `[[source: vt3xx-tp]]` pp. 203-204), once the embedder says it has a pointer (`Term::set_locator_device`; both apps do: macOS always, Linux while the seat names one), and `?53n`, "No locator", and `?57 ; 0 n`, "Unknown Locator", until then, a DECRQLP or DECEFR answering DECLRP's "unavailable" (`a_core_with_no_pointer_has_no_locator`); **`?62`** DECMSR `CSI 0 * {`, no macro space (p. 5-185, PDF 440), in decimal where xterm writes `0000`; **`?63 ; Pid`** DECCKSR `DCS Pid ! ~ 0000 ST`, the checksum of no macro definitions (p. 5-181, PDF 436); **`?75`** `?73n` once after power-up or `RIS` and `?70n` after, 070-4's algorithm (PDF 240), where xterm always sends `?70n`; `?71n` reports a communication error, and a pty has no parity or framing to fail (`data_integrity_reports_the_power_up_once_and_then_ok`); **`?85`** `?83n`, one session (070-4, PDF 239). Each in the text's shape (`each_private_status_answers_what_baud_has`, `the_reports_follow_s8c1t`, `the_reports_are_the_same_at_every_split`). **Answered while DA says VT102**, as DECRQSS, DECRQM and DECRQCRA already are: decided by Jev 2026-10-19 (ANSWER_AT_EVERY_LEVEL, 1.00 and 0.98), with the owner's pick "Keep ?6c (Recommended)" and the standing rule quoted; xterm as a VT102 answers `?6` without the page and nothing else. **A source conflict**: `[[source: vt420-rm]]` and `[[source: vt510-dsr-xcpr]]` print DECXCPR without the `?`, STD 070 and `[[source: ctlseqs]]` with it, decided for STD 070 by Jev (0.92 and 0.92). A `Ps` no text gives stays silent and counted (`an_unknown_private_status_is_silent_and_counted`) |
| `CSI Ps i` / `CSI ? Ps i` | MC media copy: print page, line, screen and all pages; autoprint; printer controller mode | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program's print opens the macOS print panel**, which shows what will print and names the window, and nothing reaches paper until the user presses Print: the owner's call of 2026-10-20 ("printing in through the macOS print panel", Jev 0.98/0.98; note AW, section 1). Built to `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-7, the Printer Port Extension, and `[[source: vt420-rm]]` chapter 11: `CSI i` or `CSI 0 i` prints the page, the scrolling region while DECPEX is reset, and `CSI ? 11 i` all pages, baud's page memory being the one page (Jev's lean ONE_PAGE, 0.80/0.70 then 0.66/0.66; xterm prints its saved lines too); `CSI ? 10 i` the composed display, whatever DECPEX says; `CSI ? 1 i` the cursor's line, within DECSLRM's margins while DECPEX is reset (070-7, PRINT LINE), the cursor not moving; each but the line ended by `FF` under DECPFF. **Autoprint**, `CSI ? 5 i` to `CSI ? 4 i`, prints each line the cursor leaves by `LF`, `VT`, `FF` or an autowrap, ended by `CR` and that character (070-7 § 7.3.7), `IND` and `NEL` printing nothing. **Printer controller mode**, `CSI 5 i` to `CSI 4 i`, sends the host's bytes to the printer and not the screen, but NUL, XON and XOFF and the sequences that turn it on and off (§ 7.3.6), where xterm matches only the literal four, `[[source: xterm-print-c]]`; any other sequence, `RIS`, `DECSTR` and `CSI 0 i` among them, is the printer's, as § 7.3.6 and DECSTR's note d say; `CSI 4 i` ends autoprint too (its reset algorithm); a sequence cut between reads is held for the next, so where the reads fall changes nothing (`a_controller_run_is_the_same_at_every_split`); under ISO 8859-1 the 8-bit `CSI 4 i` ends it. While it runs **the title says "Printing"**, as the screen is not updated. **What a job holds** (Jev, STD070_UTF8, 0.95/0.95 and 0.97/0.98): 070-7 § 7.4's stream -- `ESC [ 0 m`, `ESC # 5`/`6`/`3`/`4` at each line, `ESC [ 0 ; Ps m` at each change with bold 1, underline 4, blink 5 and reverse 7 and nothing else, invisible text as spaces, trailing spaces of the normal rendition left off, `CR LF` a line -- in UTF-8 with no character-set designations, since a cell holds Unicode. **What one job is**: a print; an autoprint run, a print in the middle of it ending its job there (Jev's lean OWN_JOB, 0.58/0.36 on the first ask, the second split by order, so the job definition Jev took at 0.98 decides it); a controller run. The core keeps the finished jobs (`baud_core::PRINT_QUEUE`, more joined to the last after a form feed) in an area the embedder sizes, 2 MiB in the app, bytes past it dropped, counted and the job marked cut, a controller run going on to its `CSI 4 i` (Jev's leans flipped, and ending it at the bound would draw printer data on the screen, a departure); **a printer lost after it was there is "not ready"**, § 7.3.2's third state: `?15` answers `?11n`, a run keeps collecting and a new print is queued as a job, "print operations shall be queued", the app holding the jobs until macOS lists a printer again (`losing_the_printer_leaves_a_run_collecting`, `a_lost_printer_holds_the_jobs_until_one_is_back`), and `RIS` forgets a printer that is gone (`ris_forgets_a_lost_printer_and_keeps_a_present_one`). **The app** takes the jobs after each read, at most one panel at a time, those finishing meanwhile waiting as one, each on a new page, up to 2 MiB (Jev's lean MERGE), lays each out off the main thread in the system's monospaced font sized to the widest line, a double-height pair printed once at double size (Jev's lean DOUBLE_SIZE), and shows the panel as a sheet once the read's callback has returned (`the_print_panel_is_always_shown_as_a_sheet`). **With no printer since power-up or `RIS` every print function is ignored and counted** in `Term::print_refusals`, never entering controller mode (§ 7.3.2): `[screen] printing = "off"`, which is read at launch, no printer listed since launch, the Linux app, and a core no embedder told. VT52's `ESC ]`, `ESC V`, `ESC ^`, `ESC _`, `ESC W` and `ESC X` are the same functions ([protocols/vt52.md](protocols/vt52.md)). `RIS` ends an autoprint run, its job kept. **Pictures print through ReGIS's hard copy** (the ReGIS row): "Graphics to Printer", `CSI ? i` and `CSI ? 0 i` (`[[source: vt3xx-gp-002]]` ch. 16), is accepted and changes nothing, a hard copy always going to the printer, since 2026-10-21 (`graphics_to_printer_is_accepted_and_to_host_refused`); **a print screen stays text** where a picture is on the screen, 070-7 § 7.6.2's "may be transmitted as sixels" being a guideline (Jev's lean, TEXT_ONLY, 0.35/0.28 after a re-ask). Refused and counted apart: the out-of-scope row's kinds. Counted as unsupported before (`with_no_printer_every_print_function_is_ignored_and_counted`, `printer_controller_mode_hides_output_and_collects_it`, `autoprint_prints_each_line_the_cursor_leaves`, `the_stream_carries_renditions_line_sizes_and_hidden_text_as_spaces`, `vt52_print_commands_print`, `the_area_bound_cuts_a_job_and_counts_the_bytes`) |
| `CSI & u` | DECRQUPSS request user-preference supplemental set | done — since 2026-10-19, answered `DCS 0 ! u % 5 ST`, DEC Supplemental (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-125, PDF 380): the set `ESC ( <` draws, and the only one baud's repertory can make the UPSS while DECAUPSS changes nothing (`decrqupss_answers_dec_supplemental`). "Takes no parameters", so `CSI 1 & u` is no request and is counted (`a_parameter_on_decrqupss_or_decrqde_is_no_request`) |
| `DCS Ps ! u Dscs ST` | DECAUPSS assign user-preference supplemental set | **partial** — consumed as a control string and not acted on. The one assignment baud's repertory can honour, `0 ; % 5`, leaves the UPSS what it already is; the other STD 070 allows, `1 ; A`, ISO Latin-1 supplemental (p. 5-123, PDF 378), has the 96-character designation and GR invocation it needs since 2026-10-19, and waits on note AE's Q14 reading of `ESC ( <`. **The gap** is that assignment and the parser edge that recognises the string; decided to record rather than build here by Jev 2026-10-19 (RECORD_GAP, 0.90 and 0.94) |
| `CSI " v` | DECRQDE request displayed extent | done — since 2026-10-19, answered DECRPDE `CSI Ph ; Pw ; 1 ; 1 ; 1 " w`: every line and column of the one page, shown from its first cell (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-88, PDF 343; `decrqde_answers_the_whole_page`). Takes no parameters, as DECRQUPSS |
| `CSI s` (DECSLRM) | left/right margins | done — with DECLRMM (`?69`). **An omitted or 0 `Pr` is the page's right border**: "default Pr: Last Column", and the algorithm reads a 0 as omitted (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` DECSLRM, pp. 5-27 and 5-28); "Default: Pr = 80 or 132 (depending on the page width)" (`[[source: vt510-decslrm]]`); as xterm (charproc.c 4891-4894, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`) and ghostty (`[[source: ghostty-d9840f3-terminal]]` 2610) read it, since 2026-10-13 (`a_bare_decslrm_sets_the_margins_to_the_page_borders`, `a_zero_right_margin_is_the_page_border`, `an_omitted_right_margin_is_the_page_border`, with `an_omitted_left_margin_is_column_one` the control); until then it decoded as column 1, so `CSI s` and `CSI 5 s` were parsed and dropped. An inverted or one-column pair is rejected. **A `Pr` past the page leaves the margins alone**: STD 070's note 3 (p. 5-27), "this control will be ignored (not executed)", since 2026-10-13 (`a_right_margin_past_the_page_leaves_the_margins_alone`); until then it was clamped to the last column, as xterm and ghostty still do (pinned in `bench-others/ghostty-oracle`). Margins act for writing, for `ICH`/`DCH`/`DECIC`/`DECDC`, and since 2026-10-12 for every vertical scroll: `SU`, `SD`, `IND`, `RI`, `LF`, a wrap at the bottom margin, `IL` and `DL` move only the rectangle (`[[source: vt510-decslrm]]`, "sets the left and right margins to define the scrolling region"; `[[source: dec-std-070]]` § 5.4.3 and its `scroll_up`/`scroll_down`, § 5.14.2-3), on either screen, the whole screen and a top region included, and keep nothing in the history (`tests/vt420/rect_scroll.rs`). A wide character a side margin cuts is erased whole first, and until 2026-10-21 an image moved when its anchor was inside the rectangle: no text settles either, both listed for Jev (`docs/record.md`, "Rectangle scrolling"); the images' rule is below. Held against ghostty by `bench-others/ghostty-oracle`. **Line starts and moves honour the fence with DECOM reset**, since 2026-10-15: `CR`, `NEL`, `CNL`, `CPL` and `LF` under `LNM` by `CR`'s rule, `CUF` and `CUB` stop at the margins, `IL` and `DL` put the cursor on the left margin (`tests/vt420/line_starts.rs`; their rows), and `HPR` passes the right margin (its row); `HT` and `BS` stop at them by where the cursor starts since 2026-10-14. **A cursor a move put right of the fence writes on to the screen's last column**, since 2026-10-15 (note V's V10 step (d), the owner's C2-min): `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s insert-or-replace algorithm bounds a write by the right margin only from at or inside it, else by the line's end (pp. 5-139 and 5-140), and xterm's `dotext` takes `last_col` past `rmargin` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`, charproc.c 6991-6995 and 7055-7058). A write that does not fit there wraps to the left margin, and with `DECAWM` reset the last column is the screen's; the cursor reports its own column and no wrap is pending. **At exactly right + 1 a move places the cursor** (`baud_domain::CursorCol`, a tag only cold code sets), so `CUP`, `HVP`, `CHA`, `HPA`, `HPR`, `VPA`, `CUB` and `BS` land on that column, `IL`/`DL`, `ICH`/`DCH` and `DECIC`/`DECDC` are inert there, `CUF` and `HT` go on from it, and `DECSC`/`DECRC` keep it placed; the sentinel a write leaves there is still a pending wrap on the margin (`tests/vt420/corner.rs`, 18 cases at every read size and split). `ICH` and `DCH` right of the fence are ignored, ICH note 2 (p. 5-142) and DCH note 3 (p. 5-144); they underflowed there, a panic in a debug build. Rows outside the top and bottom margins keep the right margin, where STD 070 gives the line's last column and the first column (p. 5-139), as xterm does: open. Under DECOM it homes to the margins' corner, `CUP` addresses from the left margin and every move stays inside the margins, since 2026-10-13 (the `?6` row). **Since 2026-10-21 the pictures in the rectangle move with it and the rest stay**: the ReGIS page's pixels in the rectangle move (`a_rectangle_scroll_moves_the_page_only_inside_the_margins`), and a Sixel across a side margin is split, the part inside moving as an image of its own (Jev's lean, SPLIT; `a_sixel_across_a_side_margin_is_split`, `a_sixel_inside_moves_and_one_outside_stays`), the part moving its whole height by its anchor as a whole image in a band does; where the arena has no room for the part, its pixels move within the image instead, only its rows inside the band, and pixels a move takes past the image's top, moving up, or past the band's bottom or the image's, whichever comes first, moving down, are lost (`with_no_room_to_split_the_pixels_move_within_the_image`) |
| `CSI ' }` / `CSI ' ~` | DECIC / DECDC | done — the columns brought in are empty in the normal rendition, "blank with normal rendition" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` pp. 5-150 and 5-151; `[[source: vt510-decic]]`, "no visual character attributes"), where ICH and DCH take the current colours; until 2026-10-19 DECIC and DECDC took them too (`decic_and_decdc_bring_in_columns_with_normal_rendition`). Every row of the region shifts to the right margin, a double line's past its end, and an image stays where it is (the `ESC # 6` row) |
| `CSI Pn $ \|` | DECSCPP select columns per page | **partial** — since 2026-10-14: the width asked for (`Term::requested_width`), by `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s `set_columns_per_page` (p. 5-74), `Pn` up to 80 -- omitted and 0 among them -- is 80 and anything past it 132, where xterm takes 0, 80 and 132 and ignores the rest (`decscpp_reads_its_parameter_as_std_070_does`). Nothing else here: no clear, no margin moved, no cursor moved, and `?3` not moved, since the algorithm changes `columns_per_page` and not `column_mode`, where xterm sets `IN132COLUMNS` (`decscpp_asks_without_40_and_leaves_the_screen`). **Not gated by `?40`**, as neither text nor xterm gates it -- the owner's pick named `?3`, and the record lists this. Under `WidthHold::On` the read stops at it, as at `?3` (`decscpp_holds_and_two_requests_stop_a_read_twice`); `RIS` after a request asks back for the width the window had before it (the `ESC c` row). `DECRQSS` for `$ \|` answers the page the grid is, by the same rule -- up to 80 columns is 80, wider is 132 -- where xterm answers the width last asked (`decrqss_answers_decscpp_with_the_grids_page`; the record's open question). `CSI ? Pn $ \|` is no control and asks nothing. **The gap**: the change is the embedder's `Term::resize`, which reflows the primary and resets all four margins, where STD 070 (p. 5-73) keeps the cursor and the right margin unless they lie past the new edge, preserves columns 1-80 and clears 81-132 |
| `CSI Ps $ ~` | DECSSDT select status line type | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can open a line of its own under the screen**, or ask for baud's: `[[source: vt420-rm]]` pp. 219-221 and `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-14 (pp. 14-4 to 14-13). 0 no line, 1 the indicator, 2 the host-writable line; an omitted `Ps` is 0, the VT420's "0 (default)" and 070-14's "If Ps is omitted, it is assumed to be 0", as xterm's code reads it, where ctlseqs' annotation marks 1; any other value is counted. **The line is a row beside the main display, never one of its rows**: `Term::geometry` and every size a program asks for stay the main display's, and the window grows a row to hold it and gives it back (`Term::status_rows`; the owner's call of 2026-10-20, Jev GROW_WINDOW 0.98/1.00), or, in full screen, in a window as tall as the screen allows, maximised on Wayland or with `[window] operations = "off"`, the grid gives the row up and takes it back when the line goes, and under `BAUD_GRID`, which pins the grid, the window and the grid both keep their size (`the_status_line_grows_the_window_where_it_can_change_size`, `a_window_as_tall_as_the_screen_gives_the_grid_row_up`, `a_cell_resize_keeps_the_status_lines_row`). The line's resize comes before the window operations of the read that showed it, so `CSI 8 ; 30 t` in the same read gives thirty rows and the line, and a move keeps the line's row (`the_status_lines_frame_comes_before_a_pumps_requests`, `a_wayland_window_grows_by_the_line_first`). **No line at power-on**, 070-14's "No Status Line (factory default)", where the VT420 powers up with the indicator (Jev's lean, NONE, 0.78/0.86, then 0.80/0.92); `RIS` "Erases and exits the status line" to none. A type changed is initialized, "the Host Writable (Remote) Status Line is cleared", and a type other than host-writable leaves the line, "the Status Display will be exited" (p. 14-9). **The indicator** is the terminal's state in reverse video, "the reverse of the Main Display background" (p. 14-5), at fixed columns with a space between (§14.2.1.2): the page and the cursor's line and column, `1(LL,CCC)` as in the VT340's example, the VT420's first two fields (`[[source: vt420-uu-ch4]]` Table 4-2), the page always baud's one where 070-14 asks for it "if more than one page is available"; "Wait" while KAM locks the keyboard, the VT420's keyboard indicator; and the locator's position while DEC locator reporting is on, 070-14's Graphics Input Position Indicator, "only visible when graphics input is enabled" (p. 14-7), so it goes with the pointer event whose report turns a one-shot DECELR off, as it did only at the next event until 2026-10-21 (`the_locator_field_goes_when_a_one_shot_report_disables_the_locator`), a frame drawn for every pointer event while the indicator is shown, on both drivers, as "The interval between the change in the state of a feature and the update of the appropriate indicator shall not exceed 80 milliseconds" (pp. 14-5 and 14-6; `a_move_redraws_the_indicator_line`). **And the printer**, since 2026-10-21, at column 42 where the VT340's line has it, in the VT420's words: `Printer: None` with no printer since power-up, `Ready`, `Not Ready` once it is lost, `Auto Print` while autoprint is on and `Controller` during a printer controller run, a run's mode shown over the port's state (Jev's lean, RUN_FIRST, 0.62/0.79 then 0.76/0.91), drawn again as soon as the embedder's printer changes (`the_indicator_shows_the_printer_and_the_print_mode`). No session, edit-mode or modem field: baud has one session and no local editing, and the VT420 leaves the modem field "blank, unless you have a modem connected", as 070-14's is "not displayed if Modem Controls are not selected", and a pty has none (`the_indicator_shows_the_page_and_the_cursor_in_reverse_video`, `the_indicator_shows_wait_while_the_keyboard_is_locked`). Level 1 does not have it, "DECSSDT and DECSASD do not work in Level 1 (VT100 mode)" (p. 14-13; `level_1_does_not_have_them`); a storage that lends no row has none and counts it (`a_storage_that_declines_the_row_has_no_line`, `the_line_writes_the_row_the_embedder_lends`). Every renderer draws it below the main display, the software and instance paths from the converted row and the cell shader from the window copy's row after the screen's, its glyphs by records (`the_frame_grows_a_row_for_the_line_and_gives_it_back`, `the_indicator_is_drawn_in_reverse_video_below_the_main_display`); a smooth-scroll slide does not run while it is shown |
| `CSI Ps $ }` | DECSASD select active status display | done — since 2026-10-20: 0 the main display, 1 the host-writable line, which "must be selected through DECSSDT for DECSASD to be able to select" it (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-14 p. 14-11; `decsasd_selects_the_line_only_when_it_is_host_writable`); any other value is counted. **The line is a second display of one row** with "its own ANSI text state and settings" (§14.2): 070-14's list of what each keeps, the active position and its pending wrap, the rendition and DECSCA, origin mode, G0-G3, GL, GR and the single shifts, and the `DECSC` slot, which the VT420 keeps "separate ... for the main display and the status line" (`[[source: vt420-rm]]` p. 271); the character sets each display's where the VT420 shares them (Jev, SEPARATE, 0.98/1.00), DECAWM shared and its pending wrap each display's (Jev, MODE_SHARED_FLAG_SEPARATE, 0.94/0.96), and DECTCEM each display's, 070-14 item 14 (`the_line_keeps_its_own_rendition_charsets_and_saved_cursor`, `autowrap_is_shared_and_the_pending_wrap_is_each_displays`). Tabs, IRM and the width are shared (`tabs_and_irm_are_shared`). On the line "only the column parameters in cursor positioning control functions ... are significant", CUU and CUD do nothing, CPR reports line 1 (`only_the_columns_position_the_cursor_on_the_line`), ED, EL and ECH erase it alone (`an_erase_on_the_line_erases_the_line_alone`), and a scroll erases it, "a single line Host Writable Status Line is erased", retiring no line into the history (`scrolling_on_the_line_erases_it_and_keeps_no_history`). DECANM is ignored there (`decanm_is_ignored_on_the_line`); DECCOLM clears it and homes its cursor (`deccolm_clears_the_line_and_homes_its_cursor`); DECSTR and DECSCL leave it, not erased, and reset its own state where it waits as they reset the main display's (`the_resets_exit_the_line_as_070_says`, `decstr_resets_the_lines_own_state`). The cursor is drawn on the line while it is selected, on every path (`the_host_writable_line_draws_its_text_and_its_cursor`, `the_cursor_is_drawn_on_the_status_line_while_it_is_selected`, `the_cell_path_draws_the_status_line_as_the_instance_path_does`). What names a line of the main display does not reach the line: a Sixel, ReGIS or kitty image and an `OSC 133` mark sent there are counted and not acted on, and text and erases there free none of the main display's images, on either screen (`the_line_leaves_the_main_displays_images_alone`); the alternate screen, DECLRMM's single-width pages and the history's erase act on the main display (`declrmm_from_the_line_resets_the_main_displays_lines`); the selection and the margin bell are left where they are; and the rendition table's and the cluster store's sweeps keep what the line names (`a_sweep_keeps_the_lines_renditions`, `a_cluster_sweep_keeps_the_lines_clusters`). A press over the line is a press past the grid's last row, clamped to it as over the padding, and the locator reports it outside. xterm's `OPT_STATUS_LINE` ignores on the line DECDHL, DECSWL, DECDWL, CUU, CUD, VPA, VPR, ED, XTHIMOUSE and SD (both `CASE_TRACK_MOUSE`, which reads a single nonzero parameter as scroll-down, charproc.c 4030-4036), DECSTBM, DECALN, XTSMGRAPHICS, CAN, SUB, SPA, EPA, SU, IND, CPL, CNL, NEL, RI, the locator's controls and the rectangle operations (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, lines 3557-3598), where 070-14 has ED and a scroll act there: a departure not followed. baud lets DECDWL and DECDHL act on the line (`StatusLine::rendition`), where xterm ignores them and 070-14 says nothing of them |
| `CSI Pts;Pls;Pbs;Prs;Pps;Ptd;Pld;Ppd $ v` | DECCRA copy rectangular area | done — since 2026-10-18. A program can move a block of text and its colours to another place on the screen in one control, the cursor staying put (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-169). The copy is taken as the source stood before it began, so a block moved onto itself does not smear (`deccra_copies_an_overlapping_rectangle_as_it_was`); a destination past the page edge is clipped there. baud has one page, so the page parameters all name it, "treated as the last page" |
| `CSI Pch;Pt;Pl;Pb;Pr $ x` | DECFRA fill rectangular area | done — since 2026-10-18. The block fills with one character in the current colours and attributes (p. 5-170). `Pch` 32-126 goes through the character set in use, so after `ESC ( 0` a 113 draws a line; 160-255 is the Latin-1 supplement under either input encoding (Jev 2026-10-18, LATIN1, 0.92 and 0.98); anything else is ignored, as the text says, and counted. xterm also takes Unicode values, an extension baud does not take. A fill, and a DECCRA, over a picture frees it, as text written over one does; DECERA and DECSERA leave it, as ED and EL do, where xterm clears it under all four. The filled cells take DECSCA's protection as a written character does, as xterm's ScrnFillRectangle keeps PROTECTED in its ATTRIBUTES mask; the text names only "the current renditions set through the SGR command". **One character a position under the wide ambiguous-width policy too**, as a DEC position holds one character and xterm's `ScrnFillRectangle` writes one a column: a filled U+00B0 takes each column where the same character written takes two (`decfra_fills_a_position_each_under_wide_ambiguous_width`) |
| `CSI Pt;Pl;Pb;Pr $ z` | DECERA erase rectangular area | done — since 2026-10-18. Written cells become spaces and empty cells stay empty, all in the default rendition (p. 5-171, "clearing all character attributes"; Jev 2026-10-18, DEFAULT, 0.98 and 0.98). xterm keeps the current SGR instead, and baud's own ED, EL and ECH erase to the current background alone under DECECM reset (the `?117` row). A wide character an edge of DECERA, DECSERA, DECFRA or a DECCRA destination cuts goes whole, its outside half a space in its own colours (Jev, ORPHAN_SPACE, the lean taken) that keeps DECSCA's protection and loses an ECMA-48 guard and an `OSC 8` link (Jev's lean, PROTECTION_ONLY, 0.67/0.70 then 0.83/0.77, log 874-877; `the_half_a_cut_leaves_keeps_its_protection_alone`); DECCARA and DECRARA change only the half inside (INSIDE_ONLY, 0.98 and 0.98) **Since 2026-10-21 it clears the Sixel and ReGIS pixels in the positions it erases** (the Sixel row; `each_erase_clears_the_sixel_in_its_positions`, `each_erase_clears_the_page_in_its_positions`). |
| `CSI Pt;Pl;Pb;Pr $ {` | DECSERA selective erase rectangular area | done — since 2026-10-18. Only characters DECSCA left erasable become spaces, keeping their colours; protected and empty cells are untouched (p. 5-172). The protection read is DECSCA's. The space keeps an ECMA-48 guard and an `OSC 8` link, as DECSED's does (Jev 2026-10-19, AGREE, 1.00 and 0.98; the link Jev's lean, KEEP; `decsera_keeps_the_guard_and_the_link`). **Since 2026-10-21 it clears the Sixel and ReGIS pixels in the positions it erases and keeps them over a protected or unoccupied one** (the Sixel row; `a_selective_erase_clears_only_the_positions_it_erases`, `a_selective_erase_spares_the_page_over_a_protected_position`). |
| `CSI Pt;Pl;Pb;Pr;Ps.. $ r` / `$ t` | DECCARA / DECRARA change and reverse attributes in a rectangle | done — since 2026-10-18. Bold, underline, blink and reverse change or flip across the block without touching the text or its colours (pp. 5-173 to 5-176). Each parameter acts as its SGR namesake does, so 24 clears any underline style and 22 faint too (Jev 2026-10-18, AS_SGR, the lean taken at 0.70 and 0.88); values the text does not list, xterm's 8 and 28 among them, are ignored. DECCARA with no valid parameter clears the four; DECRARA with none does nothing, as STD 070 says, where the VT420 and VT510 manuals reverse all four (Jev, IGNORE, 0.94 and 0.98). A cell is given a new rendition of its own, so text outside the block that shared one keeps it, and a selection on the rows stays, as in xterm |
| `CSI Ps * x` | DECSACE select attribute change extent | done — since 2026-10-18. 0 and 1 make DECCARA and DECRARA run as a stream of text from the first corner to the second, skipping empty cells; 2 makes them a rectangle, empty cells turned into blanks (p. 5-177). DECRQSS answers it (`*x`, `[[source: vt420-rm]]` Table 12-4); RIS returns it to the stream and DECSTR keeps it, as its table does not list it |
| `CSI Ps # y` | XTCHECKSUM select checksum extension | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can ask for xterm's other checksums of the screen**: "The bits of Ps modify the calculation of the checksum returned by DECRQCRA" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`). Bit 0 leaves the sum positive (`bit_0_does_not_negate`), bit 1 leaves out the video attributes (`bit_1_leaves_the_attributes_out`), bit 2 counts an unwritten cell as a space (`bit_2_counts_an_unwritten_cell_as_a_space`), bit 3 omits unwritten cells even under bit 2, as ctlseqs says, where xterm's code reads it "do not skip uninitialized cells" (`bit_3_omits_unwritten_cells_even_under_bit_2`; Jev's lean, TEXT, 0.92/0.82 and 0.90/0.72 after a second ask), bit 4 counts whole code points, a line-drawing cell its Unicode value and U+FFF0 to U+FFFF as themselves, where xterm skips that range (`bit_4_counts_whole_code_points`; Jev's lean on the first ask, COUNT, 0.30/0.00; the second ask split); a code and its weights wrap at sixteen bits, as xterm's `int` total does before `checksum &= 0xffff`, so a bold U+FFFD sums 0x007D (`bit_4_weights_wrap_at_sixteen_bits`). Combining marks count under bit 2 and not bit 4, as xterm's code adds them (`marks_count_under_bit_2_and_not_bit_4`; Jev, CODE, 0.94/0.98). A bare `Ps` is 0 and bits above 4, the manual's bit 5 among them, are ignored, as xterm's code reads five (`a_bare_ps_is_0_and_high_bits_are_ignored`; Jev's lean, IGNORE_HIGH). `RIS` and `DECSTR` restore 0 (`both_resets_restore_the_default`). Ignored and counted below Level 4, as xterm's `vtXX_level >= 4` (`below_level_4_it_is_ignored_and_counted`; Jev's lean on the second ask, GATE, 0.10/0.04, after UNGATED 0.24/0.34 on the first). Honoured with `[screen] checksum` off, which then answers nothing, as a default xterm allows `SetChecksum` and refuses `GetChecksum` (`with_the_checksum_off_it_is_honoured_and_nothing_is_answered`; Jev's lean, ALWAYS); `OSC 61 ; allowWindowOps` no longer names it. Counted and dropped until then |
| `CSI Pi;Pg;Pt;Pl;Pb;Pr * y` | DECRQCRA request checksum of rectangular area | done — since 2026-10-18. Answered `DCS Pi ! ~ xxxx ST`, four capital hex digits (pp. 5-179 and 5-181), which vttest reads screens back with. No DEC text defines the sum, so it is xterm's DEC mode, the one vttest expects: each written cell's code (a character written through GR or a single shift counts its 7-bit stored code, below), a written space included, plus weights for bold, blink, reverse, underline, invisible and protection, empty cells left out, negated. Any underline style adds 0x10 and either blink 0x40; xterm's csDEC adds 0x10 only for its single-underline bit (SGR 21 sets ATR_DBL_UNDER, which it does not count). **Answered by default, and a setting turns it off**: the owner's call of 2026-10-19 (option 3), which replaced Jev's lean to answer always (ANSWER_ALWAYS, 0.49 against 0.47 for an embedder switch). The embedder sets it (`Term::set_checksum_report`, `ChecksumReport::On` or `Off`), the app from `[screen] checksum`, `"on"` or `"off"`. Off, every request goes unanswered, whatever its corners, and is counted in `checksums_refused`, as xterm sends nothing with `GetChecksum` among its disallowed window operations (`CASE_DECRQCRA`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file); a default xterm keeps it off (`[[source: xterm-man]]`): a program can ask one cell at a time and read back the screen, other programs' output included. No sequence turns it back on, `RIS` and `DECSTR` included, and `DSR ?63`, which sums no macro definitions and reads nothing of the screen, is answered either way, as xterm's is (`decrqcra_goes_unanswered_and_counted_with_the_checksum_off`, `no_reset_turns_the_checksum_back_on`; the app's `the_screen_checksum_is_answered_unless_turned_off`, `the_checksum_setting_reaches_the_core`). XTCHECKSUM changes how the sum is taken (its row). Inverted corners get no reply, as the text says. Since 2026-10-19: **a program checking a form it drew in a national character set gets the sum a DEC terminal gives**, each character counted as the code it was received as -- German `[` drawn as `Ä` counts 0x5B, as DEC STD 070's display stores the code and the set (p. 5-140) and xterm's `charSeen` counts it -- where it counted the drawn character's low byte (Jev, CODE_RECEIVED, 0.98 and 0.98, and 0.98 and 0.98 again when asked with this build's measured instruction counts, log 864/865; `decrqcra_counts_a_national_character_as_the_code_received`). A character sent as UTF-8 was not received as a GL code and counts its own value whatever set GL holds, a UTF-8 `Ä` 0xC4 under German as under ASCII, and REP's copy of it counts the same (`decrqcra_counts_a_utf8_character_as_itself_under_a_national_set`). Line drawing already counted its byte, a departure from xterm, which counts its own internal codes there (Jev, 0.98 and 0.96). The UK `£` counts 0xA3 as xterm's does (XTERM_0xA3, Jev's lean at 0.52 and 0.44, taken). A request with a page and no corners sums the whole page under DECOM too, "If the final four parameters are unspecified, a checksum of page Pp is generated" (p. 5-179; `decrqcra_of_a_page_under_decom_sums_the_whole_page`). A position DECERA, DECSERA, ED, EL or ECH left unoccupied counts nothing, as the texts leave it unoccupied (pp. 5-153 to 5-157, 5-171, 5-172; Jev TEXT 0.98 and 0.98); xterm marks the positions DECERA, DECSERA, EL 1 and an ECH ending mid-line erase as drawn, and counts each as a space, which is its departure, and ED, EL 0 and EL 2 leave them undrawn there too (`decrqcra_leaves_out_what_an_erase_leaves_unoccupied`). Since 2026-10-19 too, **every set that replaces a code counts the code received**: DEC Supplemental's `@` drawn `À` counts 0x40 and its `W` drawn `Œ` 0x57, JIS-Katakana's `1` drawn U+FF71 0x31 and JIS-Roman's `\` drawn `¥` 0x5C, where they counted the drawn character's low byte or 0x1B, so all 63 katakana summed alike (Jev's lean, CODE_RECEIVED, 0.70 and 0.86 after a second ask, log 880/881; `decrqcra_counts_the_code_received_under_every_set`). Open: a reserved DEC Supplemental position counts DEC's error character, 0x1B, as xterm's `unmap_DEC_Supp_Graphic` does, where CODE_RECEIVED would count the code sent; the cell does not keep it. DEC Technical and the other sets that draw their byte, and Greek's unreplaced 7/9 and 7/10, count that byte, where xterm counts its own codes and 0x1B. A character sent as UTF-8 while line drawing is in GL counts itself, a UTF-8 `─` 0x1B, as xterm's `charSeen` does, where it counted `q`'s 0x71; the cell keeps the line-drawing flag, note AE Q16(a) (Jev's lean, OWN_VALUE, 0.82 and 0.94 after a second ask; `decrqcra_counts_a_utf8_character_as_itself_under_line_drawing`). Combining marks are left out of the sum, as xterm's default sum leaves them (Jev, LEFT_OUT, 1.00 and 0.97, log 878/879; `decrqcra_leaves_combining_marks_out`). **A character written through GR or a single shift counts the 7-bit code STD 070's display stores through its set** (p. 5-140), since 2026-10-19: DEC Supplemental `D7` drawn `Œ` counts 0x57 as its `W` does, and a 96-character set's character its code, Latin-1 Supplemental's `A` drawn `Á` 0x41 (Jev's lean STORED_7BIT, 0.44 and 0.70, then 0.48 and 0.58; `the_checksum_counts_the_stored_code_through_gr_and_single_shifts`); xterm counts 0xD7 and 0xC1 |
| `CSI Ps $ w` | DECRQPSR request presentation state report | done — since 2026-10-19. `Ps` 1 answers DECCIR, `DCS 1 $ u Pr;Pc;Pp;Srend;Satt;Sflag;Pgl;Pgr;Scss;Sdesig ST`, and 2 DECTABSR, `DCS 2 $ u` the columns of the tab stops `/` between them `ST`; 0 and any other value are ignored (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` pp. 5-198 to 5-204; `[[source: vt510-decrqpsr]]`, `[[source: vt510-deccir]]`, `[[source: vt510-dectabsr]]`). A program can read the cursor state and the tab stops and put them back with DECRSPS. DECCIR reports the current state of the kinds DECSC saves: the screen position, bold, underline, blink and reverse, the selective erase attribute, origin mode and a pending wrap, GL, GR, the set sizes and each slot's designation as sent, `<` for the user-preference set (070's own default report, `1;1;1;@;@;@;0;2;@;BB<<`, p. 5-203, read off the page; `the_default_report_is_std_070s_example` and a test a field). The single-shift bits are 0, the `CSI` having cancelled any shift. Answered whatever level DA claims, as DECRQSS is, where STD 070 gives the reports "Levels: 2x (TSI), 3-4" and xterm answers at level 3 and up (Jev's lean UNGATED). The reply buffer holds the longest DECTABSR, a stop at every column of the widest screen (`the_longest_dectabsr_is_sent_whole`) |
| `CSI Ps $ u` | DECRQTSR request terminal state report | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program can save most of the terminal's settings in one reply and put them back with DECRSTS**: `Ps` 1 answers DECTSR, `DCS 1 $ s D...D ST`, at most 256 characters as STD 070 guarantees (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` pp. 5-206, 5-207; `[[source: vt3xx-tp-002]]` p. 207), in baud's own layout, since "The format of the data is firmware revision dependent": a version nibble, then every mode DECSET and SM act on -- XTSAVE's sixty-one private modes and IRM, LNM, ERM, KAM and SRM -- every setting DECRQSS answers, DECSCA's among them in the cursor information DECCIR reports, the input encoding `?1020` reports, and the shown screen's `DECSC` save, which `?1048` reports is there and no control reads back, as the VT420 manual's "the entire state of the terminal, except for user-defined key definitions and the current soft character set" has it (`[[source: vt420-rm]]` pp. 245-247; Jev's lean, S_SHOWN, 0.86/0.88 after research; `the_saved_cursor_is_restored`, `the_input_encoding_is_restored`), one nibble a byte in `4/0..4/15` as the VT420's guideline has it, and two checksum bytes, the 2's complement of the 8-bit sum of the data a nibble each, high first (Jev, C_NIBBLE, 0.92/0.96; `decrqtsr_answers_a_report_in_std_070s_shape`). The page size is carried, DECSCPP's and DECSLPP's (Jev's lean, P_CARRY, 0.94/0.88). The tab stops are carried where they fit the bound, a screen up to 248 columns (`the_report_is_the_layouts_length`), and on a wider screen when they are every eighth column; other stops on a wider screen are left to DECTABSR, which reports them at any width (Jev's lean, T_FIT_DEFAULT, 0.64/0.27 after research; `the_tab_stops_are_restored_where_the_report_holds_them`). **Not carried, open**: the other screen's `DECSC` save, which is the primary's `?1049` save while the alternate screen is up, and so on the alternate screen a restore leaves the primary's saved cursor as it finds it; and the state no TSI control reports, the screen's contents, soft fonts, user-defined keys and macros, as STD 070 leaves out "large data items that are not practical to report". `Ps` 2 is DECCTR (its row); 0, omitted and any other `Ps` are ignored, "no report sent" (`decrqtsr_ignores_any_other_report`). The answer follows `S7C1T`/`S8C1T` and is sent whole or dropped and counted in `reply_overflows` |
| `CSI 2 ; Pu $ u` | DECCTR colour table report | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program can read the 256-colour palette and restore it with DECRSTS**: `DCS 2 $ s Pc;Pu;Px;Py;Pz/... ST` (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-109; `[[source: vt520-rm]]` p. 5-161), a group for each colour baud knows -- the program's `OSC 4` colour, else the embedder's palette -- and none for a colour neither gave, as `OSC 4`'s query answers nothing for it (Jev's lean, U_OMIT, 0.85/0.89 after research; `decctr_reports_each_colour_the_core_knows`). `Pu` 2 reports RGB in percent and 1 HLS on DEC's wheel, blue at 0, red at 120 and green at 240; 0 and omitted report HLS, the VT340's default, "0 or none HLS (default)" (`[[source: vt3xx-tp-002]]` p. 206; Jev, D_HLS, 0.92/0.91), where the VT520's page calls 0 illegal; any other `Pu` is ignored (`decctr_reports_hls_by_default_on_decs_wheel`). Percent holds 101 levels, so a colour restored from a report is the nearest of them, which reports the same again (`decrsts_restores_the_colour_table`). The VT520's DECCTR page prints the answer as `DCS 2 $ p`, a copy error: its own DECTSR page and STD 070 give `$ s` |
| `CSI Ps ; Pu ' z` | DECELR enable locator reports | done — since 2026-10-20, **a forms or menu program written for DEC terminals can take the mouse as DEC's text locator** (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-13, PDF 1013-1036; `[[source: ctlseqs]]` 1842-1853): `Ps` 1 reports, 2 reports once and then disables ("a single locator report (DECLRP) shall reset locator reporting mode to disabled", note 1 of DECLRP, a request's answer and a filter's included), 0 disables; `Pu` 1 reports in the device pixels `?1016` uses and 0 or 2 in cells. "Undefined values for Ps or Pu causes the entire sequence to be ignored", counted, where xterm reads them as off and cells (`decelr_with_an_undefined_value_is_ignored_and_counted`). **One of ctlseqs' mutually exclusive mouse protocols**: enabling replaces `?9` and `?1000`-`?1003`, a DECSET of one of them replaces the locator, a DECRST of any turns it off, and `Ps` 0 turns off whichever is in force, as xterm's one `send_mouse_pos` does (Jev, ALL_OFF, 0.92/0.92; `decelr_and_the_xterm_mouse_protocols_replace_each_other`). It cancels a filter rectangle (`decsle_0_and_decelr_cancel_the_filter_rectangle`). `RIS` disables it and its events, units and rectangle; DECSTR, whose table (070-4 p. 31) names none of it, keeps it (`ris_disables_the_locator_and_decstr_keeps_it`). Under it the pointer's presses go to the program, Shift still selects as for the other protocols, and the wheel scrolls as with no protocol, since the locator has no wheel (Jev's lean, AS_UNTRACKED, 0.73/0.77 then 0.53/0.38), where xterm reports its wheel as the fourth button and as event 10. Both drivers feed it: the macOS app and the Linux seat, through `gesture.rs`. Not reportable: DECRQSS answers its final invalid, as neither 070-5's list nor ctlseqs' names it (`decrqss_does_not_read_the_locator_controls`) |
| `CSI Pm ' {` | DECSLE select locator events | done — since 2026-10-20: 1 and 3 report button down and up transitions, 2 and 4 stop them, in order across the parameters; 0 or none, the power-up choice, reports only requests "and also cancels any pending filter rectangle"; a value the text does not give is counted (`decsle_selects_which_button_transitions_report_a_declrp`, `decsle_with_an_undefined_parameter_counts_it`) |
