| `CSI Pe ; Pb ; Pr ; Pc & w` | DECLRP locator report, sent by baud | done — since 2026-10-20, what baud sends; one a host sends baud is counted, as no control it acts on (`a_received_declrp_is_counted`): the event (1 request, 2-9 a button down and up, left, middle, right and fourth, 10 outside the filter rectangle), the buttons held after it ("1-Right 2-Middle 4-Left 8-Fourth"), the row and the column from 1:1. **No `Pp`**: "The page coordinate may be omitted if the locator is on page one", and baud has one page; xterm omits it too. **The position is omitted where it is undefined**, "Pr and Pc shall be omitted" (note 3): off the text area, a pixel the embedder did not give, and under DECOM in cells outside the margins, which 070-13 § 3.1 gives as its example; under DECOM cells count from the margins, as CPR's do, and pixels from the text area (Jev, PIXELS_ABSOLUTE, 0.96/0.94). xterm sends `CSI 0 & w` for an undefined position and ignores DECOM. `CSI 0 & w` alone is "locator unavailable". The fourth button is the first past three, macOS's button 3 and the Wayland seat's `BTN_SIDE` (Jev, FIRST_EXTRA, 0.98/0.98). A press outside a filter rectangle reports the press first, "by increasing event code number" (§ 3). A report the buffer cannot take is dropped and counted, and a one-shot DECELR waits for the next, from the pointer and from DECRQLP or DECEFR alike (`a_dropped_decrqlp_answer_leaves_a_one_shot_decelr_on`, `declrp_names_each_button_and_the_buttons_held_after_it`, `a_press_with_the_position_undefined_omits_the_coordinates`, `under_decom_cells_count_from_the_margins_and_pixels_do_not`, `a_press_outside_the_rectangle_reports_the_press_first`, `a_report_that_does_not_fit_is_dropped_and_a_one_shot_waits`, `declrp_writes_its_three_shapes`, `declrp_follows_s8c1t`) |
| `CSI Pt ; Pl ; Pb ; Pr ' w` | DECEFR enable filter rectangle | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can ask to be told when the pointer leaves a rectangle**, once: event 10 with where it went, or with the position omitted when it left the text area, and the rectangle ends. Omitted corners are the pointer's own, so no corners reports any move to another cell or pixel; a rectangle that does not contain the pointer reports at once. With the pointer away, a rectangle with a corner given reports at once with no position and one with none waits for the pointer to come back (note 2); with the locator disabled, or no pointer told (`a_core_with_no_pointer_has_no_locator`), it answers `CSI 0 & w` (note 4), where xterm is silent. **A rectangle whose top is below its bottom is taken as given**: it contains nothing, so it reports at once, where DEC's area operations ignore such a rectangle and xterm swaps its corners (Jev's lean, AS_GIVEN, 0.89/0.85 on a second ask, IGNORE 0.82/0.69 on the first). xterm turns the locator off when a button is pressed or DECRQLP arrives while a rectangle is armed, "This might be a bug"; 070-13 says no such thing, and it stays (`decefr_reports_once_when_the_pointer_leaves_the_rectangle`, `decefr_takes_omitted_corners_from_the_pointer_and_reports_at_once_outside`, `decefr_reports_the_pointer_leaving_and_waits_for_it_to_come_back`, `decefr_with_the_locator_disabled_reports_it_unavailable`, `decefr_with_no_pointer_reports_at_once_or_waits_by_its_corners`, `a_request_or_a_press_inside_the_rectangle_leaves_the_locator_on`) |
| `CSI Ps ' \|` | DECRQLP request locator position | done — since 2026-10-20: `Ps` 0, 1 or none answers DECLRP event 1 with the pointer's buttons and position, omitted where undefined; with the locator disabled, or no pointer told, event 0, "to avoid timing out the application" (p. 8), DECLRP's "not enabled, or physically not present"; any other `Ps` is counted (`decrqlp_answers_unavailable_until_decelr_enables_the_locator`, `decrqlp_reports_the_pointer_and_its_buttons_in_cells_or_pixels`, `decrqlp_with_an_undefined_ps_is_ignored_and_counted`, `a_one_shot_decelr_is_disabled_by_its_first_report`) |
| `ESC 6` / `ESC 9` | DECBI / DECFI back and forward index | done — since 2026-10-18. The cursor steps one column, and at the left or right margin the text inside the margins slides over by one column instead, a blank column with no attributes coming in (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` pp. 5-37 to 5-39). It slides from any row, as the algorithm tests the column alone and the table marks both "not affected" by the top and bottom margins; xterm slides nothing from a row outside them. On a double-width line DECFI stops at the line's last column, as its algorithm cuts the column back to `end_of_line` (p. 5-38); until 2026-10-19 it stepped into the half past it (`decfi_on_a_double_width_line_stops_at_the_lines_end`). From a single-width row, the slide moves a double-width row to the right margin too, its hidden cells with it, as note 1 of each says, "Lines of text are shifted one column regardless of their line attributes", and as the scroll_left and scroll_right routines they call move every row to the right margin (§ 5.14.4 and § 5.14.5, pp. 5-212 and 5-213; `decfi_and_decbi_shift_a_sized_line_to_the_right_margin`) |

DECSCA, DECSED and DECSEL land together on purpose: the two erases without the
protection are silent aliases for ED and EL, and an application that protected a
region would watch it vanish.

Protection is **not** an `Attrs` bit. `[[source: vt510-decsca]]`: "DECSCA does
not effect visual character attributes set by the select graphic rendition (SGR)
function" -- so it sits in its own field of `StyleWord`, where a renderer
reading attributes cannot mistake it for one. It *is* part of the interning key,
so protected and unprotected text are distinct renditions and the flag survives
in the template table without widening the 8-byte `PackedCell`.

Two rules follow, and both are pinned by tests. A plain ED/EL erases protected
characters -- only the private forms are selective. And the blank an erase
leaves behind does not inherit the protection, or the first DECSEL under
`DECSCA 1` would protect everything it just cleared. A selective erase leaves
a space in the character's own rendition and protection, which is erasable
because only erasable characters become one.

---

## Tier 3 — xterm extensions in universal use

In scope because applications assume them, not because DEC specified them.

| seq | name | status |
|---|---|---|
| `OSC 0/1/2` | window/icon title | done — stored in the core (`Term::title`, `Term::icon_name`), and **set on the window** by both drivers since 2026-09-24, where until then nothing read it. **Not drawn on macOS**: the window crate's titled glass window hides its title text, so a program's title reaches the Window menu, Mission Control and accessibility, not the titlebar. On Wayland it reaches the compositor, whose titlebar shows it only under server-side decorations, which baud does not negotiate. The app reads the bytes in the encoding the core kept with them (`Term::title_encoding`, the XTSMTITLE row) and turns controls into spaces (`baud-app/src/title.rs`). **When a program sets no title, the window's title is the folder and the program running in it**, `~/src/baud — vim`, since 2026-10-21, a deep folder as its last three after an ellipsis, as kitty's and ghostty's shell integrations spell a prompt's title: the folder the shell's `OSC 7` names when it names this machine (the `OSC 7` row), else the foreground program's own, and the program by its argv[0], read from the pty's foreground process group (`TIOCGPGRP` on the master; macOS's `proc_pidinfo` and `KERN_PROCARGS2`, Linux's `/proc`; `baud-app/src/fg.rs`), read at the group's id as a pid, the group leader, as wezterm and alacritty read it; a pipeline whose leader has exited reads nothing, and the title keeps the half it knows (note AK 2.2, UNVERIFIED there). A program's title wins outright, as "Change Window Title to Pt" says, so nothing is added to one (Jev's lean, T2, 0.61/0.72, note AK question 2); the derived title is read once a frame when a read, a key or, on macOS, a process fork, exec or exit owes it, never on a timer (`many_owed_checks_are_one_and_an_unowed_one_is_none`, `the_foreground_reads_name_the_program_and_its_folder`). **The icon name is the minimized window's title on macOS** since 2026-10-21 (`OSC 1`, the icon half of `OSC 0`, and `OSC 3`'s icon properties): AppKit's `miniwindowTitle`, set through the crate's `ns_window` after every title set, which "automatically changes the minimized window's title as well" (`[[source: apple-nswindow-miniwindowtitle]]`); with none the minimized window shows the title (Jev, I1, 0.92/0.92, note AK question 8; `the_icon_name_is_the_minimized_title_after_every_title_set`). Wayland's `xdg_toplevel` has no icon name (`[[source: wp-xdg-shell]]`), so the Linux app keeps it stored |
| `OSC 4` | set colour palette | done — **every `c ; spec` pair of a sequence is applied, in order**, since 2026-10-18: "Any number of c/spec pairs may be given", and a `?` in any pair is answered in its own reply where it stands, with the query's terminator (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`). Replies share the reply buffer, 10,412 bytes since 2026-10-21 (the longest XTGETTCAP answer; 9,139 from 2026-10-19, the longest DECTABSR; 256 before), so one sequence asking for more than about 371 palette entries has the rest dropped and counted in `reply_overflows`; the cap is open (note AG, section 1; held for the owner's A/B). As xterm's `ChangeAnsiColorRequest` walks them (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 2993-3023), a bad index, an index with no spec or a spec no reader takes ends the walk and keeps the pairs before it, counted once; an index of 256 to 260 names one of xterm's special colours, set or asked for as `OSC 5` does (the `OSC 5` row), and the walk goes on (`osc_4_applies_every_pair`, `a_query_in_any_pair_is_answered_in_turn`, `a_bad_pair_stops_the_walk_and_is_counted`, `a_special_colour_by_osc_4_is_set_and_the_walk_goes_on`); it was counted before 2026-10-19. Only the first pair was taken before. Including the `?` query, **answered for every entry** since 2026-09-29, as xterm's `ReportAnsiColorRequest` does: the application's override, else the embedder's table (`Term::set_default_palette`, pushed with the default colours by both apps), else nothing on a core no embedder told (`baud-core/tests/palette_query.rs`). It answered only overridden entries before. **Both resets clear every override** since 2026-09-29, as xterm's `ReallyReset` calls `ResetAnsiColorRequest` in its common path, and leave `OSC 10`/`11`/`12` as it does (`baud-core/tests/palette_reset.rs`, `a_soft_reset_returns_an_overridden_entry_to_the_theme`); they kept every override before, so a program reset after recolouring the sixteen left its colours on everything drawn after it. **A colour name is resolved as the X server resolves it**, since 2026-10-18: XParseColor sends a name to the server (`[[source: libx11-parsecol-b8d5a70]]` 114-139), which ignores case and nothing else (`[[source: xserver-oscolor-98aed9d]]` 852-884), over X.Org's rgb.txt (`[[source: xorg-rgb-0d2caec]]`, 782 names), built into a table at build time and found by two hashes and one comparison; the sixteen names of xterm's FAQ resource file and its `lime green` set what they name (`xterms_own_colour_names_resolve`, `every_name_in_rgb_txt_resolves_in_any_case`). A name the server lacks is left alone and **counted** as unsupported, as every name was until then (`a_name_the_server_lacks_is_counted`). So are the three sample names a stock libX11 installs in its client-side `Xcms.txt` -- `cms red`, `cms green`, `cms blue`, "provided just as an example" (`[[source: libx11-xcms-txt-b8d5a70]]`, installed by every build, `[[source: libx11-xcms-makefile-b8d5a70]]` 88-89) -- which XParseColor tries before the server, case and spaces ignored (`[[source: xparsecolor-obs-xcms-names-out]]`, and with no database `[[source: xparsecolor-obs-xcms-names-nodb]]`): the Xlib manual reads "zero or more client-side databases", their number and content "implementation-dependent" (`[[source: xlib-ch06-b8d5a70]]` 533-537), and baud keeps none (Jev's lean, 0.50/0.50, 0.58/0.56 after research, under the bar). **`rgbi:` and the six device-independent forms are colours** since 2026-10-18 (`[[source: xlib-ch06-b8d5a70]]` 637-695): `CIEXYZ:`, `CIEuvY:`, `CIExyY:`, `CIELab:`, `CIELuv:` and `TekHVC:` converted to the sRGB surface baud draws on, CIELAB and CIELUV as CIE 015:2018 defines them (`[[source: cie-015-2018]]`), TekHVC, the conversions and the gamut clip as libX11's Xcms computes them (`an_in_gamut_spec_is_the_texts_colour`, `an_out_of_gamut_spec_keeps_its_hue_and_value`, `clip_matches_libx11_under_its_own_profile`). libX11 converts through a 1990 CRT profile when the server publishes none and reads CIELAB and CIELUV at a hundredth of CIE's scale; both are its departures, decided at 0.92 or more twice. A value off the manual's grammar or range is refused and counted (`a_spec_off_the_grammar_or_the_range_is_refused`). **A short `#` form is its channels' high bits, and a prefix is read in any case**, since 2026-10-18, as the Xlib manual's "Color Strings" defines them (`[[source: xlib-ch06-b8d5a70]]`): `#fff` draws `#f0f0f0`, `#3a7` is `#3000a0007000`, and `RGB:FF/00/00` is red. Every form is read to XParseColor's sixteen bits and drawn as its high byte, the pixel a 24-bit X server gives it; 607 specs run through libX11 on Xvfb are held line by line (`every_spec_reads_as_the_oracle_drew_it`, `[[source: xparsecolor-obs-rgb-out]]`). `rgb:1/2/3/4`, which libX11's reader takes, is refused, as the manual's grammar has three fields. **Under `?5` an entry set, queried or reset is the entry drawn wherever a cell names it**, since 2026-10-14, when every cell's colours exchange and no entry moves (decided 2026-10-13; the `?5` row; `a_palette_override_is_drawn_where_its_entry_is_named`); under xterm's palette swap, drawn until then, an override of entry 7 was drawn where a cell named 0 |
| `OSC 10` / `OSC 11` | default foreground / background | done — **under `?5` each names the colour shown in its role**, `OSC 11` the stored foreground, and the reply keeps the code asked (the `?5` row, since 2026-10-11). Set from any spec `OSC 4` takes, read by the renderer before its theme (`Term::dynamic_color`), and the `?` query answered in the setter's shape, **four hex digits a channel** (`rgb:efef/efef/efef`), which is xterm's reply form: `rgb:` accepts one to four digits in what is *sent*, but readers parse the reply at fixed offsets, and yazi read baud's two-digit `rgb:ef/ef/ef` as a failure it reports as a dark background whatever the theme, until 2026-09-22 (`yazi_reads_a_light_background_as_light_and_a_dark_one_as_dark`). The `OSC 4` reply takes the same width. **A query on a fresh terminal answers the embedder's theme**, which both apps push in through `Term::set_default_colors` -- the query every theme-aware program sends to learn light from dark; a core that was never told says nothing, the `OSC 4` refusal for the same reason. **Each spec after the first sets the next colour**, since 2026-10-18: "Each successive parameter changes the next color in the list. The value of Ps tells the starting point in the list" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), so `OSC 10 ; fg ; bg ; cursor` sets all three and `OSC 11 ; bg ; cursor` starts at the background; an empty field passes over its colour, each `?` is answered in its own reply, in turn (within the reply buffer's cap, as the `OSC 4` row says), and a number baud keeps no colour for -- the pointer's 13 and 14, Tektronix's 15, 16 and 18, which baud does not draw -- and the specs past 19 are counted while the walk goes on, as xterm's `ChangeColorsRequest` walks them (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3680-3703; `each_spec_after_the_first_sets_the_next_colour`, `an_empty_field_leaves_its_colour`, `each_query_in_the_list_is_answered`, `a_colour_baud_does_not_keep_is_counted_and_the_walk_goes_on`). Only the first spec was taken before. `osc_10_and_11_set_the_dynamic_colours`, `osc_11_query_answers_the_embedders_default`, `dynamic_colours_are_chunk_invariant`, and the pixels in `baud-render/tests/dynamic.rs` |
| `OSC 110` / `OSC 111` | reset default foreground / background | done — clears the application's override and leaves the theme's default, which is what reset means; under `?5` of the role shown, as xterm's `ResetColorsRequest` (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3718-3750; `osc_110_and_111_under_reverse_reset_the_colour_shown`, since 2026-10-11). `an_override_wins_the_query_and_reset_restores_the_default` |
| `OSC 12` / `OSC 112` | cursor colour | done — **stored and read** now that a renderer draws a cursor colour, which is the day the old counted-not-stored deferral named. The block cursor fills with it and the bar or underline cursor takes it, on all three paths -- `Renderer::convert_row` for the software and instance arms, the cell shader for the cell arm, which `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws` gates pixel-for-pixel. A spec is read as `OSC 4` reads it -- a colour name, the `#` and `rgb:` forms, `rgbi:` and the CIE and TekHVC forms -- since 2026-10-18 (`xterms_own_colour_names_resolve`), and the `Cs` the three `xterm` profiles carry replays with `red` in the conformance suite. The `?` query answers in the code-12 shape and `OSC 112` resets it. **A configured cursor colour** -- `[palette] cursor`, or a theme file's -- is drawn while no program has set one, answers the query and is what `OSC 112` returns to, since 2026-10-22: xterm reports the colour in use, at start its `cursorColor` resource, and its `ResetColorsRequest` allocates that resource again (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3568-3595, 3718-3750); with none configured the query answers nothing and the reset returns to none, as before. A configured colour equal to the text moves on a `?5` change and with a new `OSC 10` colour as a program's does (`a_configured_cursor_colour_is_drawn_answered_and_what_112_restores`, `a_configured_cursor_colour_equal_to_the_text_moves_as_a_programs_does`). The text under a block keeps a configured cursor text colour under a program's `OSC 12`, as kitty, alacritty, ghostty and foot keep theirs (Jev, 0.94 and 0.96). `osc_12_sets_and_reads_the_cursor_colour`. **A colour equal to the text moves to the background on a `?5` change** (the `?5` row, since 2026-10-11). **A cursor colour equal to the text follows a new `OSC 10` colour**, since 2026-10-19, when the same sequence sets no cursor colour, as xterm's `ChangeColors` moves it (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]` util.c 3062-3070), so a program that painted the cursor in its text colour keeps it visible; before, an `OSC 10` left it where it was (`a_cursor_colour_equal_to_the_text_follows_osc_10`). `OSC 110`, the reset of the text colour, does not move it: xterm's `ResetColorsRequest` goes through `ChangeColors` (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3743-3747) and does, and only `OSC 10` was built |
| `OSC 17` / `OSC 19` | highlight background / foreground: the selection's colours | done — since 2026-10-18. **A selection takes the colours a program sets**: "Ps = 1 7 -> Change highlight background color to Pt", "Ps = 1 9 -> Change highlight foreground color to Pt" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), each drawn whenever it is set, on all three paths (`Hilite`, `Resolved::drawn`, the cell shader's `colors()`): the page alone draws each selected cell's own text on it, the text alone draws on the reversed page, both draw the text on the page, and neither is reverse video, the default. That is kitty's practice (`[[source: kitty-conf]]`) and the `highlightColorMode` true rows of xterm's manual; xterm decides that mode once at startup and, configured with nothing, never draws a runtime `OSC 19` -- Jev leaned to drawing each when set, 0.58 and 0.40, 0.58 and 0.53 after research, under the bar and taken as the lean. No per-cell exception: xterm draws a cell whose own colour equals the highlight reversed, code its manual and ctlseqs do not state (Jev's lean, 0.84/0.72, 0.78/0.80). Under `?5` each names the colour sent, the selection being drawn after DEC's exchange (Jev's lean, 0.72/0.64, 0.84/0.72), where xterm's handlers trade them under its own `reverseVideo`. A glyph in a highlight colour takes that colour's contrast curve row. The `?` query answers in `OSC 10`'s shape with the query's terminator: the colour set, else the embedder's configured colour (the selection colours `Term::set_theme` is given, since 2026-10-21), else the colour a reverse-video selection is drawn in: the text shown for `17`, the page shown for `19`, whether or not the other is set -- as a default xterm answers (Jev, 0.90 both orders), silent on a core never told its colours. `RIS` keeps the application's colours and the configured ones, as xterm's `ReallyReset` touches no dynamic colour (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`); no DEC mode covers them, so DECRQM has none to report. A change repaints the frame. `osc_17_and_19_set_the_selection_colours_and_117_and_119_reset_them`, `a_highlight_query_answers_the_colour_the_selection_is_drawn_in`, `a_configured_selection_colour_answers_and_is_what_117_and_119_restore`, `ris_and_a_theme_flip_keep_the_configured_selection`, `osc_17_and_19_under_reverse_name_the_colour_sent`, `a_selection_is_drawn_in_the_highlight_colours_each_when_set`, `an_incremental_frame_after_osc_17_equals_a_full_repaint`, the pair scene of `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws`. Before, both were counted as unsupported. **A configured colour is drawn**, since 2026-10-21, on all three paths and in both apps: the `[palette]` `selection` and `selection_text` keys, else a theme file's selection colours, else reverse video, the default (the owner's V13 call; Jev 1.00 both orders with renders beside Terminal.app's and iTerm2's); a program's colour wins over each while set; the find field, its current match and the `?1001` region draw in them too (Jev, 0.96/0.98). `a_theme_selection_colour_is_drawn_until_a_program_sets_its_own`, `the_configs_selection_wins_over_the_files_and_none_takes_it_away`, `osc_17_and_19_answer_the_themes_selection`, the theme scene of `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws`. **In a macOS window that is not key, reverse video is AppKit's unemphasized pair**, since 2026-10-22: `unemphasizedSelectedTextBackgroundColor` and `unemphasizedSelectedTextColor`, "e.g. when the associated control/view/window is not key" (`[[source: sdk27-nscolor-h]]` 232-235), resolved under the window's appearance; the find field's marks and the `?1001` region, drawn in the selection's colours, take it too. A colour of its own, the program's or the configured one, is drawn as it is (Jev's lean, 0.70/0.49 and 0.71/0.58). The core is not told the pair, so while the window is not key the `?` query answers the colours a key window draws. `a_reverse_video_selection_takes_the_unemphasized_pair_while_not_key`, `a_selection_colour_of_its_own_is_unchanged_while_not_key`, `the_unemphasized_pair_is_appkits_under_each_appearance` |
| `OSC 117` / `OSC 119` | reset highlight background / foreground | done — since 2026-10-18, and since 2026-10-21 **back to the embedder's configured colour**, "their default (resource) values" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), as xterm's `ResetColorsRequest` allocates the resource again (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3718-3750); reverse video where none is configured. `osc_17_and_19_set_the_selection_colours_and_117_and_119_reset_them`, `a_configured_selection_colour_answers_and_is_what_117_and_119_restore` |
| `OSC 8` | hyperlinks | done — bounded link table that **refuses a colliding link rather than evicting the occupant**: a cell never resolves to an address other than the one written under its index, a clipped address is refused too, and both are counted in `links_refused` / `links_clipped`. **Activated in `baud-app` by Cmd-click**, and on Linux since 2026-10-21 by Ctrl-click, which opens with `xdg-open` and reveals through the file manager's `ShowItems` (`a_ctrl_click_on_a_link_opens_it`): `http`, `https` and `mailto` are opened, a `file` link on this host is revealed, in Finder on macOS and by the file manager's `ShowItems` on Linux (the opener would *launch* an application bundle), everything else is refused and counted; Cmd-Shift-click is a selection, a repeat click is swallowed whole, and a tracking child never sees the gesture. `a_cell_never_resolves_to_a_uri_other_than_the_one_written_under_its_index`, `a_link_whose_uri_may_have_been_cut_is_refused_and_counted`, `the_four_schemes_are_admitted_and_nothing_else_is`, `a_cmd_click_on_a_link_arms_on_the_down_and_opens_on_the_up`; see security note. **Since 2026-10-22 holding Cmd (Ctrl on Linux) over a link shows where it goes**: every cell naming its slot underlined, as the spec underlines cells sharing a URI and a nonempty `id` (`[[source: osc8-spec]]`), and its address in a bar at the bottom, a byte outside printable ASCII shown as `\xNN`, shortened to keep the end of its host, with the reason in words when a click will not open it (`the_preview_shows_every_byte_a_target_holds`, `every_cell_naming_the_link_is_underlined`, `ctrl_held_over_a_link_previews_it`, `the_link_preview_is_drawn_moved_and_cleared_without_a_trace`); the bar is drawn over a program's images, as kitty draws its hyperlink target after them (`[[source: kitty-hyperlink-target]]`; `baud_s_own_overlays_are_drawn_over_an_image`) |
| `OSC 52` | clipboard | done — **writes honoured, reads deliberately refused** (silence, counted in `clipboard_reads_refused`; see security note). Since 2026-10-10, the owner's V11: the body after `52;` leaves the 512-byte OSC buffer for two caller-provided areas (`Owned::with_clipboard`), is decoded as it arrives by `baud-domain`'s RFC 4648 decoder, and is published at the terminator (`Term::clipboard`, `Term::clipboard_writes`), whatever the read boundaries (`baud-core/tests/clipboard.rs`, the `osc52-*` property atoms). `Pc`'s twelve selections are kept as a set and an empty `Pc` is `s 0`; a `Pd` that is neither base64 nor `?` **clears** the selection, ctlseqs' rule. A copy is refused whole and counted, never cut, when the storage has no area, `Pc` names nothing ctlseqs knows, or it outgrows the area (`Term::clipboard_refusals`). The decoder accepts an unpadded final quantum and non-zero pad bits, both allowed by RFC 4648 3.2 and 3.5 and both a choice. macOS puts a write naming `c`, `p` or `s` on the general pasteboard when it is UTF-8, under `[clipboard] osc52` (on by default); `q` and the cut buffers have no counterpart and are refused (`baud-app/src/clip.rs`). Since 2026-10-20 Linux provisions the area and puts a write on each selection it names, through `wl_data_device` (`[[source: wayland]]`) and the primary-selection device (`[[source: wp-primary-selection-unstable-v1]]`): `c` the clipboard, `p` the primary, and `s` -- an empty `Pc` too -- xterm's SELECT, the primary while `?1041` is reset (Jev, XTERM, 0.96/0.94; foot, alacritty and kitty send an empty `Pc` to the clipboard, `[[source: foot-2705e36-osc]]`, `[[source: alacritty-vte-ansi]]`, `[[source: kitty-6d5d0c4-clipboard-py]]`, so a tmux yank, whose `Pc` is empty (`[[source: tmux-3-5a-window-copy]]`), lands on the primary here as in xterm). A write names the serial of the last key or click, as foot names its last keyboard serial, `set_selection` asking for "the event that triggered this request" and a program's write having none; a copy the window could not make is said once (`the_linux_driver_polls_after_every_read`). Since 2026-10-21 a write that comes while the window lacks the keyboard is sent the same way, counted and said once, and a cancel of it before the keyboard returns is counted and said: neither protocol's `set_selection` asks for the keyboard, GNOME's compositor refuses a client without it and cancels the source (`[[source: mutter-47-data-device]]`), and wlroots takes it unless a later copy came first (`[[source: wlroots-0-18-2-data-device]]`); Jev's lean, LAND, 0.64/0.32, over holding it until the keyboard returns, where foot and alacritty drop it (`[[source: foot-2705e36-osc]]`, `[[source: smithay-clipboard-0-7-2-state]]`) and kitty sends it (`[[source: kitty-0-43-1-wl-window]]`) (`a_copy_without_the_keyboard_is_sent_and_its_cancel_said`, `a_copy_without_the_keyboard_and_its_cancel_are_counted`) |
| `OSC 133` | semantic prompt marks | done — every letter of `[[source: semantic-prompts]]` and every `A` option shells send; `Z`, which the text names twice (under `err=` and with the omitted newline) and never defines, is counted in `semantic_marks` and does nothing. **Since 2026-10-19**: `L` starts a fresh line, and `A` and `N` do first, so a prompt after output with no newline begins on a line of its own, as in Ghostty (`a_fresh_line_starts_a_line_only_where_none_is_started`); at a redraw the erase stands for it, the cursor going to the start of the block's emptied first row. `A`/`N` start a command and `P` (`k=i`) a prompt, `k=s`/`k=c`/`k=r` later lines, `B` and `I` the input, `C` the output, `D` the end; an `I` input ends with its line, read at the next mark or resize (`an_i_input_ends_with_its_line`). Options reset at each new command, as kitty's `screen.c` does: `redraw=0` skips the redraw erase and lets a resize re-cut the prompt as output, as kitty does (`a_redraw_mark_under_redraw_0_erases_nothing`, `a_prompt_under_redraw_0_is_recut_like_output`); `redraw=last` erases as `1`, since an initial mark reaches a block under it only where the shell redraws a line that begins with one (Jev split: LAST_LINE at confidence 0.31, AS_ONE at 0.41 with the order reversed; AS_ONE taken); `cl=` (any value read as `line`, which the text permits), `move-keys=`, kitty's `click_events=` and `special_key=` are what a click at the prompt sends (`a_click_sends_what_the_a_asked`; the app's input-coverage row), and a prompt re-cut under `redraw=0` keeps its block start and its input on their cells (`a_recut_prompt_keeps_its_input_where_it_began`). `D`'s status is kept on the prompt its command began with -- `err=` first, a `D` with no `C` cancelled as iTerm2 reads it (`d_keeps_its_status_on_the_prompt_it_ends`), the command found by `aid` through nested ones (`a_nested_command_takes_its_status_by_aid`) -- and read through `Term::prompts`. **Since 2026-10-22** it is drawn when `[screen] exit_status` is on: a disc, a cross or a ring in a strip of its own beside the prompt's row, for success, failure and a cancelled command, nothing for a command not reported ended, read each frame from the prompts on the window shown (`Term::prompts_from`), so a mark keeps to its line through scrolling, a reflow and the history, and none on the alternate screen (`a_mark_shows_each_status_the_shell_sent`, `a_mark_follows_its_line_through_the_history_and_a_reflow`, `the_alternate_screen_has_no_marks`, `the_marks_differ_without_their_colours`). The prompts are kept by line, follow a re-cut, and are forgotten on the history lines `CSI 3 J` erases, all of them at `RIS`, and when a prompt is drawn above them, as after a clear, the primary's alone (`a_clear_takes_the_prompts_it_erased`, `a_jump_lands_on_its_prompt_after_a_reflow`, `erasing_the_history_forgets_its_prompts`, `a_full_log_forgets_its_oldest_prompts`); `Term::jump_to_prompt` moves the window to one (`a_jump_goes_to_the_previous_and_the_next_prompt`). kitty's `C;cmdline=` and `cmdline_url=` are unread: nothing in baud shows a command line. The zone the marks open is acted on by `Term::resize`, which does not re-cut the rows a shell will redraw, and counted in `semantic_marks`. **Each screen has its own**: the zone and the block start are swapped by `?1049` as the kitty stacks are, the alternate screen's start fresh on each entry, and the primary's zone alone decides whether the primary re-cuts (`a_mark_on_the_alternate_screen_does_not_redraw_the_primarys_block`, `a_block_opened_on_the_alternate_screen_is_not_redrawn_on_the_primary`, `a_mark_on_the_alternate_screen_does_not_stop_the_primary_re_cutting`). Since 2026-10-14 the terminal keeps the window over the history (note V, V2): a redraw mark whose block retreats over the history sends a window it reaches to the live screen at the mark's byte (`a_take_back_that_reaches_the_window_sends_it_live_at_that_byte`) |
| `OSC 9 ; Pt` | iTerm2's notification | done — since 2026-10-21, **a program can post a notification**: "OSC 9 ; [Message content goes here] ST" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), the whole body the message, shown as a desktop notification while the window is not focused, since no text says when (Jev, 0.98 and 0.97): through the notification center on macOS, from the app's bundle and once you allow it, and the session bus's notification server on Linux; at most four at once and then one a second (`the_bound_lets_four_through_then_one_a_second`), and none with `[screen] notifications = "off"`. A body in one of ConEmu's forms is ConEmu's (its own rows), so `9 ; 9 ; dir` at a prompt posts nothing (Jev's lean, 0.83 and 0.91; ghostty reads the same, kitty posts it). `osc_9_posts_its_message`, `conemus_other_forms_post_nothing` |
| `OSC 9 ; 4 ; st ; pr` | ConEmu's progress | done — since 2026-10-21, **a program can show how far along it is**: "0: remove progress ... 1: set progress value to pr (number, 0-100) ... 2: set error state ... pr is optional ... 3: set indeterminate state ... 4: set paused state, pr is optional" (`[[source: conemu-ansi-escape-codes]]`), and iTerm2's bare `OSC 9 ; 4` that clears it; a pause with no `pr` keeps the percentage shown (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]` 3.7), a percentage past 100 is 100, as Windows Terminal clamps it (`[[source: wt-adapt-dispatch]]`). Shown as a bar on the Dock icon on macOS, red for an error and yellow for a pause, and sent as `com.canonical.Unity.LauncherEntry`'s progress on Linux (`[[source: libunity-launcher-entry]]`), which a dock shows for an installed desktop file -- none ships yet -- whatever the notification setting. A state no text defines, or a `pr` that is no number, changes nothing and is counted (Jev, 0.98 and 0.96). No timeout clears it (Jev, 0.98 and 0.98; kitty's 60 s is its practice); `RIS` hides it. `progress_follows_every_state`, `a_pause_keeps_the_percentage_and_ris_hides_the_bar` |
| `OSC 777 ; notify ; title ; body` | Terminology's and VTE's notification | done — since 2026-10-21, **a program can post a notification with a title and a body**: "OSC 777 ; notify ; SUMMARY ; BODY" and "OSC 777 ; notify ; SUMMARY" (`[[source: vte-notify-patch]]`), the body everything after the title's `;` -- VTE's own `vte.sh` sends a command line there, which carries `;`s -- and a title alone posted (Jev's lean, 0.85 and 0.67; VTE's tokeniser stops at the next `;`, ghostty refuses a title alone). Shown as `OSC 9`'s is. `osc_777_notify_takes_a_title_and_the_rest` |
| `OSC 99 ; metadata ; payload` | kitty's desktop notifications | **partial** — since 2026-10-21, **a program can post a notification with a title, a body, buttons and an icon, update it, close it and ask what baud shows** (`[[source: kitty-desktop-notifications]]`). **Two gaps are open.** On Linux, icon data is shown only as a PNG of at most 1024 pixels a side: JPEG and GIF, the text's other two formats, and a larger PNG are not shown, counted and logged once, until a decoder exists (Jev, 0.95 and 0.97; `an_image_decodes_and_markup_is_escaped`). On macOS a program that asked is told `p=close ; untracked` when the post is made, before permission is known; if permission is then refused, it was told of a notification that never showed. Built: chunks (`i`, `d=0`) concatenated into a title, a body and buttons, each chunk up to 4096 bytes, routed past `OSC_MAX` as `OSC 52` is (`a_chunk_carries_four_kilobytes`); base64 (`e=1`) chunked before or after encoding (`base64_spans_chunks_either_way`); an update by id and none without (`the_same_id_updates_and_no_id_never_does`), `p=close`, `p=alive`, buttons, urgency, occasion (`o`, default `always`, so shown while focused unless it says otherwise), expiry, sound, the application name and type, icon names -- mapped to the freedesktop names on Linux -- and icon data, cached under `g` sixteen at a time and shown as `image-data` on Linux (PNG) and as an attachment on macOS (PNG, JPEG, GIF) (`icons_are_cached_under_their_key`). A click focuses the window on macOS and reports where `a=report` asks, on both; since 2026-10-21 a click on the body of one that asked `focus` on Linux activates the window with the token the server's `ActivationToken` signal sent for it, "an activation token that can be used to activate a toplevel" (`[[source: freedesktop-notification-spec]]`), through `xdg-activation-v1`'s `activate`, where the compositor offers it and decides what it does (`[[source: xdg-activation-v1]]`); where the server sends no token nothing is activated, and such a notification carries the `default` action whenever it asks `focus` or `report` (`an_activation_token_comes_with_its_notifications_click`, `the_default_action_is_offered_for_focus_or_report`, `the_linux_driver_activates_on_a_click_that_asked_focus`). Neither the token nor the compositor's answer can be known when a program asks, so Linux still claims `report` alone (Jev's lean, 0.88/0.76 then 0.49/0.31), and it and the buttons only where the server lists `actions` (`support_claims_what_the_platform_delivers`). On macOS, which does not say when one closes, `p=alive` names what Notification Center still holds, read every two seconds while any is shown (`the_macos_driver_keeps_alive_true`); on Linux the server's `NotificationClosed` takes it out. The query goes unanswered on Linux until the session bus's notification server answers, and with none, and on macOS outside the app's bundle or once you refuse permission. The query `p=?` is answered with what the embedder says it delivers (`Term::set_notice_support`) and nothing else, in order with the read's other replies, and not at all while it has said nothing or notifications are off (`the_query_answers_what_the_embedder_delivers`), `buttons` among its payloads only where they are shown (`the_query_claims_buttons_only_where_they_are_shown`); a click reports only under `a=report` (`a_click_reports_only_when_asked`), a close under `c=1`, `untracked` where the platform cannot tell (`closes_are_reported_tracked_or_untracked`). Every id is sanitised to `[a-zA-Z0-9_\-+.]` before it is echoed, as the text says it must be (`ids_are_sanitised_before_they_are_echoed`). Refused and counted (`Term::notice_refusals`): no area, no support, malformed metadata -- a key not one letter, or a value outside the text's character set with the `=` its base64 values pad with (Jev's lean, 0.30 and 0.48) and the `?` of its own `p=?` -- a chunk or an id too long, `e=1` that is not base64, a notification with nothing in it, more than eight in one read |
| `OSC 21 ; key=value ; ...` | kitty's colour control | **partial** — since 2026-10-22, **a program written for kitty can read and set the colours in one sequence**: the default text and background, the selection's two, the cursor's, the text under a block cursor and the 256 palette entries, "<OSC> 21 ; key=value ; key=value ; ... <ST>" (`[[source: kitty-6d5d0c4-color-stack]]`), each record read as kitty's color_control reads it (`[[source: kitty-window-py]]` 571-641). **One gap is open: the visual bell takes no colour.** baud's flash is the other video, the "Automatic color selection based on current screen colors" kitty's table names, so visual_bell=? answers empty and a colour is refused and counted (Jev's lean, V_EMPTY_COUNT, 0.38/0.76 and 0.82/0.95 after research; `the_visual_bell_has_no_colour`). The transparent background colours are their own row, in scope and unbuilt. Built: foreground and background are the colours `OSC 10`/`11` set, selection_background and selection_foreground `OSC 17`/`19`'s, cursor `OSC 12`'s, a number `OSC 4`'s entry, and cursor_text the text under a block cursor, drawn on all three paths over the theme's (`the_text_under_the_cursor_is_set_asked_and_reset`). A query answers the program's colour, else the configured one, else empty, "the value must be empty" for a colour with no defined value, so a selection drawn in reverse video answers selection_background= where `OSC 17 ; ?` answers the text colour (`a_selection_made_automatic_answers_empty`, `a_terminal_told_nothing_answers_empty`), and so does a selection a theme draws in each cell's own text or page, which has no one colour, while `OSC 17`/`19`, which have no empty answer, answer the default page or text in its place (Jev's lean, S_EMPTY, the first ask split, then 0.60/0.86; `a_selection_in_each_cells_colours_answers_empty`, `a_selection_in_each_cells_colours_answers_osc_21_empty`). A query of a key baud does not know answers `?` and is not counted, as DECRQM's "not recognized" is not; a set or a reset of one is counted. **The reply is kitty's to the byte**: one `OSC 21` for a sequence's queries and none for a sequence that asks nothing, each key as sent, once, where first asked, with what the last ask found, as kitty's dict keeps them, a colour as `rgb:rr/gg/bb`, and always ended by `ESC \`, as kitty's write_escape_code_to_child ends every OSC (`[[source: kitty-6d5d0c4-screen]]` 1728-1741; `a_query_answers_in_kittys_shape`, `a_key_asked_twice_is_answered_once_with_the_last_value`, `a_key_is_echoed_as_sent`, `the_reply_ends_in_st_whatever_ended_the_request`). An empty value is the terminal's automatic colour, reverse video for a selection and the cell's colours for the cursor and the text under it, over a configured colour (`an_automatic_colour_wins_over_the_configured_one`); the default colours take none, "Not applicable", counted. **A palette entry takes no empty value either**, since 2026-10-22: "Setting the ANSI color table colors to dynamic is not allowed" (`[[source: kitty-6d5d0c4-color-stack]]` 82), so the record is refused and counted, the entry keeps its colour, a later query in the same sequence answers that colour, and the sequence's other records still apply (`a_palette_entry_takes_no_dynamic_value`; decided by Jev, REFUSE, 0.96/1.00 after research). Until then baud followed kitty's code, which sends an entry of the sixteen back to its configured colour and gives one past them xterm's fixed colour (`[[source: kitty-window-py]]` 621-629, and kitty's own test, `[[source: kitty-6d5d0c4-tests-screen]]` 1978-1981), a departure not followed; ghostty reads every empty value as a reset (`[[source: ghostty-f54340a-osc-kitty-color]]`), practice not followed. A bare key resets (`a_palette_entry_is_set_and_reset`). **A value is read by kitty's text**: `rgb:` scaled, `#` as the high bits, `rgbi:` clipped to 0 to 1 and then read as baud's `OSC 4` reads it, the intensities converted to device values through the sRGB profile, so rgbi:0.5 draws `bc`, where ghostty scales linearly (0.5 to `7f`, `[[source: ghostty-f54340a-color]]` 701-708), kitty's colour names, an `@` alpha checked and dropped; kitty's parser reads `rgb:` by its first two digits, refuses every `rgbi:` and takes `oklch(`, `lab(` and a comment the text does not name, its departures (Jev's lean, G_DOC, 0.50/0.74 and 0.44/0.74; `a_value_is_read_by_kittys_text`). **Under `?5` foreground and background name the colour shown in that role**, set, asked and reset, as `OSC 10`/`11` do by the owner's V17, "make OSC 4, 10 and 11 set, query and reset the colour you see", so `background=?` and `OSC 11 ; ?` never disagree; kitty's code reads its stored profile, and Jev's lean was that (R_STORED, 0.40/0.34 after research, the first ask split, under the bar), which the owner's call outranks (`under_reverse_video_the_colours_shown_answer`). A number names the stored entry under `?5`, as `OSC 4` does, since no entry moves under the exchange. **A sequence of any length is honoured**: routed at `21;` as `OSC 52` is and read a record at a time, so the 256 entries are set and asked in one (Jev's lean, L_STREAM, 0.82/0.88 and 0.72/0.80; `every_entry_is_set_and_asked_in_one_sequence`); a record longer than `OSC_MAX` is refused and counted (`a_record_past_osc_max_is_refused_and_counted`). **Nothing changes until the terminator**, so a sequence `CAN` or an `ESC` cancels changes nothing (`a_cancelled_sequence_changes_nothing`). Only printable ASCII is kept, as kitty strips the rest before it splits (`a_byte_past_printable_ascii_is_dropped`). XTPUSHCOLORS keeps the text under the cursor with the dynamic colours (`the_colour_stack_keeps_the_text_under_the_cursor`). The number is kitty's, not DECSWT's (the DECSWT row; Jev's lean, N_KITTY, 0.46/0.55 and 0.27/0.34) |
| `OSC 1337 ; RequestAttention=Pv` | iTerm2's attention request | **partial** — since 2026-10-21, **a program can ask for the user's attention**: "yes to request attention by bouncing the dock icon indefinitely, once to bounce it a single time, or no to cancel a previous request" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), the Dock icon bounced until baud is active, once, or the request cancelled on macOS, and the launcher's `urgent` set on Linux (Jev, 0.97 and 0.92). **The gap is `fireworks`**, "fireworks explode at the cursor's location": not built, and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`). iTerm2's source reads any other value by `boolValue` and has a `flash` the page does not name (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-vt100terminal]]`); baud follows the page and counts them. `request_attention_reaches_the_embedder` |
| `OSC 1337 ; CursorShape=N` | iTerm2: the cursor's shape | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program can make the cursor a block, a bar or an underline and leave its blink alone**: "where [N]=0, 1, or 2. 0: Block 1: Vertical bar 2: Underline" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), the blink kept as iTerm2's `terminalSetCursorType` keeps it (Jev, KEEP_BLINK, 1.00/1.00); a value that is not a number is 0, as iTerm2's `intValue` reads it, "Bogusly, non-numbers are treated as 0" (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-vt100terminal]]`; Jev, BLOCK, 0.92/0.98); one outside 0 to 2 changes nothing, as in iTerm2, and is counted under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`). `cursor_shape_sets_the_shape_and_keeps_the_blink` |
| `OSC 1337 ; ClearScrollback` | iTerm2: erase the history | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program can erase the lines behind the screen**: "To erase the scrollback history" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), the erasure `CSI 3 J` makes, the screen kept. iTerm2's source clears the screen as well and keeps the prompt (`clearBufferSavingPrompt:`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-screen-terminal-delegate]]`), a departure from the page Jev leaned to below 0.9 (0.50/0.48, asked again after research 0.46/0.54), so baud follows the page. `clear_scrollback_erases_the_history_and_keeps_the_screen` |
| `OSC 1337 ; CurrentDir=dir`, `OSC 1337 ; RemoteHost=user@host` | iTerm2: the shell's directory and its host | done — since 2026-10-22, **the window's title follows a shell that reports its folder this way too**: "Reports the current directory" and "Reports the user name and hostname", `OSC 7` being "a combination of RemoteHost and CurrentDir" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`). The directory is kept as sent, with no percent-decoding, on the host the last `RemoteHost` named after its last `@` (iTerm2's split, "Windows UPN format includes an @ in the user name", its control characters dropped as there, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-screen-mutable-state]]`), this machine until one is named or when one is empty; a `RemoteHost` alone leaves the directory unknown until the next `CurrentDir` (Jev, LAST_REMOTE_HOST, 0.96/0.98). An empty `CurrentDir` leaves none and is counted with `OSC 7`'s refusals (`Term::directories_refused`; Jev's lean, LEAVES_NONE, 0.88/0.92) -- iTerm2 asks the process then, which the core cannot see -- and so is one cut at the 512-byte body, one that does not fit beside its host, and any after a host past 255 octets. `current_dir_and_remote_host_name_the_directory` |
| `OSC 1337 ; ReportCellSize` | iTerm2: the cell size in points | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program that draws images can ask how big a cell is**: "OSC 1337 ; ReportCellSize=[height];[width];[scale] ST" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), the cell's device pixels over the backing scale the embedder tells (`Term::set_backing_scale`; macOS the window's, Linux 1), each number as iTerm2 writes it, `%0.2f` with the trailing zeros trimmed to one (`17.0;8.5;2.0`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-nsstringiterm]]`; Jev, COMPACTED, 0.98/1.00), ended with the query's terminator, which the page's "ST means either BEL (hex code 0x07) or ESC \\" allows (Jev's lean, QUERY_TERMINATOR, 0.64/0.58 and 0.80/0.68 asked again; iTerm2 always sends `ESC \`). Silent, and counted under its key, until the embedder has told both the cell and the scale: a number baud does not know would be a wrong answer. `report_cell_size_answers_in_points` |
| `OSC 1337 ; ReportVariable=base64` | iTerm2: report a session variable | done — since 2026-10-22, answered with the empty value, `ESC ] 1337 ; ReportVariable=`, iTerm2's own answer for a variable it does not report (`reportVariableNamed:`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-vt100output]]`; iTerm2 denies each until the user allows it, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-nagging]]`), for every name: baud keeps no variable a program may read (Jev's lean, NONE, 0.56/0.30 and 0.24/0.18 asked again), and `SetUserVar` is unbuilt with it. Ended with the query's terminator (Jev's lean, QUERY_TERMINATOR, 0.44/0.20 asked again; iTerm2 sends BEL). A name that is not base64 of UTF-8 gets no reply, as iTerm2 sends none, and is counted under its key. `report_variable_answers_with_the_empty_value` |
| `OSC 1337 ; Copy=:base64` | iTerm2: copy to the pasteboard | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program can put text on the clipboard this way too**: "the base64-encoded string to copy to the pasteboard" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), the general one, through `OSC 52`'s path and under its rules: streamed past the 512-byte body from the `:` on, refused whole past the area, put on the pasteboard as an `OSC 52` write to `c` is. Data that is not base64 copies nothing, as iTerm2's decode leaves it nothing to copy, and is counted under its key. **A copy from inside tmux reaches the clipboard too**: iTerm2's segmented copy, which the pages do not name and its own `it2copy` sends whenever `TERM` is tmux's -- `Copy=2;uid`, a `Copy=3;uid:base64` for each line of the base64, `Copy=4;uid` (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-vt100terminal]]`) -- is built (Jev's lean, BUILD_SEGMENTS, 0.06/0.08 and 0.80/0.78 asked again with `it2copy` read): the pieces are decoded into the area as they come, whatever output falls between them, and copied at the end; a piece under another `uid`, or a `Copy=2` or `3` with none, abandons it, and an `OSC 52` or a whole `Copy=:` while it is open, which takes the same area, abandons it too, each counted. `copy_puts_the_text_on_the_clipboard`, `a_segmented_copy_is_copied_at_its_end` |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetColors=key=value` | iTerm2: change a colour | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program written for iTerm2 can recolour the text, the page, the cursor and the text under it, the selection, bold text and the sixteen ANSI colours**: each `key=value` of a list split on `,`, as iTerm2's source splits it (Jev, COMMA_PAIRS, 0.98/1.00), sets the colour `OSC 10`, `11`, `12`, `17`, `19`, `OSC 5 ; 0` and `OSC 4` set for `fg`, `bg`, `curbg`, `selbg`, `selfg`, `bold` and `black` to `br_white`, and their resets undo it (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`). "RGB (three hex digits)" and "RRGGBB", alone (sRGB) or after `srgb:`, `p3:` or `rgb:`; P3 and `rgb` -- macOS's Generic RGB, what iTerm2's source makes of "the device-specific color space" (Jev's lean, CALIBRATED, 0.97/0.89) -- converted to sRGB as a theme file's are. `curfg` sets the text under a block cursor, as `OSC 21 ; cursor_text` does. `link`, `underline`, `tab`, `preset` and the source's `match` and `badge` name colours baud keeps none of, and each such pair is counted, as an `OSC 4` pair baud cannot honour is; so is a pair off the form and the source's `i:N`, a binding that tracks a palette entry from then on, which baud has none of (Jev's lean, COUNTED, 0.90/0.78 and 0.56/0.62 asked again). A colour space none of the three, which the page says a space "should be one of", is read as sRGB, as iTerm2's source reads it in its default space (Jev's lean, DEFAULT_SPACE, 0.62/0.78 and 0.06/0.48 asked again). `set_colors_sets_each_colour_named` |
| `OSC 104` | reset palette | done — **every entry named is reset**, since 2026-10-18: "Any number of c parameters may be given", and with none "the entire table will be reset" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`); one that is no number ends the list, as xterm's `ResetAnsiColorRequest` stops (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3050-3083), and 256 to 260 reset the special colours, as do no parameters at all, xterm's reset walking its 261 colours (`osc_104_resets_every_entry_it_names`, `osc_104_resets_the_palette`, `osc_105_and_104_reset_the_special_colours`). **A number past 260 is passed over and counted, and the list goes on**, since 2026-10-19, as xterm's `ResetOneAnsiColor` ignores it (3036-3046): `104;1;999;2` resets 1 and 2 (`osc_104_goes_on_past_a_number_out_of_range`); baud stopped there before. **An index is its digits, however many**, in `OSC 4` and `OSC 104` alike: `0001` is entry 1, as xterm's `atoi` and `strtol` read it (3001, 3064), where three digits were the most baud read before; a sign or a space is no number, the grammar's `c` being digits, where xterm's readers skip them (`an_index_with_leading_zeros_is_its_number`). A list of more than one entry was refused and counted before |
| `OSC 5`, `OSC 105`, `OSC 6` / `OSC 106` | xterm's special colours (bold, underline, blink, reverse, italic) and their modes | done — since 2026-10-19, **a program can show bold, underlined, blinking, reversed or italic text in a colour of its own instead**, as xterm's colorBD to colorIT do. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "Ps = 5 ; c ; spec -> Change Special Color Number c", any number of pairs, also "by adding the maximum number of colors ... in an OSC 4 control" (256 to 260); "Ps = 1 0 5 ; c -> Reset Special Color Number c", all with none; "Ps = 6 ; c ; f -> Enable/disable Special Color Number c ... OSC 6 is the same as OSC 1 0 6", `c` 0 to 4 the five modes and 5 colorAttrMode. A spec is read as `OSC 4` reads it, a `?` answered in the shape it was asked, `OSC 5 ; c` or `OSC 4 ; 256+c` (xterm's `ReportAnsiColorRequest`, `[[source: xterm-misc]]` 2636-2659), a colour nobody set being the text foreground, drawn and answered so, colorRV's page included (Jev's lean, 0.32 and 0.28, under the bar); `OSC 104` with no parameter and both resets clear them, as xterm's reset of its 261 colours does, and `RIS` turns the modes off (ECMA-48 8.3.105), where xterm keeps its resources, not followed. **What a mode does**: a cell whose foreground is default, or any cell under colorAttrMode, takes the first special colour whose mode is on and whose attribute it carries, blink, then bold, underline and italic, as xterm's `MapToColorMode` orders them (`[[source: xterm-xterm-h]]`), and the attribute is no longer drawn -- no bold face, no underline, no blink, no italic -- "displayed in color or as bold characters" (xterm(1)), decided by Jev at 1.00 both orders; a reversed cell whose page is default, or any under colorAttrMode, takes colorRV as its page and is not exchanged, so its text keeps its colour on a colorRV page, as xterm draws it: `setExtendedBG` gives it colorRV (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file 1140-1150) and `ReverseOrHilite` is false for a reversed cell outside the selection while colorRVMode is on (`[[source: xterm-ptyx-h]]` 1603-1607), so `updatedXtermGC` makes no exchange; a reversed cell with a page of its own keeps its exchange outside colorAttrMode, as xterm(1)'s "displayed only when no ANSI colors have been set" reads, where xterm's `ReverseOrHilite` drops the exchange on every reversed cell, an open departure from xterm's code that follows its manual. **Applied where a rendition is resolved for drawing**, so turning a mode on recolours what is on the screen: the manual's "should be displayed in color", and xterm's own `extract_fg` maps at draw time a cell whose colours are both default (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]` util.c 5680-5690), where its `setExtendedFG` bakes a cell with only a default foreground at `SGR` time (charproc.c 1110-1135); Jev's lean, 0.88 and 0.90. xterm's `OSC 6` handler stops at 4 and ignores colorAttrMode's 5, which ctlseqs lists; ctlseqs is followed (Jev's lean, 0.66 and 0.74). The `c ; f` walk is xterm's: an empty field before a `;` is 0, a `c` with no `f` or a field that is no number ends it, counted, and a `c` no mode has is passed over, counted (`osc_5_sets_and_answers_the_special_colours`, `osc_105_and_104_reset_the_special_colours`, `osc_6_and_106_turn_the_modes_on_and_off`, `the_resets_clear_the_special_colours`; the pixels' rendition in `baud-render/tests/special_colors.rs`: `a_bold_cell_takes_colorbd_and_loses_its_face`, `turning_a_mode_on_recolours_what_is_on_the_screen`, `each_attribute_gives_way_to_its_colour`, `an_explicit_colour_wins_unless_attr_mode`, `a_reversed_cell_shows_colorrv`, `an_unset_colorrv_is_the_text_foreground`). All four were counted as unsupported before |
| `OSC 22 ; Pt` | pointer shape | **partial** — since 2026-10-19, **a program can change the mouse pointer**, as a text editor shows the I-beam or a game a crosshair. "Change pointer cursor shape to Pt. The parameter Pt sets the pointerShape resource. If Pt is empty, or does not match any of the standard names, xterm uses the resource's default" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`): the core keeps one of the 77 names of xterm's table, "adapted from <X11/cursorfont.h>" and matched as `strcmp` matches (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 889-975), for the embedder (`Term::pointer_shape`); an empty or unknown name is the embedder's own pointer, and an unknown one is counted. xterm's code returns on an empty `Pt` ("found no data", 4136-4160) and keeps the shape it had, where the text is followed. `RIS` returns the embedder's pointer, as ECMA-48 8.3.105's initial state, and `DECSTR` keeps it; xterm's reset keeps it, not followed (`osc_22_sets_the_pointer_shape`, `an_empty_or_unknown_osc_22_is_the_embedders_pointer`, `ris_returns_the_embedders_pointer`). **The gap**: the macOS app sets each name as the nearest of AppKit's cursors -- the I-beam for "xterm", the pointing hand for "hand1" and "hand2", the crosshair for the crosses, the open and closed hands for "fleur" and "sizing", not-allowed for "X_cursor" and "pirate", the contextual-menu cursor for "question_arrow" -- and the arrow for the rest, the resize arrows among them, AppKit's resize cursors being deprecated (`baud-app/src/pointer.rs`); a session that names no shape sets no cursor. Whether AppKit keeps the cursor set over the window, whose view has a tracking area and no cursor rects, is unobserved: no test runs the set call. Since 2026-10-20 the Linux driver sets each name as the nearest of `cursor-shape-v1`'s shapes (`[[source: wp-cursor-shape-v1]]`), named with the pointer's last enter serial: "xterm" the text cursor, the hands the pointer, "watch" wait, "question_arrow" help, the crosses the crosshair and "plus" the cell, "fleur" move, "X_cursor" and "pirate" not-allowed, and the side, corner and double-arrow names their resize shapes, 25 names in all, the other 52 the arrow (Jev's lean, NEAREST_ALL, 0.74/0.94); with no `OSC 22`, the text cursor, xterm's `pointerShape` default (`[[source: xterm-man]]`; Jev, TEXT_IBEAM, 0.94 both), because Wayland leaves the image undefined until the client sets one (`[[source: wayland]]`, `wl_pointer.enter`). Since 2026-10-20 a compositor without the cursor-shape global gets a pointer baud draws itself, so the image is never left undefined, as `wl_pointer.enter` asks ("a client should respond to this event by setting an appropriate pointer image with the set_cursor request"; `[[source: wayland]]`): an I-beam for the text shapes and an arrow for the rest, 24 logical pixels, GNOME's `cursor-size` default (`[[source: gnome-interface-schema-2417a27]]`), black in a white rim, drawn at the window's `preferred_buffer_scale` and set with the enter's serial; no cursor theme is read (`each_name_takes_its_nearest_shape`, `the_pointer_reports_by_frame_and_takes_its_shape`, `an_old_pointer_reports_at_once_and_no_global_draws_its_own`, `text_is_the_beam_and_the_rest_the_arrow`). Counted as unsupported before |
| `OSC 60`, `OSC 61 ; Pt`, `OSC 62 ; Pt` | XTQALLOWED, XTQDISALLOWED, XTQALLOWABLE: which optional features are allowed | done — since 2026-10-19, **a program can ask which of xterm's feature categories baud honours**, and gets a true answer in xterm's words. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: `OSC 60` replies "OSC 6 0 ; Pt ST where Pt is a comma-separated list of the allowed optional runtime feature categories", `OSC 61 ; cat` "the optional runtime feature subcategories which would be disallowed if the main feature is disabled", `OSC 62 ; cat` "the optional runtime feature subcategories which correspond to the main feature category". baud has no resources and no switch for any of them, so its fixed behaviour is written in xterm's own model, where a subcategory is allowed when its category is or when the category's disallowed list leaves it out (`AllowWindowOps`, `[[source: xterm-misc]]`): `OSC 60` names the categories baud honours whole, `allowMouseOps` and `allowTitleOps` (the mouse since 2026-10-20, when the DEC `Locator` was built), and `allowFontOps` once the embedder has told the font with changes on; `OSC 61` names, for the others, what baud refuses -- for `allowColorOps` `SetColor` and `GetColor`, because `OSC 10` to `12`, `17` and `19` are honoured but xterm gates them under the same two as the pointer and Tektronix colours, `OSC 13` to `16` and `18`, which baud refuses (`AllocateTermColor` and `ReportColorRequest`, `[[source: xterm-misc]]` 6456 and 3572); for `allowWindowOps`, since 2026-10-21, **exactly the window operations the app does not do**: a manipulation and `SetWinLines` unless the embedder carries out every form of it (`Term::set_window_abilities`) and the window operations are on -- macOS does them all, Linux all but `RestoreWin`, `SetWinPosition`, `RaiseWin`, `LowerWin` and `MaximizeWin`, whose one-axis forms xdg-shell lacks, and, since 2026-10-21, `SetWinSizePixels` and `SetWinSizeChars` too while the compositor's `xdg_wm_base` is below version 4 or not yet bound, since `configure_bounds` is "since 4", a size in pixels is held to it and a 0 in pixels or in cells, "the display's height or width", is taken from it (`osc_61_answers_what_the_bound_xdg_wm_base_allows`) (a subcategory done in part is refused, as `SetColor` is: `SetWinSizePixels` by Jev's lean, LISTED, 0.64/0.34 and 0.78/0.66 after research, and `SetWinSizeChars`, whose 0 needs the bounds too, by that rule), and a core no embedder told none -- a report until the fact it answers from is told (`GetWinState`, `GetWinPosition`, `GetWinSizePixels`, `GetScreenSizeChars`, each by xterm's gate; `GetWinSizeChars` always), the title reports, `GetSelection` (`OSC 52`'s read), `SetXprop` (`OSC 3`) while the window operations are switched off, `GetChecksum` while DECRQCRA is switched off, and `SetSelection` on a storage with no clipboard area, which refuses every write, or while the app's `[clipboard] osc52` is off (`Term::set_osc52_writes`), where it named every manipulation and `SetWinLines` whatever the app did and `SetSelection` only on a storage with no area (`osc_61_names_the_window_operations_the_embedder_does_not_do`, `the_window_abilities_reach_the_core_from_both_drivers`, `the_abilities_are_what_each_driver_does`); for `allowTcapOps` both of its, and for `allowFontOps` both until the embedder has told the font, and `SetFont` while `[font] changes` is off (the `OSC 50` row) -- and `OSC 62` xterm's table for the category, as a default build compiles it, and since 2026-10-20 `StatusLine` last in `allowWindowOps`', as an `OPT_STATUS_LINE` build lists it, baud having the status line, which `OSC 61` never names: with the operations off the grid gives the line its row (the DECSSDT row; `the_feature_queries_name_the_status_line_truthfully`). The paste controls are the embedder's to filter, and `OSC 60` never names `allowPasteControls`; `OSC 61 ; allowPasteControls` is empty until the embedder says what it replaces (`Term::set_paste_controls`), and since 2026-10-21 both apps say xterm's default, so it answers `NUL,EOT,ENQ,BS,ESC,DEL,STTY`, xterm's list in xterm's order (`osc_61_names_the_paste_controls_the_embedder_replaces`). Shapes are xterm's `report_allowed_ops` and `unparse_disallowed_ops` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file 10666-10722): the query's terminator, no `;` before an empty list, the category matched in any case with the alias `allowWinOps`, an empty list for a name that is no category; `OSC 60` with data and a bare `61` or `62` are answered with silence, as `do_osc` returns, and counted. Decided by Jev, confidence 0.98 both orders, over silence (probability 0.01) and xterm's default values (probability 0.00); the first asking, 0.86 both, was re-asked after research (`osc_60_names_the_categories_baud_honours_whole`, `osc_61_names_what_baud_refuses`, `osc_62_names_every_subcategory`, `a_malformed_feature_query_is_counted`, `the_longest_feature_reply_fits`). Counted as unsupported before |
| `CSI ? 1049 h/l` | alternate screen buffer | done — `?1049 h` saves the cursor "as in DECSC", switches to the alternate screen and clears it (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), and **leaves the cursor where it was**: the text names no move, and xterm's set arm is `CursorSave; ToAlternate; ClearScreen` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, 7732-7745), whose `ClearScreen` does not home (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]`'s file, 2356-2383). Since 2026-10-13, the owner's decision between leaving the cursor (conforming) and homing as a stated departure; until then a first `?1049 h` homed, to the origin under DECOM (`entering_leaves_the_cursor`, `entering_the_alternate_screen_under_origin_mode_leaves_the_cursor`). `ClearScreen` resets the wrap (`ResetWrap`), and so does the entry: a cursor in pending wrap at the screen's edge stays in the last column with nothing pending (`entering_from_a_pending_wrap_drops_the_wrap`); one pending one past a right margin narrower than the screen is kept, the sentinel and not a placed cursor, and the next character wraps (counted by a scratch harness at all 31 read configurations of `?69h`, `CSI 1;10 s`, `CUP 9;10`, `a`, `?1049 h`, `b`). **The save is the primary's `DECSC` slot**, since 2026-10-20, xterm's `CursorSave` into `sc[screen->whichBuf]` (`[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]`): an `ESC 7` before `?1049 h` is overwritten by it, and `ESC 8` on the primary after `?1049 l` or `?47 l` finds it (`the_1049_save_is_the_primarys_decsc`); the cursor's style and `?12` ride beside it, `?1049` only. A second `?1049 h` on the alternate screen clears it and saves the alternate screen's cursor into the alternate screen's slot, leaving the primary's (`a_second_1049_set_saves_into_the_alternate_slot`), and does not reset a pending wrap made on the alternate screen, where `ClearScreen` would: `CUP 9;200`, `a`, `?1049 h`, `CUP 9;200`, `c`, `?1049 h`, `b` puts the `b` at 10;1 at all 36 read configurations. `?1049 l` restores the cursor and rendition (`the_cursor_is_saved_and_restored`, `the_rendition_is_saved_and_restored`) **as `DECRC` does, on the primary too and with nothing saved**: "restore cursor as in DECRC", xterm's `CursorRestore` after `FromAlternate`, so a `?1049 l` with nothing saved, a stray `rmcup` at a shell prompt included, puts the cursor home with the rendition normal, where it did nothing until 2026-10-20 (`leaving_with_nothing_saved_restores_as_decrc_does`, `leaving_when_not_on_the_alt_screen_restores_as_decrc`). The restore does not empty the slot. **Its clear takes the pictures too**, since 2026-10-21, as xterm's `ClearScreen` does: `?1049 h` on the alternate screen, after `?47 h`, frees the Sixel there (`the_1049_clear_on_the_alternate_screen_frees_its_sixel`), and entering from the primary leaves the primary's (`entering_the_alternate_screen_leaves_the_primary_s_sixel`) |
| `CSI ? 2031 h/l` | colour palette update notifications | done — since 2026-09-29. Reset at power-on and DECRQM answers the bit, so neovim's handshake subscribes (`neovims_handshake_subscribes_and_re_queries`); **nothing is sent on setting it**, as tmux learned the hard way. While set, an embedder colour change that moves the dark/light class sends one `CSI ? 997 ; 1\|2 n`: `Term::set_theme` and `Term::set_default_colors` compare the class before and after the whole change, so a class crossed inside one call is not reported (`a_class_crossed_inside_one_call_is_not_reported`). An application's own colour escapes send nothing and change the next `?996 n` answer. `RIS` clears it; `DECSTR` keeps it -- baud's rule, since xterm has no `?2031` (`ris_clears_the_mode_and_decstr_keeps_it`). The macOS app's OS light/dark flip under `theme = "auto"` reaches it through `tell_defaults` with `Overrides::Drop`, dropping the application's `OSC 4`/`10`/`11`/`12`/`17`/`19` colours -- only when the resolved appearance changes, since AppKit also calls back for accessibility variants of the same one (`palette_switch`, `a_flip_drops_the_applications_colours_and_reports_once`). Since 2026-10-21 the Linux app's does too, on the Settings portal's `SettingChanged`; the portal's answer at launch, which races the shell's first output, keeps them (`the_launch_answer_keeps_a_programs_colours_and_a_change_drops_them`) |
| `CSI ? 2048 h/l` | in-band resize notifications | done — reports `CSI 48 ; rows ; cols ; height_px ; width_px t` on enablement, on re-enablement, after every resize, and **after a cell-size change even when the grid did not move**, carrying what the terminal **has** rather than what was asked for. The pixel fields are the text area -- the grid times the cell size `Term::set_cell_size` was given -- and zero until one is given, which `[[source: inband-resize]]` defines as the answer of a terminal that cannot report them; "A terminal MUST report pixel sizes if it is capable", and "Font size changes can also affect the text area size", which is why a zoom reports. Until 2026-09-22 the fields were always zero. `the_inband_report_carries_pixels_once_the_cell_is_known`, `a_cell_size_change_reports_even_with_the_grid_unchanged` |
| `CSI 11 t`, `CSI 13 t`, `CSI 13 ; 2 t`, `CSI 14 t`, `CSI 14 ; 2 t`, `CSI 15 t`, `CSI 16 t`, `CSI 18 t`, `CSI 19 t` | XTWINOPS reports: window state, position and size, the text area, the cell, the screen | done — **a program can ask where the window is, how big it and the screen are, and whether it is minimized**, since 2026-10-19: `CSI 1 t` or `CSI 2 t` (open or iconified), `CSI 3 ; x ; y t` for the window's position and for the text area's (x first, a negative position as its sixteen-bit two's complement, ctlseqs' "Negative values correspond to 32768-65535"), `CSI 4 ; h ; w t` for the window with its frame, `CSI 5 ; h ; w t` for the screen in pixels and `CSI 9 ; rows ; cols t` for the screen in characters, its pixels over the cell's as xterm divides them (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`; `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, `window_ops`). Each answers from what the embedder hands `Term::set_window`, every position and size in the device pixels `CSI 16 t` answers in, and **is silent and counted where it handed nothing**, never a made-up number (`the_window_reports_answer_from_what_the_embedder_handed_over`, `a_report_the_embedder_cannot_answer_is_silent_and_counted`, `the_window_and_screen_answers_have_ctlseqs_shapes`). The macOS app reads AppKit before every read it feeds -- the frame, the view taken to the screen, the window's screen and the primary one, measured from the primary screen's top-left (`[[source: apple-nswindow-frame]]`, `[[source: apple-nswindow-converttoscreen]]`, `[[source: apple-nsscreen-frame]]`, `[[source: apple-nsscreen-screens]]`; `the_window_facts_are_appkits_geometry_in_device_pixels_from_the_top`); the Linux app knows only its surface, so it answers `CSI 14 ; 2 t` and nothing else of the window -- xdg-shell tells a client neither its position nor that it is minimized, and no `wl_output` is bound. **The text area and the cell**: `CSI 4 ; h ; w t` (text area in pixels), `CSI 6 ; h ; w t` (one cell in pixels) and `CSI 8 ; rows ; cols t` (text area in cells), in `[[source: ctlseqs]]`'s shapes, height first. **The two pixel answers need the cell size and are silence and a count without it**, the way an unanswerable `DSR` is; the cell count is always answered. Any other second parameter is undefined and counted. This is the reply an image client sizes its pictures from -- yazi sends `CSI 16 t` in its detection burst and read nothing back before 2026-09-22, so it drew every Sixel at a fixed 600x900. `the_window_queries_answer_from_the_cell_size_and_the_grid`, `without_a_cell_size_only_the_cell_count_is_answered`, `yazi_reads_the_cell_size_from_its_detection_burst`, `every_other_t_is_refused_or_not_xtwinops` |
| `CSI 22 ; Ps t`, `CSI 23 ; Ps t` | XTWINOPS title stack: save and restore the window title and icon name | done — since 2026-10-19, **a program that sets its own title can put the shell's back when it exits**, as vim does with `CSI 22 ; 2 t` and `CSI 23 ; 2 t`. `Ps` 0 both names, 1 the icon name, 2 the title (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), an omitted `Ps` 0 as xterm reads it (Jev's lean, AS_ZERO, 0.88/0.88); a third parameter from 1 to 10 stores or reads that slot without pushing or popping, and one past 10, outside ctlseqs' range, is counted. The stack is xterm's (`xtermPushTitle`, `xtermPopTitle`, `[[source: xterm-misc]]`; `MAX_SAVED_TITLES`, `[[source: xterm-ptyx-h]]`): ten slots, the eleventh push over the oldest, a pop of an empty stack doing nothing, a half a push did not take filled from the nearest older slot, and a half still empty leaving that name -- so vim's icon-name restore, the second of its two pops, finds nothing and leaves vim's icon name, as it does in xterm (`vims_title_push_and_pop_restore_the_shells_title`, `a_half_the_push_did_not_take_comes_from_an_older_slot`, `the_eleventh_push_overwrites_the_oldest`, `a_slot_from_one_to_ten_reads_and_writes_without_pushing`, `the_title_stack_is_chunk_invariant`). `RIS` and `DECSTR` keep it, as both keep the title itself and xterm's resets do (Jev, RIS_KEEPS, 0.94/0.98; `the_resets_keep_the_title_stack`). |
| `CSI # S` | XTTITLEPOS report the title stack's position | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can ask how many titles it has saved**: answered `CSI Pn ; Pm # S`, the pushes not yet popped and the stack's ten levels (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 664), as xterm's `xtermReportTitleStack` writes them, so `Pn` passes 10 once a push has overwritten the oldest slot (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`; Jev, RAW, 0.90/0.96). A direct slot moves neither (`xttitlepos_reports_the_pushes_and_the_depth`, `a_direct_slot_leaves_the_title_position`). |
| `CSI > t`, `CSI > T` | XTSMTITLE / XTRMTITLE title modes, `CSI > Pm t` and `CSI > Pm T` | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can send its title in hexadecimal, and send a UTF-8 title under `ESC % @`**. Mode 0 decodes `OSC 0`, `1` and `2` from hexadecimal, either case, as xterm's x_decode_hex and `ChangeGroup` do: a body that is not an even count of hex digits changes nothing and is counted, and the name ends at its first byte below 32 (`[[source: xterm-xstrings-c]]`, `[[source: xterm-misc]]`; `a_hex_title_is_decoded_while_the_mode_is_set`, `a_title_that_is_not_hex_changes_nothing_and_is_counted`). Mode 2 reads a title as UTF-8: under UTF-8 every title is, set or reset, as xterm's `utf8Title` resource follows the encoding (Jev, RESOURCE_FOLLOWS, 0.96/0.98; `under_utf8_mode_2_changes_no_title`); under `ESC % @` it keeps the GR bytes of an `OSC 0`, `1` or `2` body, the text's "window/icon labels", where xterm keeps `0` and `2` alone (Jev's lean, BUILD_TEXT: after a second ask the orders split, BUILD_XTERM at 0.38 in one and BUILD_TEXT at 0.82 in the other, decisions 976-977, and the one with the higher mean probability was taken; `under_latin1_the_utf8_mode_keeps_a_titles_gr_bytes`, `a_kept_gr_title_is_chunk_invariant`). Each name keeps the encoding its bytes are in, UTF-8 where they are valid under mode 2 or in hexadecimal and ISO 8859-1 otherwise, on the title stack too (`Term::title_encoding`, `a_hex_title_is_utf8_only_where_it_is_valid`, `the_title_stack_keeps_each_titles_encoding`). **Modes 1 and 3, the query modes, are refused and counted with the title reports** they shape, never recorded (Jev, REFUSE_COUNT, 0.90/0.90; `the_query_modes_are_refused_with_the_title_reports`); a value past 3 is counted. With no parameter both return to the default, none set, as the text says; xterm's code never reaches that branch. `RIS` resets them and `DECSTR` keeps them, as xterm's resets do (`ris_resets_the_title_modes_and_decstr_keeps_them`). DECRQSS answers them (the DECRQSS row). |
| `CSI # {`, `CSI # }` | XTPUSHSGR / XTPOPSGR save and restore the rendition, and their aliases `CSI # p` and `CSI # q` | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can save the colours and attributes it is drawing in and put them back**, ten levels deep (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 1525-1568 and 1871-1953), as xterm's `xtermPushSGR` and `xtermPopSGR` keep them (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`): a bare push saves the whole rendition, an underline colour, a font and the attributes xterm lacks among it (`a_bare_push_and_pop_restore_the_whole_rendition`, `the_aliases_push_and_pop`); a list saves the groups it names, 1 bold to 9 crossed-out, 21 double underline, 30 and 31 the colours (`a_list_push_restores_only_its_groups`, `the_colour_groups_are_thirty_and_thirty_one`), and `CSI 0 # {` none (`a_push_of_zero_saves_nothing`); 10 and 11, SGR's fonts, name no group, where xterm reads them as 30 and 31 (Jev's lean, ALIAS, 0.94/0.86, below the 0.9 a departure needs, so the text is built; `the_colour_groups_are_thirty_and_thirty_one`). 5 restores both blink rates, one aspect by the owner's call (`five_restores_the_blink_rate`); 4 and 21 restore xterm's two underline bits on baud's one underline style (Jev's lean, XTERM_BITS, 0.28/0.18; `four_and_twenty_one_restore_xterms_underline_bits`); what xterm does not have is restored only by a bare push (Jev's lean, NEAREST, 0.86/0.38). An eleventh push stores nothing and its pop restores nothing, as xterm's stack counts on; a pop of an empty stack is counted (`the_stack_is_ten_deep`). **`RIS` empties the stack and `DECSTR` keeps it**: RIS returns to the initial state, where nothing is saved, as it empties XTSAVE's cache; xterm's resets keep it (Jev's lean, KEEP, 0.84/0.86, below the 0.9 a departure needs, so the text is built; `ris_empties_the_rendition_stack_and_decstr_keeps_it`). |
| `CSI # \|` | XTREPORTSGR report the rendition a rectangle shares | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can ask what colours and attributes a block of the screen has in common**: answered `CSI 0 ; Ps... m`, every attribute, colour and font all its cells share, in DECRQSS's spelling (Jev, ALL_COMMON, 0.96/0.98), where xterm reports its own subset (`xtermReportSGR`, `[[source: xterm-misc]]`; `xtreportsgr_answers_the_attributes_every_cell_shares`). The rectangle is read as DECRQCRA's is, from the origin under DECOM and clamped to the page (`xtreportsgr_counts_from_the_origin_under_decom`); one holding no cell answers `CSI 0 m`, as xterm's does (Jev, ANSWER_ZERO, 0.96/0.98; `an_empty_rectangle_answers_zero`). |
