| `CSI # P`, `CSI # Q` | XTPUSHCOLORS / XTPOPCOLORS save and restore the palette | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program that recolours the terminal can put the colours back as they were**, ten palettes deep (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 644-656): a palette is the 256 `OSC 4` colours, the five special colours and the dynamic ones, `OSC 10`, `11`, `12`, `17` and `19`, as xterm's `ColorSlot` holds them (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`). A pop puts each back through its setter, so the screen repaints only where a colour changed (`a_pop_restores_the_palette_the_specials_and_the_dynamic_colours`, `a_pop_that_recolours_moves_the_colour_epoch`). Each parameter is one push or pop, a bare one the current entry, as xterm's loop takes them (`each_parameter_is_one_push`); 1 to 10 stores or reads that palette "without pushing" or "without popping", the current entry left where it is, as the title stack's direct slots are; xterm's `xtermPushColors` and `xtermPopColors` move it (Jev's lean, XTERM, 0.58/0.50, below the 0.9 a departure needs, so the text is built; `a_direct_palette_leaves_the_current_entry`). An eleventh push, a pop of an empty stack, a palette never stored and one past 10 change nothing and are counted (`the_stack_is_ten_deep_and_refusals_are_counted`). **`RIS` empties the stack and `DECSTR` keeps it**, as for the rendition stack (Jev's lean, KEEP, 0.84/0.86, below the 0.9 a departure needs; `ris_empties_the_palette_stack_and_decstr_keeps_it`). |
| `CSI # R` | XTREPORTCOLORS report the palette stack | done — since 2026-10-20, answered `CSI ? Pu ; Ps # Q`, the current entry and the palettes stored, with the `?` xterm's `xtermReportColors` writes, which keeps an echoed answer from popping two palettes: a departure from ctlseqs' "same form as XTPOPCOLORS", `CSI Pm # Q` (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 658-660), decided by Jev (XTERM_Q, 0.94/0.96) and on the owner's veto list (`xtreportcolors_answers_the_entry_and_the_palettes_stored`, `the_report_follows_s8c1t`). The palettes stored are counted, the text's "number of palettes stored on the stack", which is xterm's high-water mark wherever only bare pushes were made. |
| `CSI 1 t` to `CSI 10 t` | XTWINOPS window operations: de-iconify, iconify, move, resize in pixels, raise, lower, refresh, resize in cells, maximise, full screen | done — since 2026-10-19, **a program can move, resize, minimise, raise, lower, maximise or full-screen the window**, the owner's call (2026-10-19, option 4, "4 it is"): built, on by default, and `[window] operations = "off"` turns them off. Each form takes the parameters `[[source: ctlseqs]]` gives it: 1, 2, 5, 6 and 7 none, 3 `x ; y`, 4 `height ; width` in pixels, 8 `height ; width` in characters, 9 `Ps` 0 to 3, 10 `Ps` 0 to 2, an omitted `Ps` 0 as xterm's `zero_if_default` reads it (`window_ops`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file). For 4 and 8, "Omitted parameters reuse the current height or width. Zero parameters use the display's height or width": an omitted or empty one keeps the size, 0 is the screen's, as xterm's `optional_param` and `RequestResize` read them. A move's 32768-65535 are -32768 to -1, the sixteen-bit two's complement `CSI 13 t` reports a position in, so the position reported, sent back, is where the window goes; xterm reads its move through X's signed `Position` (Jev's lean SIGNED16, 0.65 and 0.68 after a second ask). **Refused and counted in `unsupported_param`**, as forms ctlseqs does not give: 12 and 17, 9 ; 4 and up and 10 ; 3 and up, where xterm's `RequestMaximize` falls through to a restore, and a parameter more than a form takes. The core cannot move a window: it queues each request for the embedder (`Term::window_requests`, sixteen between drains, a request past them dropped and counted in `Term::window_requests_dropped`) and changes nothing itself, the grid included; a resize reaches the grid through the embedder's `Term::resize`, as the user's own does, so the rest of the read that asked is parsed at the old size, where xterm resizes before it parses on. Off (`Term::set_window_ops`, `WindowOps::Off`), nothing is queued and each is counted in `Term::window_ops_refused`, as xterm performs none it disallows; no sequence turns them back on, `RIS` and `DECSTR` included, and the reports and the title stack answer either way (`each_window_operation_is_queued_for_the_embedder`, `a_form_ctlseqs_does_not_give_is_counted_and_not_queued`, `omitted_and_zero_parameters_reach_the_embedder_as_sent`, `with_the_operations_off_nothing_is_queued_and_each_is_counted`, `no_reset_turns_the_window_operations_back_on`, `a_full_queue_drops_and_counts_the_rest`; the domain's `the_window_operations_parse_to_their_names`, `a_resize_tells_omitted_from_zero`, `a_move_reads_its_coordinates_as_the_report_writes_them`). **macOS** performs each with AppKit once the callback that asked has returned: `deminiaturize:`, `miniaturize:`, `orderFrontRegardless`, which takes no focus, `orderBack:`, `toggleFullScreen:` when it changes the state asked for, and `setFrame:display:` for the rest. 3 puts the frame's top-left where `CSI 13 t` measures it; 4 sizes the content view, the text and its padding, as xterm sizes its VT100 widget, the text and its border (Jev, CONTENT_VIEW, 0.98 and 0.98); 8 the content a grid that size fills; each resize keeps the top-left and is no larger than the screen's visible frame, as xterm's `limitResize` of 1 limits one to the display's size, and since 2026-10-21 stays inside that frame top to bottom, a window that grows down past its bottom moving up rather than going under the Dock, as AppKit's `constrainFrameRect(_:to:)` holds a window's top edge and height to the screen (`a_frame_that_grows_stays_inside_the_visible_frame`; the status line's row too), and with no screen to measure against a resize in pixels is refused and one in cells is held to the largest grid. The requests one read brings are each measured on the window the ones before them leave, since AppKit performs none until the callback returns, so a move then a resize keeps the move. 9 resizes to the visible frame, both ways, its height or its width, saving the frame once for 9 ; 0 to return to, as xterm's `RequestMaximize` saves one; 7 draws the next frame whole. In full screen a move, a resize and a maximise do nothing, and a `BAUD_GRID`-pinned grid keeps the window's size (the app's `a_move_lands_where_the_report_measures_from`, `a_pixel_resize_sizes_the_content_view`, `a_cell_resize_gives_the_grid_asked_for`, `a_maximise_saves_the_frame_a_restore_returns_to`, `a_toggle_is_called_only_when_it_changes_the_window`, `the_reported_position_moved_to_is_where_the_window_was`, `the_window_operations_run_after_the_callback_that_asked`, `with_no_screen_a_pixel_resize_is_refused`, `a_batch_is_measured_on_the_window_each_request_leaves`). **Linux** does what xdg-shell has (`[[source: wp-xdg-shell]]`): 2 `set_minimized`, 9 ; 1 and 9 ; 0 `set_maximized` and `unset_maximized`, 10 `set_fullscreen` and `unset_fullscreen`, 10 ; 2 by the state the last configure carried, 7 a frame drawn whole, and 4, 8 and DECSLPP a size the window chooses while floating, held inside the compositor's `configure_bounds` (`wayland_does_what_xdg_shell_has`, `the_window_operations_reach_the_toplevel_as_its_requests`, `a_floating_window_takes_its_own_size_and_a_maximised_one_does_not`). Unbuilt there, each counted and warned once with its reason: 1, since `set_minimized` has no undoing, "nor is there any way to unset minimization on this surface"; 3, since `move` is "an interactive, user-driven move" that "must be used in response to some sort of user action"; 5 and 6, since xdg-shell has no stacking request; 9 ; 2 and 9 ; 3, since `set_maximized` has no axis; a resize while maximised, where the size "must be obeyed", or in full screen, where it "is a maximum"; and a size in pixels, 0 or given, before the compositor has sent its bounds, the one size of the screen xdg-shell tells a client; one in cells is held to the largest grid then. Full screen on 10 is ctlseqs' "Change to full-screen"; xterm 411's `window_ops` calls `RequestMaximize` there, the same resize as 9 ; 1, which is its departure. Nobody has watched any of it on a screen: the app's halves are proven as data and by source gates, as no test opens a window |
| `CSI Ps t` with `Ps` 24 and up | DECSLPP set lines per page | done — since 2026-10-19, the owner's call (2026-10-19, option 4): `[[source: ctlseqs]]`'s "Ps >= 2 4 -> Resize to Ps lines (DECSLPP), VT340 and VT420. xterm adapts this by resizing its window", as xterm's `RequestResize(xw, code, -1, True)` does, the columns kept: a request for `Ps` rows, the same `Term::window_requests` entry as `CSI 8 ; Ps t`, through the same setting, `[window] operations`. `[[source: vt510-decslpp]]`: "If the number of lines specified is not one of the values supported by the implementation, then the next higher supported number of lines per page is assumed. If the number of lines specified exceeds the maximum number supported, then the maximum number supported is used"; baud's page is its screen, every count from 24 to the window's largest is one it supports, and a count past the screen is the screen's, as the macOS app holds a resize inside its screen and the Linux one inside the compositor's bounds once it has sent them. Since 2026-10-22 DECRQSS `t` answers the page as the control that sets it, the screen's rows and 24 on a shorter one (the DECRQSS row). A count with a second parameter is refused, as a form ctlseqs does not give; 23 and below are XTWINOPS's own forms (`each_window_operation_is_queued_for_the_embedder`, `the_other_requests_carry_what_ctlseqs_gives_them`, `a_cell_resize_gives_the_grid_asked_for`) |
| `DCS Pa ; Pb ; Ph q ... ST` | Sixel graphics | done — decoded into caller memory with no external decoder (`[[source: vt3xx-sixel]]` is the raster grammar, `[[source: ctlseqs]]` the DCS envelope), placed at the cursor and drawn by all three render paths. **A colour register takes RGB and HLS**: HLS since 2026-10-11, with DEC's hue origin, "Blue: 0 degrees Red: 120 degrees Green: 240 degrees" (`[[source: vt3xx-screen-control]]`), by the double hexcone of `[[source: css-color-4-hsl]]`, within one percent of the VT340's sixteen default colours on that chapter's Table 2-3 (`hls_defines_reproduce_the_vt340_default_colour_map`, `the_hls_hue_origin_is_blue_at_zero_red_at_120_green_at_240`); an HLS value past its range ignores the whole command, `Pc` included, `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 §8.1.3 (`an_hls_define_out_of_range_is_ignored_whole`). It was counted and left unpainted until then. **A sixel pixel is drawn at its pixel aspect ratio where the two DEC texts agree**, since 2026-10-11: `P1` omitted, 0, 1 or 5 is 2:1, 3 is 3:1 and 9 is 1:1 (`[[source: vt3xx-sixel]]`; `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 §7.1), and a raster command's whole `Pan:Pad` before any data or `$` or `-` overrides it, an omitted or zero one reading as 1 (070-9 §8.1.2) -- `a_sixel_pixel_is_drawn_at_its_pixel_aspect_ratio`, `the_macro_parameter_sets_the_aspect_where_both_dec_texts_make_it_whole`, `a_raster_ratio_before_the_data_overrides_the_macro`. Every image was 1:1 before. **`P2` 0 or 2 sets the positions the data leaves at zero to the background**, since 2026-10-22, and so does an absent or empty `P2`, which is 0 (`[[source: vt3xx-sixel]]` 192-203 and 302-303): over the raster extent `Ph` by `Pv` when a raster command before the data sends one, else from the image's corner to the right and bottom margins in force, the extent `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 §10 starts a Sixel with and what a VT340 clears (`[[source: vt340test-p2effect-sh]]` 6-7, 12-13; Jev, EXTENT_ELSE_MARGINS, 0.92/0.94), to the screen's edge on a side the cursor is past, stopped at the screen's edges in display mode, and over the data's reach on a side no cell size measures (`a_raster_extent_is_filled_where_the_data_left_zero`, `without_a_raster_the_fill_reaches_the_margins`, `a_cursor_past_a_margin_fills_to_the_screen_edge`, `display_mode_stops_the_fill_at_the_screen_edges`, `with_no_cell_size_the_fill_is_the_datas_reach`, `p2_1_keeps_the_zero_positions_clear`); **and since 2026-10-22 the fill goes on as the image scrolls**: data that runs past the rows left to the bottom margin scrolls the screen, and the rows it scrolls in are filled across the fill's width from the band that overran them to the data's last row rounded up to a whole cell, the rows left counted as filled, so a raster extent shorter than them leaves the rows between as they were, as Windows Terminal's `_fillImageBackgroundWhenScrolled()` does, "to match the behavior of the original hardware terminals" (`[[source: wt-sixel-parser]]`; `the_fill_goes_on_as_the_image_scrolls`, and the pixel oracle on all three paths), where the fill stopped at `Pv` or the margins before (open: Windows Terminal goes on filling at every graphics new line, an empty trailing band included, where baud fills to the data's reach; and with the cursor below the bottom margin, the fill reaches rows below the screen that never scroll in, where Windows Terminal stops extending the image once no height is left); in the default background as `OSC 11` leaves it, read each frame (Jev, DEFAULT_BG_EACH_FRAME, 0.97/0.94), and unreversed under `?5`, as a VT340 fills with colour-map entry 0 on a reversed screen (`[[source: vt340test-background-select-2-png]]`) and xterm's graphics read no reverse video (`[[source: xterm-graphics-c]]`; Jev, UNREVERSED, 0.98/0.94); opaque over the text under it, alike on all three paths (the pixel oracle, `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws`). The cursor goes below the data, not the fill (`the_fill_does_not_move_the_cursor_further`); text on the fill alone clears it in the cells it writes, as a VT340 writes text into its one bitmap, and text on the data frees the image as before (`text_on_the_fill_clears_its_cells_and_keeps_the_image`, `text_on_the_data_frees_the_image`); an erase clears it in the cells it erases (`an_erase_clears_the_fill_in_the_cells_it_erases`); a hardcopy prints it as the background (`a_sixel_fill_prints_as_the_background`); and a filled image is counted in `sixels_filled`. **On the see-through window the fill is not drawn yet**: how a filled position draws over backgrounds that carry the window's alpha is the owner's question (Jev leaned to opaque at 0.77 and 0.79, under the bar; foot draws it opaque), so there a `P2` of 0 draws as 1 does. Until 2026-10-22 every zero position was transparent and a `P2` of 0 or 2 was only counted. **Two gaps, for Jev**: a ratio that is not a whole number (`P1` 2, 4, 6, 7, 8, which 070-9 gives as 4.5, 2.5, 1.5, 1.3 and 1.12 to 1, and a raster `"3;2`), and a `P1` past 9, are drawn 1:1 (note AN's item 12); and the horizontal grid size, the envelope's third parameter (ctlseqs' `Ph`, not the raster command's), is dropped, a sixel pixel one image pixel wide, where 070-9 §12 asks a device to state its fallback. **An undefined colour register paints the foreground shown** since 2026-10-15, `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 §10, "Sixel color: Contrasting to background. Color numbers: All unspecified color numbers image as the default Sixel color" -- the embedder's default foreground, the theme's background under `?5`, and nothing while the core has not been told one; decided by Jev at 0.92 and 0.95 over the VT340's colour map, which xterm and foot load, and again at 0.96 and 0.98 once measured: yazi paints every fully transparent pixel with register 0, which it never defines (`[[source: yazi-sixel]]`), so its previews of images with transparency now show the foreground there, on the owner's veto list (`an_undefined_register_paints_the_foreground_shown`). Every undefined register was transparent before. A register number past 255 is mapped back to 255, which 070-9 §8.1.3 requires ("the unimplemented index must be mapped back into the supported range") and fixes no mapping for; Jev did not choose to count it or wrap it. **The row the cursor ends on is the owner's question**: it is the row below the image, where `[[source: vt3xx-sixel]]` sets the text cursor "to the current sixel cursor position", which a VT340 rounds up to the row it falls in (`[[source: vt340test-textcursor-sh]]`), and 070-9 §11.3 allows only "an adjustment less than one character height ... in a downward direction"; Jev chose the VT340's row at 0.91 and 0.88. **The column after an image is the one it began in**, 070-9 §11.3, and a VT340 keeps it (`[[source: vt340test-textcursor-sh]]`), where the VT330/VT340 manual's "the text cursor is set to the current sixel cursor position" reads the other way, a conflict between the texts recorded for Jev (`the_advance_keeps_the_column_where_the_image_started`); a zero repeat count is one, 070-9 §8.1.1, and so is an omitted one, `[[source: dec-pplv2-pm]]` p. 5-26 (`a_zero_repeat_draws_one_column`). **An image follows the row it was placed on**: scrolling into the history, a shorter screen retiring its row and a taller one pulling it back, a narrower screen re-cutting the lines above it, and a reader scrolled back to it. Its column is kept, and it is cropped at the edge rather than moved. It goes when its last row does -- the `ceil(h / cell_h)` rows it is drawn over, the rows text written over it counts, leaving the far end of the history or the alternate screen's top -- or when its anchor is blanked by a reverse scroll or dropped by a reflow that did not fit, and each is counted in `placements_expired`. So a tall image whose top line scrolled off the screen keeps its lower rows there, as "The top line of the sixel image may scroll off the screen" (`[[source: vt3xx-sixel]]`, "Sixel Scrolling Enabled") describes and xterm draws (`[[source: xterm-graphics-c]]` 1787-1808), since 2026-10-18; it went whole with its anchor row before (`a_tall_sixel_goes_when_its_last_row_leaves_the_history`, `a_tall_sixel_on_the_alternate_screen_goes_with_its_last_row`, `a_taller_cell_takes_a_sixel_whose_rows_are_then_all_gone`). A cell height the embedder has not given is one row. `CSI 3 J` keeps one with a row on screen, as xterm's erase of the saved lines touches no graphic (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]`, `case 3`; `erasing_the_history_keeps_a_sixel_with_rows_on_screen`). **Each screen owns its images**: the shell's are not drawn over a full-screen program, and the program's are deleted when it leaves. Text over an image frees it. **A Sixel is decoded as its body arrives** (since 2026-10-14, the owner's decided end state of 2026-10-13): each byte the handler keeps paints into the gap of the image arena the `q` claimed, the largest free one, and no copy of the body is kept, so **what limits an image is its pixels** -- the arena (`[images] pixels_mb`, 95 MiB by default since 2026-10-16, 4 MiB before), the 65535 a coordinate holds each way and the slot table -- and never its bytes, as `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 has it: §8.1.2's raster extent "does not limit the number of pixels a device may image" (its p. 9, PDF p. 920), §2 asks that "The protocol should support any size rasters, although not all devices will be capable of displaying them" (p. 2, PDF p. 913), and no section read limits a body's bytes. The raster's `Ph`, "a summary to avoid a complete scanning of the data" (§8.1.2), lays the rows out; data past it is drawn, and a stride wider than the image is given back at the next band. At its terminator an image moves to the lowest gap that holds it, where a measured decode would have stored it, and over generated programs of Sixels, with frees between them and between two reads of a body, the store is the buffered decoder's of 2992a8e9 (but for a band refused before such a free), image for image, offset for offset, refusal for refusal (`a_program_of_sixels_leaves_the_store_the_buffered_decoder_left`, `a_free_inside_a_body_leaves_the_store_the_buffered_decoder_left`; a resize inside a body, `a_resize_inside_a_body_frees_room_the_rest_of_it_can_use`), as are the committed yazi walk's thirteen previews (`every_preview_in_a_yazi_walk_is_the_buffered_decoders_image`); a terminal draws the same at every read boundary (`a_sixel_program_draws_alike_at_every_read_boundary`, `every_read_size_and_every_split_of_an_abandoning_program_draw_alike`), a body cut off by `CAN`, an `ESC` that is not `ST` or a C1 is placed from what it received and the byte then runs, as if `ST` had come first, since 2026-10-19, where it left nothing, and a `SUB` in one is a blank sixel, `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 §9.1 and §9.3 (Jev, KEEP 0.94/0.94 and BLANK_SIXEL 0.92/0.94; the lean KEEP 0.72/0.68 for `CAN`; `a_body_cut_off_is_placed_from_what_it_received`, `a_sixel_cut_off_is_placed_from_what_it_received`, `a_sub_inside_a_sixel_is_a_blank_sixel`, `a_sixel_cut_off_keeps_its_claim_and_ris_gives_it_back`), and a body of any length draws (`a_body_past_any_old_scratch_draws_its_image`, `a_body_of_any_length_stores_at_the_default_budget`). Until then a body was held whole in a scratch and one past it refused: 256 KiB, at which 19 of the 41 Sixels in nineteen recorded yazi runs were refused, and from 2026-10-14 the owner's 3 MiB interim. **A refused image is counted by the limit it hit**: `ImageStore::refusals` splits `images_refused` into the pixel arena (with the image's whole size, an unprovisioned terminal's empty arena included), the slot table, an image with no pixels and, since 2026-10-11, one wider or taller than the 65535 pixels an image coordinate holds (`an_image_past_65535_pixels_either_way_is_refused_whole`; it tripped a debug assertion and placed a cut image before), and the app logs one `warn` line a pump naming the limit and the `[images]` key that raises it -- `an_image_past_the_arena_is_counted_against_the_arena_with_its_size`, `an_image_that_outgrows_its_claim_is_refused_at_its_whole_size`, `a_full_slot_table_is_counted_against_the_slots`, `a_sixel_with_no_pixels_is_counted_as_empty`; `every_yazi_preview_of_the_walk_stores_at_the_default_budget` (note S). Gates: `baud-core/tests/sixel.rs` (decode and place, chunk invariance, refuse-and-count by cause, text over an image), `baud-core/tests/sixel_stream.rs` (the decode as the body arrives, against the buffered decoder), `baud-core/tests/image_anchor.rs` (every resize and scroll case, asserted by the text beside the image, including foot's blank-line trap and WezTerm's text-on-the-row trap), `baud-render/tests/image.rs` (a pinned frame digest, the one-row scroll, a view scrolled back after a shrink, the per-screen rule) and the pixel oracle. **It follows its row inside the screen too**: a scrolling region, `IL` and `DL` move rows without moving the window, so each moves the images on the rows it moves, and one whose row the operation deletes -- off the top of a region that copies, which is a middle region, or a top-anchored one on the alternate screen with at least as many rows under it as in it, or past a region's bottom -- goes and is counted; a top-anchored region that rotates on the primary, at full width, retires its top line, image and all, to the history; on the alternate screen, which keeps none, the image goes and is counted. **Under side margins narrower than the screen every scroll is a rectangle's** (since 2026-10-12): nothing retires, and an image moves only when its anchor's column is inside the rectangle, and goes and is counted when the scroll takes its row off (`docs/record.md`, "Rectangle scrolling"). A status line under a region keeps its image while the region scrolls, on either screen. **Open**: a Sixel that a margin scroll, `IL` or `DL` moves still goes whole when its anchor leaves the band, its lower rows with it; a kitty placement there is clipped and kept until its last shown row leaves. **An image on a wrapped continuation row** -- placed after text that wrapped onto its row -- follows the cell its row began with, not whichever cell a narrower re-cut put first on the row it landed on, so a narrow-then-widen round trip brings it back to its row and every path of widths ends where one resize from the width it was placed at ends (`an_anchor_on_a_wrapped_row_comes_back_on_its_row_after_a_round_trip`, `an_anchor_on_a_wrapped_row_lands_by_the_width_not_by_the_path`, `an_anchor_on_a_wrapped_row_of_wide_characters_round_trips`). Until 2026-10-11 that round trip brought it back a row higher. A column a resize at a prompt cut off is asked as its row's first cell (`an_anchor_whose_column_a_prompt_resize_cut_off_asks_by_its_rows_first_cell`). **Erasing clears its pixels**, since 2026-10-21: DEC STD 070's B.8.11 leaves the interaction of text erasing and graphics to the device ("application software should not depend on interactions between graphics and text"), and the VT330 and VT340 keep text and graphics in one bitmap, so ED, EL, ECH and DECERA clear a Sixel's and the ReGIS page's pixels in exactly the positions they erase, back to clear, and a Sixel left with no pixel is freed, as text over it frees it (Jev, PIXELS, 0.94/0.91, over xterm's whole-rows ED with no EL or ECH and over freeing the whole image); DECSED, DECSEL and DECSERA clear the positions they erase, the occupied unprotected ones, and keep a protected or unoccupied one's ("Unoccupied character positions are not affected") (Jev's lean, ERASED_POSITIONS, 0.72/0.76, where xterm's DECSERA clears the whole rectangle and its DECSED and DECSEL nothing, `[[source: xterm-screen]]`); ICH, DCH and IRM's gap clear the span they shift, from the cursor to the right margin, as a VT340 was seen to ("Colors are removed from the line when text is shifted left by Esc [ P", `[[source: vt340test-faketextcolor]]`; Jev's lean, CLEAR, 0.72/0.62 on a second ask, where xterm leaves them); IL, DL and a scroll inside a region move the ReGIS page's rows with their lines (Jev, MOVE, 1.00/1.00), as a Sixel moves by its anchor; DECIC and DECDC leave an image where it is (IMAGES_STAY, 2026-10-19); a kitty image is untouched, its text's rule (`each_erase_clears_the_sixel_in_its_positions`, `a_shift_of_characters_clears_the_sixel_from_the_cursor`, `a_selective_erase_clears_only_the_positions_it_erases`, `each_erase_clears_the_page_in_its_positions`, `a_selective_erase_spares_the_page_over_a_protected_position`, `the_lines_a_band_moves_take_the_page_with_them`, `irm_s_gap_clears_the_page_from_the_cursor`, `erm_spares_the_page_over_a_guarded_position`). Under side margins a rectangle scroll leaves the ReGIS page where it is. |
| `CSI ? 80 h/l` | DECSDM, Sixel display mode | done — since 2026-10-15 (note V, V18). **Set turns Sixel scrolling off**, `[[source: ctlseqs]]`'s numbering and the VT382 manuals' it quotes, decided by Jev 2026-10-11 (0.90/0.90) against `[[source: vt3xx-sixel]]`'s "When sixel display mode is set, the Sixel Scrolling feature is enabled", the source conflict on the owner's veto list; `[[source: vt3xx-tp]]`'s Table 12-3 lists DECSDM as mode 80 and gives no direction. Set, a Sixel starts "at the upper-left corner of the active graphics page" -- the window's first cell, whatever DECOM and the margins say, as xterm -- and "the text cursor does not change" (`display_mode_draws_at_the_windows_corner_and_leaves_the_cursor`); "The terminal ignores any commands that attempt to advance the active position below the bottom margin of the graphics page": a `-` whose band would start at or past the screen's bottom edge moves nothing, and rows past the edge are not kept (`display_mode_ignores_a_new_line_past_the_bottom_edge`) -- the literal reading, which Jev did not decide over a `-` that still returns left (0.47 and 0.58) or clipping, so it is on the owner's list; with no cell height known nothing limits it. **And nothing at or past the screen's right edge is drawn**: `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 §11.2.2, "Sixels defined to be printed past the right margin are not printed", so the bands of a tall image that the ignored `-` lays side by side stop at the screen's width, and the image is stored at most the screen's size rather than refused for the arena (`display_mode_draws_nothing_past_the_right_edge`, `display_mode_keeps_a_tall_image_at_the_screens_size`, `display_mode_lays_no_row_out_past_the_right_edge`); with no cell width known nothing limits it. **Recorded only while DA lists Sixel**, the claim and a provisioned store, the condition ctlseqs' Sixel section puts DA's `4`, these three modes and `DCS q` under ("If xterm is configured as VT240 ... it supports Sixel Graphics controls"); otherwise counted in `unsupported_param` and answered `0`, and withdrawing the claim returns all three to power-on (`while_da_does_not_list_sixel_the_modes_are_counted_and_not_kept`, `withdrawing_the_claim_returns_the_modes_to_power_on`). xterm answers DECRQM from its flags whatever its configuration; Jev did not decide between the two (0.61, 0.67). Reset at power-on and by `RIS`, kept by `DECSTR`, as xterm's full reset alone restores it; DECRQM answers `1` or `2`; XTSAVE and XTRESTORE carry it (`decrqm_answers_each_sixel_mode_from_its_state`, `ris_restores_the_sixel_modes_and_decstr_keeps_them`). `CSI 80 h`, ANSI mode 80, which timg sends, is not it (`ansi_mode_80_is_not_decsdm`). |
| `CSI ? 1070 h/l` | private Sixel colour registers | done — since 2026-10-15 (note V, V18). `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "use private color registers for each graphic". **Set at power-on**, xterm's resource default, so each Sixel starts with every register undefined, which is also 070-9 §10's "No Sixel state is preserved from one entering of Sixel mode to the next". Reset, a register a Sixel defines starts the next shared Sixel with that definition; a picture already drawn keeps its pixels, which are colours and not register numbers. That is what ctlseqs states; recolouring drawn pictures, as a VT340's one colour map and xterm do, is the owner's question (Jev, CARRY at 0.90 and 0.88). A private Sixel neither feeds nor clears the shared set, and `RIS` forgets it (`shared_registers_carry_into_the_next_sixel_and_private_ones_do_not`, `a_private_sixel_neither_feeds_nor_clears_the_shared_set`). Recorded under the claim, reset and saved as `?80` is. |
| `CSI ? 8452 h/l` | Sixel cursor to the right of the image | done — since 2026-10-15 (note V, V18). `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "Sixel scrolling leaves cursor to right of graphic, RLogin, xterm" -- the column `ceil(w / cell width)` past where the image began, and past the right margin the left margin of the next row, scrolling at the bottom, as xterm does, decided by Jev (WRAP_NEXT_ROW, 0.94 and 0.92); with no cell width known the column stays (`cursor_right_mode_leaves_the_cursor_past_the_image`). The row is the image's last row, where xterm's `finished_parsing` puts it (`[[source: xterm-graphics-sixel]]`, `new_row = charrow - 1 + ceil(h / FontHeight)`, the column alone changing under the mode); on the right margin itself the cursor stays. Until then it was the row below the image, below and right of the graphic rather than to its right. Recorded under the claim, reset and saved as `?80` is. Note S credited this mode to `[[source: vt3xx-sixel]]`, which has no 8452. |
| `CSI ? 1 ; 1 ; 0 S` | XTSMGRAPHICS, `CSI ? Pi ; Pa ; Pv S`, Sixel colour registers and geometry | done — since 2026-10-15. Answered `CSI ? Pi ; Ps ; Pv S` in xterm's shape (`CASE_GRAPHICS_ATTRIBUTES`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file): **256 registers**, 070-9 §8.1.3's "Devices must implement color numbers 0 to 255", to a read, a reset and a read of the maximum, and a set succeeds only to 256; **the Sixel geometry** reads as the text area in pixels, a failure (`3`) while no cell size is known, and its maximum as `0 ; 0`, ctlseqs' shape for "implementations which do not use the maximum graphics dimensions" (baud limits an image by its arena and a coordinate's range, not a width and height); a geometry set or reset fails; **ReGIS's geometry** (`Pi` 3), since 2026-10-20, reads as the page ReGIS draws on, the text area in pixels or the VT300's 800 by 480 while no cell size is known, its maximum as `0 ; 0` as the Sixel maximum reads (Jev's lean, ZERO, 0.46 and 0.52), and a set or reset fails, the page being the window's; any other item is an error in `Pi` (`1`), an unknown action an error in `Pa` (`2`) (`xtsmgraphics_answers_the_registers_and_the_geometry`). While DA does not list Sixel every success is a failure, ctlseqs' "not configured (runtime) for graphics", but ReGIS's: ReGIS is always on, so baud is configured for it though DA lists no attribute 3 (decided by Jev, ALWAYS, 0.96 and 0.96). **Only the three-parameter form**, "xterm accepts a three-parameter control sequence", decided by Jev (THREE_ONLY, 0.96 and 0.96): another arity is counted in `unsupported` and unanswered (`xtsmgraphics_of_another_arity_is_counted_and_unanswered`). Jev did not decide the Sixel maximum geometry (0.78 and 0.82), so it is the plain reading of the text, on the owner's list. **It does not scroll**, though `CSI Ps S` is SU on the same final byte (`xtsmgraphics_does_not_scroll_and_is_counted`). Counted and unanswered until then. |
| `DCS Pn p ... ST` | ReGIS graphics (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-8) | **partial** — since 2026-10-20, the owner's call, "ReGIS - yes" (TODO.md; note AW, section 7). A program's vectors, circles, curves and filled areas draw on a page over the text area. **Built**: the envelope, where the low bit of `Pn` resynchronises and any other entry resumes where ReGIS was left (070-8 § 8.9.1; `[[source: vt3xx-regis-intro]]`'s `DCS p` to `DCS 3p`); § 8.4's general grammar, keyletters, bracketed extents, quoted strings with their doubled quotes, numbers kept to 1/65536 until whole ("1.02e2 must resolve to 102"), options to six levels and the synchronising `;`; macrographs (§ 8.4.4), 10,000 characters, sixteen levels, a macrograph playing itself refused and counted, a full store failing soft; the position stack of sixteen; **the page**, an image of the text area's own pixels placed at the window's top line, the VT300's 800 by 480 device fitted into it at its upper-left with its shape kept (Jev's lean, TEXT_AREA, 0.53 and 0.34), drawn from a plane of output-map indices through the VT340's sixteen-entry map, a pixel never written clear; `P` and `V` with pixel vectors and the stack, `C`'s circles, arcs either way, circumferential arcs and interpolated open and closed curves (a uniform Catmull-Rom spline, which meets § 8.5.5.4.2.1's four constraints, streamed for any number of points), `F` by the even-odd rule with `F(F ...)` sub-polygons and a 4,096-vertex list, `W`'s overlay, replace, complement and erase writing, negate, the plane mask, the VT300's 8-bit pattern memory and its multiplier, and shading to a line, a column or a point; `S(A)`, `S(E)` filling the page with the background, opaque (Jev's lean, OPAQUE, 0.92 and 0.88), `S(I)` and `S(M)` recolouring what is drawn; text, `T`'s sizes, spacing, direction, italics and the cell's own rotation, in a loaded alphabet (`L`, `L[h,w]` height first, decided by Jev, 0.90 and 0.90) or the built-in one, which is baud's embedded face rasterised by `baud-font` into each unit cell (§ 8.5.6, "must have a built in character definition"), a quoted string's UTF-8 decoded and its character taken as the alphabet's code, one past 15/15 drawn as the error character (Jev's lean, DECODE_TO_TABLE, 0.79 and 0.74); command display, `Pn`'s second bit, the body drawn in a line of its own along the page's foot ("not mandatory", Jev's lean BUILD, confidence 0.38 and 0.28 on a second ask, where BUILD's probability was 0.69 and 0.64, and confidence 0.04 and 0.26 on the first); text the shell writes over the page clearing the page in its cells, the VT340's one bitmap (Jev's lean, CELLS, 0.64 and 0.40); the page scrolling with its lines and the next drawing after a scroll beginning a page at the window's top; and the reports, `R(P)`, `R(P(I))` as a non-interactive device answers it (§ 8.8.5.3), `R(M(=))`, `R(L)` as `(A'<name>')` (§ 8.7.4.1; Jev's lean, 0.34 and 0.16), `R(E)`, and `R(M(x))` answered empty, a carriage return alone, the owner's call (§ 8.5.7.4.1, "The preferred fallback is the empty report"). `S(T)`'s delay, interpretation "suspended" for its ticks, a sixtieth of a second each (§ 8.7.1.4.3): the read stops after the delay, the macrograph it was in stops where it is, and the app feeds the rest and every read behind it on the display link's tick or the `poll` timeout its deadline falls on, nothing sleeping, a delay with more than four mebibytes behind it ended early and counted (an embedder that holds nothing passes each over and counts it, `Term::set_regis_hold`); XTSMGRAPHICS's `Pi` 3 (that row). **Graphics input and the cursors**, since 2026-10-21: a program can ask the user to pick a point on its drawing. `R(P(I))` in one-shot mode (`R(I0)`, the default) shows a crosshair the pointer and the arrow keys move -- a device pixel a key, Shift ten, wrapping at the screen's edge (`[[source: vt3xx-report]]`; Jev, DEVICE_PIXEL, 0.92 and 0.96) -- and holds the program's output (070-13 § 5.1) until a key or a button ends it: any key that sends something sends its own bytes, a button `CSI 241 ~`, `243`, `245` or `247` (`[[source: vt3xx-locator]]` Table 15-1) or what DECLKD loaded (that row), then the position in user coordinates to a tenth, `[102.5,200]`, and a carriage return (Jev, TENTHS, 0.96 and 0.98). Multiple mode (`R(I1)`) answers `R(P(I))` at once behind the null button `CSI 240 ~` and reports each button press, while the keys stay the program's, arrows included (Jev, SEND, 0.96 and 0.96); it works inside an envelope only and resumes at the next `DCS p` where it was left (070-13 § 5.2; Jev's lean, SUSPEND_RESUME, 0.28 and 0.10, the VT340 ending it at `ST`); `R(I0)` and `S(A)` end it, and `DECSTR`, whose list names no graphics state, keeps it (decided by Jev, KEEP, 1.00 and 0.98). A press is the input's, not the selection's, Shift still selecting, and DEC's locator reports after it (070-13 § 5.5). An embedder that holds no read answers one-shot as a non-interactive device, counted; one that holds more than four mebibytes behind a one-shot input gives it up with the empty report, a carriage return alone (§ 8.8.5.3; Jev's lean: EMPTY_REPORT at 0.38, POSITION_REPORT at 0.22 in the other order, EMPTY_REPORT having the higher probability averaged over the two orders, 0.495 against 0.37). `S(C ...)`: the input cursor a crosshair by default, as 070-13 § 5.1 has it, "a cross hair cursor appears", numbered as the VT330/VT340 manual numbers it (`[[source: vt3xx-screen-control]]`): `I0`, `I2` and an omitted number the crosshair, `I1` the diamond, `I3` and `I4` rubber bands anchored at the drawing position, a valid `S(A)` selecting the crosshair (Jev's lean, VT340, 0.59 and 0.67 after a re-ask); the output cursor, the diamond or `S(C(H2))` the crosshair at the drawing position, shown whenever a read ends inside an envelope and gone at `ST`, or at the end of the read that cancels the envelope with `CAN`, `SUB` or a stray `ESC`, `S(C0)` turning it off; a user-defined pair of characters from the drawing alphabet with its offset, `S(C(I[+x,+y]"XO"))`. A standard cursor is drawn in map entry 0 with a one-pixel border in entry 15, a pair in entries 14 and 1, as the VT340 colours them (Jev, TWO_TONE, 1.00 and 1.00), into the page shown and never into its plane, so nothing drawn under a cursor is changed by it. The indicator line shows the input position while input runs (070-14 p. 14-6). **Open departure, on the owner's veto list**: the input cursor's numbers are the VT340's, against 070-8 § 8.8.1.4.2 b, whose selection syntax "has the force of a standard": "A value of zero or 1 for <n> selects a the diamond cursor ... This too is the default cursor, and is selected after a valid S(A) has been executed", so `I0`, an omitted number and `S(A)` select the crosshair where 070-8 selects the diamond, and `S(A)` differs from its written equivalent in § 8.8.1.4.1 g, "S(C(H1)(I1)1)", which selects the diamond; taken as Jev's lean below the bar. **Open departure, on the owner's veto list**: DA lists no attribute `3` though ReGIS is built, against 070-4's DA, "The rest of the parameters indicate the architectural extensions supported at the current operating level" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]`, PDF 221), its Table 1 registering "3 1-4 ReGIS Display" (PDF 223), kept out by the owner's pick of 2026-10-15, "Keep ?6c (Recommended)" (the `CSI c` row). **The clip rectangle**, since 2026-10-21: a program can fence its drawing in, or out. `S(R<n>[][])` limits every drawing to the rectangle's interior, its boundary included, for `n` above zero, and to its exterior for `n` below zero, "shielding", where "the boundary is NOT part of the exterior"; `S(R0)` turns it off and keeps the extent, and an extent alone keeps the switch; one corner leaves the extent and more than two use the last two, a relative part measured from the corner before and the first corner's from the drawing position, "multiple specifiers have cumulative action" (§ 8.4.5.1; Jev's lean, CUMULATIVE, 0.52 and 0.48 on a second ask); `S(E)` erases the whole page whatever the clip and erase writing stays inside it; a valid `S(A)` sets the extent to its own and turns clipping off; the command display's line is the terminal's and is not clipped (070-8 § 8.8.1.4.6, § 8.8.1.4.1 d; `a_clip_rectangle_limits_drawing_to_its_inside_and_boundary`, `shielding_draws_outside_and_not_on_the_boundary`, `the_extent_and_the_switch_are_set_apart`, `relative_corners_accumulate_from_the_drawing_position`, `screen_erase_ignores_the_clip_and_erase_writing_keeps_it`, `a_valid_s_a_restores_the_default_clip`, `the_clip_is_not_counted_and_does_not_clip_the_command_display`). **Line width**, since 2026-10-21: `W(L<n>)` draws lines and curves `n` times the nominal width, today's one page pixel, as a round pen swept along the line (Jev's lean, ROUND, 0.25 and 0.19 on a second ask), a fraction to the nearest pixel as a coordinate rounds, `W(L0)` the minimum, a pixel, and no width too wide, "W(L3) three times, and so on" (decided by Jev, UNBOUNDED, 0.96 and 0.96): the width field's 65,535 pixels is past twice the diagonal of any page up to 23,170 pixels a side, and the pen costs a step its rows on the page and a line at most one whole pen held to the page; each pixel of a stroke, a polyline or a curve, is written once, so complement writing turns it once; a valid `S(A)` sets it back to nominal (070-8 § 8.8.3.2, § 8.8.1.4.1 b; `a_wide_line_is_a_round_pen_swept_along_it`, `nominal_minimum_and_fractions`, `a_width_has_no_limit_short_of_the_page`, `a_width_changed_inside_a_stroke_draws_from_the_next_step`, `a_patterned_wide_line_is_the_pen_at_each_step`, `a_wide_stroke_complements_each_pixel_once`, `a_new_stroke_writes_over_the_last`, `the_width_resets_at_s_a_and_a_temporary_one_ends_with_its_command`, `moved_rows_keep_no_marks`). **The screen scroll**, since 2026-10-21: a program can slide its picture, as a strip chart does. Under the scrolling model the VT340 has (070-8 § 8.8.1 b), `S[x,y]` makes the address it names the screen's upper-left and `S<pv>` moves it a pixel vector step at the `W(M)` or `S(W(M))` multiplier, each digit a scroll (§ 8.8.1.2, § 8.8.1.4.8): the page's pixels and the indices they are drawn from move together, every plane, the clip holding nothing back (§ 8.8.1.4.6 e), the lit count recounted, and the drawing position and the coordinates stay ("Only the data is offset, not the coordinate system", `[[source: vt3xx-screen-control]]`); a relative or omitted part is measured from the origin, `S(A)`'s first corner, not the drawing position (Jev's lean, FROM_ORIGIN, 0.86 and 0.88 on a second ask), and what the move reveals is the background, opaque, "moved off and erased from the display medium" (Jev's lean, BACKGROUND, 0.48 and 0.64 on a second ask). **On the owner's veto list**: the text does not move with it, baud's text being cells beneath the page where the VT340 keeps one bitmap; and the revealed band is opaque over the text (`a_scroll_moves_the_page_and_reveals_the_background`, `a_scroll_is_measured_from_the_origin`, `a_pixel_vector_scrolls_a_step_at_the_multiplier`, `a_scroll_moves_the_indices_keeps_the_count_and_ignores_the_clip`). **The display's scale**, since 2026-10-21: a program can magnify its picture. `S(S<n>)` shows each pixel `n` by `n`, `S(S(X<n>)(Y<n>))` each way apart, from the screen's upper-left (Jev's lean, UPPER_LEFT, 0.88 and 0.86 on a second ask), whole factors to sixteen, the limit a larger one is mapped to (Jev's lean, LIMIT_16, 0.72 and 0.80 on a second ask), zero and negatives changing nothing; the bitmap is kept, so what is drawn while magnified is drawn on it, a scroll moves it through the window, "S(S) and S[] then combine to move a small window through a large display space", and `S(S1)` or a valid `S(A)` shows it again (070-8 § 8.8.1.4.7, § 8.8.1.4.1 h). Text over a magnified page and the erases clear the bitmap's pixels shown in their cells, a whole block each; a line's move and the window's move take the bitmap's rows nearest the line's height; the pointer names the bitmap's pixel under it; the cursors keep their size; the command display stays on the screen's bottom line, its characters the size they are unmagnified, "Other ReGIS-settable states have no effect", drawn over the page shown as the cursors are and recoloured with the map, and a line feed erases the bitmap's pixels shown on the line, a whole block each, so at a scale that does not divide the line's rows it erases up to a block row above it (Jev's lean, OVERLAY, 0.88 and 0.76 on a second ask) (`magnified_the_command_display_stays_on_the_bottom_line`, `magnified_a_line_feed_erases_what_the_bottom_line_shows`, `magnified_a_line_feed_clears_the_line_s_characters`, `the_magnified_line_stays_over_later_drawing_and_goes_with_the_scale`, `scaling_magnifies_from_the_upper_left_and_s_s1_shows_the_bitmap_again`, `drawing_and_scrolling_while_magnified_work_on_the_bitmap`, `a_drawing_past_the_magnified_window_stops_at_the_edge`, `the_limit_the_refusals_and_s_a`, `a_magnified_page_stays_one_with_its_bitmap`, `a_magnified_page_moves_with_its_lines`, `the_input_position_is_the_bitmap_s`). **Hard copy**, since 2026-10-21: `S(H)` prints the visible image to the printer as a sixel dump (§ 8.8.1.4.4, "Sixels are the implementation choice in video terminals"; 070-7 § 7.8), the whole screen, the rectangle between two positions or between one and the drawing position, `S(H(P[x,y]))`'s offset kept until the next or a valid `S(A)`, `[0,0]` at power-up (§ 8.8.1.4.4; the VT330/VT340's `[50,0]` is that device's). Magnified by `S(S)`, the visible image is the page as it is shown, and a region's positions name the plane pixels it shows as blocks (`a_magnified_page_prints_as_it_is_shown`). **The texts disagree on a signed corner**: § 8.4.5.2 item 6 says H's brackets "ARE positional ... and may be expressed in terms of the current position", and § 8.4.5.1 makes multiple specifiers cumulative, which is built, the second corner relative to the first (Jev, CUMULATIVE, 0.97/0.97); the VT330/VT340 manual says relative values are "relative to the cursor position, not to any other position specified" (`[[source: vt3xx-gp-002]]` p. 29), and § 8.8.1.4.4 calls `S(H[][])` "a non-positional use of bracketed extents ... always interpreted absolutely". Both are departures. The offset is held to 17 inches of the dump's pixels each way, 2040 at grid 6 and 1360 at grid 9, the text giving it no range and its relationship to the coordinate space "implementation- and retransmission protocol- dependent" (§ 8.4.5.2 item 6; Jev's lean, CLAMP_SHEET, 0.25/0.35 after a re-ask; `a_large_offset_is_held_to_a_sheet`). **The visible image is text and pictures** (Jev, VISIBLE_IMAGE, 0.98/0.98), as the VT340's one bit map is and its own dump shows (`[[source: vt340test-background-select-2-png]]`): each cell's background, its character drawn by the face the built-in alphabet uses at the cell's size, an underline along its last pixel row, then the Sixel images and the page in drawing order; reverse video swaps, an invisible character is not drawn. **Level 2** (070-7 § 7.8.2.4; Jev's lean, LEVEL_2, 0.78/0.94): SSU `ESC [ 2 SP I`, `DCS 0 ; Ps2 ; Pn3 q`, raster attributes at 1:1, their width and height, "number of pixels in image horizontally" and "vertically", counting the offset's blank pixels as well as the picture's, so a printer reading the extent takes the offset as part of the image, no `CR` first, `ST`, an `FF` after under DECPFF; black and white by default, every pixel with a bit set in any plane inked, a ReGIS pixel written with index 0 printing nothing and covering the text under it as it does on the screen (070-7 § 7.8.2.1, "a logical OR of all pixels which are selected for any color"; `index_zero_prints_no_ink_and_covers_the_text`), or the registers named in HLS or RGB with the background as register 0 when it is printed, past 255 colours a fixed 6 by 7 by 6 cube (Jev's lean, EXACT_ELSE_CUBE, 0.24/0.66); grid 6 or 9 decipoints by DECGEPM, turned counter-clockwise when rotated (the `?43` row). With no printer it is counted, and one the print area has no working room for prints nothing and is counted (`Term::print_refusals`). The app's printer reads the dump into a picture on its own line at the grid's size, shrunk to fit the sheet; a job's pictures hold at most 2^25 pixels together, a larger one reduced to fit and one with none left left out, each counted and named in the panel's title (Jev's lean, SHRINK, 0.24/0.72 after a re-ask; `a_picture_past_the_budget_is_reduced_and_the_title_says_so`) (`s_h_prints_the_screen_as_a_level_two_dump`, `a_region_prints_between_its_corners`, `pictures_print_over_the_text`, `offset_size_rotation_syntax_and_background`, `no_printer_form_feed_and_no_room`, `reverse_and_invisible`, `any_picture_round_trips`, `a_hard_copy_reaches_the_panel_as_a_picture`). **Microcoded writing**, since 2026-10-21: a program can say what each colour becomes where it draws. `W(W<i>(P<j>N<k>))` writes a pixel holding index `i` to `j` where the pattern, the area pattern or the character cell has a one and to `k` where it has a zero; only the entries loaded take effect, the rest writing as the writing mode does, and any writing mode, `V`, `R`, `C`, `E` or `N`, clears them, as a valid `S(A)` does (070-8 § 8.8.3.4). The bit is the pattern's own, before `W(N1)` inverts it (Jev's lean, RAW, 0.20 and 0.36 on a second ask), and the plane mask still limits the write (decided by Jev, MASK_APPLIES, 0.96 and 0.92) (`w_w_writes_its_loaded_entries_and_the_mode_the_rest`, `a_writing_mode_command_clears_the_entries`, `the_bit_is_the_pattern_s_own_before_negate`, `the_plane_mask_limits_a_microcoded_write`, `a_valid_s_a_resets_the_entries_and_a_temporary_one_ends_with_its_command`, `an_entry_over_index_zero_writes_a_clear_pixel_too`). **Flood**, since 2026-10-21: a program can colour in an area already drawn, whatever drew its edges. `F[x,y]` and `F<pv>` flood from a seed, each seed in turn, a relative one measured from the seed before it and the first from the drawing position, which no flood moves (070-8 § 8.8.6.1; § 8.4.5.1's "cumulative action", Jev's lean, CUMULATIVE, 0.42 and 0.46 on a second ask); the flood spreads side by side and never corner to corner, "A boundary need only be connected by diagonally adjacent pixels", leaves the boundary as it is, and is written in the writing mode and foreground in force with the last shading snapshot, "even if shading has been subsequently disabled" (§ 8.8.6.4), each pixel once. `F(B(F1))`, the default and restored by a valid `S(A)`, spreads over the seed's index, § 8.8.6.4's second way, "any change in attribute with respect to the seed location attribute value" (Jev's lean, SEED_ONLY, 0.56, then SEED_OR_BACKGROUND at 0.08 in the other order, SEED_ONLY the more probable averaged over the two, 0.62 against 0.38); **an open departure, on the owner's veto list**: § 8.8.6.3.1.1 bounds it "by any foreground value different from the seed value, or any foreground if the seed location has the background attribute", which leaves the background open from a foreground seed, and SEED_ONLY follows § 8.8.6.4 against that sentence; `F(B(F0))` over the seed's state, foreground or background, § 8.8.6.4's "any change between the foreground and the background state" (Jev's lean, SAME_STATE, 0.74 and 0.38 on a second ask); `F(B(I<n>))` and `F(B(I(<colour>)))` up to that index; a pixel never written holds index 0, as complement reads it. The clip limits what a flood writes, not where it spreads (Jev's lean, WRITES_ONLY, 0.62 and 0.74 on a second ask) (`a_flood_fills_the_closed_area_and_not_its_boundary`, `a_diagonal_boundary_closes_the_flood`, `f1_floods_the_seed_s_index_and_f0_the_seed_s_state`, `f_b_i_floods_up_to_the_named_index`, `a_flood_writes_each_pixel_once`, `the_texture_is_the_last_shading_snapshot`, `the_clip_limits_what_a_flood_writes_not_where_it_spreads`, `flood_seeds_accumulate_and_the_position_stays`, `a_stack_of_one_floods_what_a_full_stack_floods`). **Blink**, since 2026-10-21: a program can make what it draws blink. `W(A1)` sets the blink attribute on the foreground data drawn after it, a one of the pattern, cell or texture under `V`, `R` and `C`, every pixel under `E` with `N1`, a microcoded one, while a background write and any write without blink clear a pixel's (decided by Jev, FOREGROUND_WRITES, 1.00 and 1.00); `W(A0)`, `S(E)` and a valid `S(A)` end it and what is drawn keeps blinking (070-8 § 8.8.3.1). A blinking pixel shows its own colour and then the background index's, opaque over the text as `S(E)` is (decided by Jev, BACKGROUND, 0.90 and 0.92 on a second ask), at SGR 5's rate on the display link, held shown under Reduce Motion (decided by Jev, SGR5_RATE, 0.98 and 0.98 on a second ask), never a sleep; the embedder flips it (`Term::set_regis_blink`), both apps with text blink's slow phase, the Linux app waking at its flips while the page shown keeps blinking pixels (`Term::regis_blink`; `a_blinking_regis_page_wakes_at_the_slow_flips`). A page the next drawing leaves behind keeps its pixels in their own colours, and a hard copy prints them so in either phase (`a_blinking_drawing_shows_the_background_in_the_hidden_phase`, `only_foreground_data_blinks_and_any_other_write_clears_it`, `w_a0_s_e_and_a_valid_s_a_end_the_attribute_and_drawn_pixels_keep_it`, `a_page_with_no_blinking_pixel_left_stops_repainting`, `a_page_left_behind_keeps_its_own_colours`, `a_blinking_flood_and_a_blinking_character_blink`, `a_blinking_pixel_prints_its_own_colour_when_hidden`, `the_tick_flips_regis_blink_with_the_slow_phase`). **Drawing surfaces**, since 2026-10-21: a program can draw a second picture out of sight and show it at once. `P(P1)` draws on the VT340's second page of graphics memory and `P(P0)` on the first, `S(P1)` displays the second and `S(P0)` the first, other values ignored, "All drawing surfaces share the same state store" (070-8 § 8.8.2.1; `[[source: vt3xx-position]]`, `[[source: vt3xx-screen-control]]`); the surface drawn on is kept from one envelope to the next and is the first after a reset, `S(E)` erases it (decided by Jev, DRAWING_SURFACE, 0.90 and 0.90 on a second ask), and the cursors show only while it is displayed. The second is the screen's: the shell's text, its erases and its scrolling act on the first, the text page it is coupled to, and never reach it (decided by Jev, SCREEN_FIXED, 1.00 and 0.98 on a second ask). Each envelope's entry and its `ST` display the first again, and a cancelled envelope's end does at the read's end, the VT340's "When you exit ReGIS, the displayed text page becomes the same as the active text page (if the Page Coupling feature ... is enabled)", coupling the VT420's default (`[[source: vt420-uu-ch9]]`; Jev's lean, COUPLED, 0.94 and 0.86 on a second ask). A hard copy prints the surface displayed, a page a resize leaves behind shows the first, and text or an erase after an envelope left on the second, in the same read, clears only its own cells of the first. **On the owner's veto list**: the second surface is shown over the shell's text, baud's text having one page where the VT340 shows the second page's own (`the_second_surface_shows_only_while_it_is_displayed`, `each_surface_keeps_its_own_picture`, `the_drawing_surface_persists_and_each_entry_displays_the_first`, `s_e_erases_the_drawing_surface`, `the_second_surface_is_the_screen_s_and_the_text_scrolls_the_first`, `the_cursors_show_on_the_displayed_surface_only`, `a_reset_forgets_the_second_surface`, `a_hard_copy_prints_the_surface_displayed`, `a_resize_while_the_second_is_displayed_keeps_the_first`, `text_after_st_on_the_second_keeps_the_first`, `text_after_a_cancel_on_the_second_keeps_the_first`, `an_erase_after_st_on_the_second_keeps_the_first`, `text_after_an_escape_that_leaves_the_second_keeps_the_first`, `an_erase_of_all_of_the_first_frees_the_page_once_the_first_shows`). **Data movement** is the scrolling model, the VT340's (070-8 § 8.8.1 b, above), and baud has no other: "Neither model is required", and "For devices supporting both data movement models, the D option switches between them" (§ 8.8.1.4.3), so `S(D1)` names the model in force and `S(D0)`, the windowing model, is parsed and counted (Jev's lean, SCROLLING_ONLY, 0.70 and 0.54 on a second ask, against BUILD_BOTH; **on the owner's veto list**, the standing rule quoted to Jev) (`s_d1_names_the_scrolling_model_and_s_d0_is_counted`). **Not built yet**: kitty's images in a hard copy, which take three layers against the text, above it, under its characters and under its backgrounds, and blend by their alpha, where the dump reads one picture's pixel over the text; the windowing model; and `S(N)`, which no pinned DEC text defines, each parsed and counted (`Term::regis_counts`) (`a_bare_p_is_regis_and_answers_its_reports`, `a_dollar_or_plus_p_is_not_regis`, `the_interpreter_resumes_in_the_next_envelope`, `a_body_split_anywhere_answers_the_same`, `a_macrograph_report_is_empty_whatever_it_holds`, `a_vector_draws_its_pixels_in_the_foreground_and_nothing_else`, `complement_twice_is_nothing_and_a_joint_is_complemented_once`, `text_written_over_the_page_clears_its_cells_and_no_more`, `a_built_in_character_is_drawn_by_the_font_at_its_unit_cell`, `without_a_font_a_built_in_character_is_counted`, `command_display_shows_the_commands_on_the_bottom_line`, `a_delay_holds_the_rest_of_the_read_until_it_is_fed`, `a_delay_in_a_macrograph_stops_it_and_the_next_byte_plays_on`, `a_delay_holds_the_reads_behind_it_until_its_deadline`, `a_one_shot_report_waits_for_a_key_and_holds_the_read`, `arrow_keys_step_the_cursor_and_wrap_at_the_edge`, `a_button_ends_one_shot_with_its_string`, `multiple_mode_works_inside_the_envelope_and_resumes_on_reentry`, `the_default_input_cursor_is_a_two_tone_crosshair`, `s_c_i1_selects_the_diamond_and_i0_i2_and_omitted_the_crosshair`, `a_valid_s_a_selects_the_crosshair_and_s_c_i1_the_diamond`, `the_cursor_is_not_drawn_into_the_plane`, `the_output_cursor_shows_while_the_envelope_waits`, `a_cancelled_envelope_takes_its_cursors_away`, `a_one_shot_input_holds_the_reads_until_a_key_ends_it`, `a_regis_input_cursor_is_drawn_and_taken_away`) Erasing, shifting characters and moving lines clear or move the page's pixels as the Sixel row says, since 2026-10-21. **A page with nothing left lit is freed**, its plane and memory with it, since 2026-10-21, as a Sixel with no pixel left is; the store keeps the page's lit count through every write, so no erase reads the page again (`a_page_with_nothing_lit_is_freed`, `the_page_s_lit_count_is_kept_exactly`). **Complement writing reads what a scroll left on the screen**, since 2026-10-21: "complement the contents of the pixels present in the image" (070-8 § 8.7.2.4.1.1), so when the window has moved down the next drawing's page takes the old page's lines still on the screen and the indices they are drawn from, and the old page keeps only its lines in the history (`complement_reads_the_page_that_scrolled_under_it`, `a_page_scrolled_wholly_away_carries_nothing`) |
| `CSI ? 2004 h/l` | bracketed paste | done — recorded and exposed as `Term::bracketed_paste()` |
| `CSI ? 1004 h/l` | focus reporting | done — `Term::focus` queues `CSI I` on gain and `CSI O` on loss only while the mode is set, and nothing otherwise, because an application that did not ask reads `CSI I` as a keypress. The macOS app polls the window's key status on the tick and before a mouse event and forwards a change (`Driver::poll_focus`), because the window crate's hook reports the *view's* first-responder status, which fired once at `show()` before the driver was installed and never on an app switch -- so **no transition was ever reported on macOS until 2026-09-20**, found by the OSC 8 live check, and a live check reads `CSI I`, `CSI O`, `CSI I` around an app switch now. The Linux app calls it from its keyboard hook. **Dropped uncounted until 2026-09-20**: `1004` was named nowhere in the code and had no row here, while `vim-unicode.raw` sends it on its first line; `set_private_mode`'s wildcard counts now, as the ANSI table's always did. `gain_sends_csi_i_and_loss_sends_csi_o`, `nothing_is_sent_unless_the_application_asked`, `the_vim_recording_enables_it` |
| `CSI ? 9 h/l` | X10 compatibility mouse, a tracking level | done — since 2026-10-09. A press of button 1, 2 or 3 and nothing else: no release, no motion, no wheel and no modifiers (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`, "X10 compatibility mode": "only on button press ... Cb is button-1, where button is 1, 2 or 3"). The encoding in force still applies, as xterm's `EmitButtonCode` writes it (`[[source: xterm-button]]`), so `?9` with `?1006` is `CSI < 0 ; x ; y M`. A level beside `?1000`-`?1003`, and a reset of any of the five turns tracking off (xterm's `really_set_mousemode`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file); `RIS` clears it and `DECSTR` keeps it, as for the others. It was counted in `unsupported_param` and dropped before. The Linux app sends it too, since 2026-10-20, from `wl_pointer` (`seat.rs`). `x10_compatibility_reports_a_press_and_the_button_alone`, `x10_compatibility_sends_no_release_no_motion_and_no_wheel`, `x10_compatibility_takes_the_encoding_in_force`, `any_tracking_reset_turns_x10_compatibility_off_and_on_is_a_level`, `ris_clears_x10_compatibility_and_the_encoding_and_decstr_keeps_them` |
| `CSI ? 1000/1002/1003 h/l` | mouse tracking | done — a level; any reset turns it off. No level reports a wheel release, "Release events for the wheel buttons are not reported" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`, `wheel_releases_are_not_reported`). **Since 2026-10-21 the wheel across is reported too**, a tilted wheel or a sideways swipe: button 6 toward the left and 7 toward the right, "encoded like the wheel mice, by adding 64 (for buttons 6 and 7)", 66 and 67 in every encoding, a press a column of the cell width and no release (Jev's lean, PRESS_ONLY, 0.58/0.64, where xterm reports their release and neovim reads one as a bare `<Release>`); `?9` reports neither. Shift with a mouse wheel, which macOS gives as a sideways scroll, takes Shift's override as the vertical wheel does (the `CSI > 1 s` row), so it reaches the program only under XTSHIFTESCAPE 1. ctlseqs' "button 7 (left) and button 6 (right) with a Logitech mouse" is that mouse's own numbering; the X convention and foot send 6 for the left (`[[source: foot-input]]`). Each event scrolls along its larger delta alone, as AppKit's scroll views do by default (`[[source: apple-nsscrollview-usespredominantaxisscrolling]]`; Jev, PREDOMINANT, 0.92/0.96), so a vertical swipe sends no stray 6 or 7 (`buttons_6_and_7_are_encoded_like_the_wheel`, `the_wheel_across_is_buttons_6_and_7_and_nothing_untracked`, `the_wheel_across_reports_buttons_6_and_7_under_tracking`, `each_event_scrolls_along_its_larger_delta`). **Motion is reported per cell**, as ctlseqs' button-event text has it: "Motion events are reported only if the mouse pointer has moved to a different character cell". `Term::mouse_report` keeps the cell of the last report that went out -- a press or release as well as a move, as xterm's `EditorButton` sets `mouse_row`/`mouse_col` on every report (`[[source: xterm-button]]`) -- and drops a move into it, comparing the position and not the button or modifiers; a refused or unqueued report does not move it, and a tracking or encoding change and both resets forget it, where xterm forgets only at `VTInitialize` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file; practice). Presses and releases are never held back. Until 2026-10-09 it kept no last position and the macOS app's `Driver::mouse` hands it every AppKit move and drag, so a pointer inside one cell sent one identical report per event. The Linux app reports at every level since 2026-10-20, from `wl_pointer`'s frames (`a_tracking_child_gets_the_buttons_and_the_wheel`). `motion_inside_one_cell_is_reported_once`, `a_drag_inside_one_cell_is_reported_once`, `a_modifier_change_inside_the_cell_is_not_a_move`, `a_press_or_release_is_always_sent_and_moves_the_cell`, `a_refused_motion_does_not_move_the_cell`, `a_mode_change_or_a_reset_forgets_the_cell` |
| `CSI ? 1005 h/l` | UTF-8 mouse encoding | done — since 2026-10-09. X10's bytes, each UTF-8 encoded past 127: one byte below one-based 96, two from 96 to 2015 (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "expanding the maximum encodable position from 223 to 2015"), and past 2015 refused and counted in `mouse_unencodable`, where xterm clamps and sends a `0` byte (`EmitMousePosition`, `[[source: xterm-button]]`). **The same bytes under `ESC % @`**: the mode is the encoding, ctlseqs names the cost ("will not pass through luit(1) unchanged") rather than a fallback, and xterm's emit path reads no encoding. Counted and dropped before. The Linux app reports in it too since 2026-10-20. `utf8_encoding_is_x10_below_ninety_six_and_two_bytes_from_it`, `utf8_encoding_reaches_2015_and_refuses_past_it`, `utf8_encoding_ignores_the_input_encoding`, `utf8_mouse_reports_are_x10_until_127_and_utf8_after` |
| `CSI ? 1006 h/l` | SGR mouse encoding | done — no ordinate limit. **A set replaces the encoding in force and a reset undoes only its own**, xterm's rule for all four ("a reset is only effective against the matching mode", `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file); ctlseqs makes them "mutually exclusive" and says no more. `?1006 l` returned to X10 whatever was set until 2026-10-09. The Linux app reports in it too since 2026-10-20. `setting_an_encoding_replaces_the_one_in_force`, `resetting_an_encoding_undoes_only_that_encoding`, `a_set_selects_an_encoding_and_a_reset_undoes_only_its_own` |
| `CSI ? 1007 h/l` | alternate scroll | done — since 2026-10-09, `Term::alternate_scroll()`, read by the macOS app's wheel (`wheel::route`): on the alternate screen with no tracking, set sends the cursor keys through `Term::key`, so in DECCKM's form, and reset sends nothing (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`, "Wheel mice": "if Alternate Scroll mode is set, then cursor up/down controls are sent when the terminal is displaying the Alternate Screen Buffer"). **Set at power-on**, which is what baud did before it read the mode; xterm's `alternateScroll` resource defaults to `False` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file), and ctlseqs leaves the initial state to the resource -- baud's default differs from xterm's, the owner's call. Kept by `RIS` and `DECSTR`, as xterm keeps it: no `ReallyReset` path names it. Tracking takes the wheel whatever it says. **The wheel across sends no keys**: ctlseqs gives Alternate Scroll "cursor up/down controls" alone, and with no tracking a sideways step does nothing on either screen, as in xterm and foot, where alacritty sends cursor left and right (`[[source: alacritty-0151-input-mod]]`; Jev's lean, NOTHING, 0.86/0.92). The Linux app's wheel reads it too since 2026-10-20 (`the_alternate_screens_wheel_is_the_cursor_keys`). `after_1007l_the_wheel_on_the_alternate_screen_sends_nothing`, `the_alternate_screen_sends_arrows_until_an_application_says_otherwise`, `tracking_and_the_primary_screen_ignore_1007`, `the_wheels_keys_are_the_cursor_keys_as_decckm_has_them`, `alternate_scroll_survives_both_resets` |
| `CSI ? 1015 h/l` | urxvt mouse encoding | done — since 2026-10-09. `CSI Cb ; Cx ; Cy M`: X10's button value, its `+ 32` and its release code kept, in decimal, and `M` for a release too (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "the same button encoding as X10, but printing it as a decimal integer rather than as a single byte"). herdr and zellij send it before `?1006`, which replaces it. Counted and dropped before. The Linux app reports in it too since 2026-10-20. `urxvt_is_the_x10_button_value_in_decimal`, `urxvt_mouse_reports_have_the_documented_shape`, `herdrs_start_ends_in_sgr_any_event_and_nothing_is_counted` |
| `CSI ? 1016 h/l` | SGR-Pixels mouse encoding | done — since 2026-10-09. `?1006`'s shape with the position in pixels (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "report position in pixels rather than character cells"), one-based on the wire as xterm writes it (`value + 1`, `[[source: xterm-button]]`). The pixel is the event's `PointerPx`: zero-based from the text area's first pixel, in the device pixels `CSI 16 t` and `?2048` answer in; the macOS app derives it and the cell in one function and clamps it to the text area, where xterm reports the border as `0` or less. An event with no pixel, or at `u16::MAX` where `ImagePx` saturates, is refused and counted, but for `?1001`'s short release report, which writes the region's one cell in cells and no mouse position (the `?1001` row). **Motion is gated by the cell, not the pixel**: button-event tracking's "moved to a different character cell" names no encoding and `?1016` changes only the format; xterm reports a move per pixel here, its row and column holding pixels in `SET_PIXEL_POSITION_MOUSE` (`[[source: xterm-button]]`), a practice baud departs from and the owner's call. Counted and dropped before. The core takes the pixel from whichever embedder puts one on `MouseEvent::px`: the macOS app, and since 2026-10-20 the Linux app, whose surface coordinates are its pixels with no buffer scale set and whose grid starts at the frame's first pixel. `sgr_pixels_motion_is_gated_by_the_cell_as_the_text_says`, `sgr_pixels_reports_the_pointer_in_pixels`, `sgr_pixels_refuses_an_event_with_no_pixel_position`, `sgr_pixel_mouse_reports_carry_the_pixel_one_based`, `a_point_names_its_pixel_and_the_cell_that_holds_it`, `a_wheel_under_1016_reports_the_pointer_in_pixels` |
| X10 mouse encoding (`ESC [ M Cb Cx Cy`) | the default, what tracking gives with no encoding mode set | done — positions past 223 refused and counted, not truncated. **A release is code 3** since 2026-10-09, ctlseqs' "3=release": it carried the released button, so under plain `?1000` a left release went out byte for byte as a left press. `an_x10_release_says_release_not_which_button`, `x10_family_releases_are_code_three_and_sgr_keeps_the_button` |
| `CSI ? 1016 $ p` — DECRQM on the mouse modes, `?9`, `?1000`-`?1003`, `?1005`-`?1007`, `?1015`, `?1016` | DECRQM | done — every tracking level and every encoding answers `1` only while it is the one in force, and `?1007` its flag, as xterm's `do_dec_rqm` answers them (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`); `?9`, `?1005`, `?1007`, `?1015` and `?1016` answered `0`, not recognised, before. The answer is the core's state, so the Linux app gives the same answers, and since 2026-10-20 sends the reports they name. `decrqm_answers_every_mouse_mode`, `the_mouse_modes_land_the_same_at_every_read_split` |
| `CSI ? 1001 h/l` | highlight tracking | done — a tracking level, the handshake, the drawn region and the release reports; see the mode table. The "xterm will hang" warning describes an event loop, and `baud-core` has none: an event before the program's answer is normal tracking's. `under_sgr_the_reports_are_decimal`, `a_release_before_the_answer_is_normal_and_a_late_answer_is_ignored` |
| `CSI ? 1042 h/l` | bell urgency, xterm's `bellIsUrgent` | done — since 2026-10-14. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "Enable Urgency window manager hint when Control-G is received, xterm. This enables the bellIsUrgent resource", and the reset disables it. `Term::bell_is_urgent()`, read by the macOS app's bell: a `BEL` while the window is not focused asks for the user's attention, `requestUserAttention` of the informational kind: "The dock icon will bounce for one second" (`[[source: apple-nsapplication-informationalrequest]]`), and to an application already active it "has no effect" (`[[source: apple-nsapplication-requestuserattention]]`) (`BEL` above). xterm likewise sets the hint only while unfocused (`setXUrgency`, `[[source: xterm-misc]]`). **Reset at power-on**, the resource's default (`Bres(XtNbellIsUrgent, ..., False)`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file; `[[source: xterm-man]]`); **reset by `RIS`**, ECMA-48 § 8.3.105's "set the modes into the reset state" (`[[source: ecma48]]`), where xterm keeps it, no `ReallyReset` path naming it -- a departure not followed; kept by `DECSTR`, whose list does not name it. DECRQM answers `1` or `2`, as xterm's `MdBool(screen->bellIsUrgent)`; XTSAVE and XTRESTORE carry it. The config's `bell = "none"` does not silence it. Before, it was counted as unsupported and DECRQM answered `0`. Since 2026-10-21 the Linux app asks the compositor to activate the window through `xdg-activation-v1`, with a token of its own, as foot marks a window urgent; the compositor decides whether it comes forward or is marked for attention (`BEL` above; `the_window_is_activated_with_a_token_it_asks_for_or_is_given`). `each_mode_sets_and_resets_alone`, `ris_resets_both_and_decstr_keeps_them`, `xtsave_and_xtrestore_carry_both`, `the_bell_modes_land_the_same_at_every_read_split`, `an_unfocused_window_does_what_the_modes_say` |
| `CSI ? 1043 h/l` | raise on bell, xterm's `popOnBell` | done — since 2026-10-14. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "Enable raising of the window when Control-G is received, xterm. This enables the popOnBell resource", and the reset disables it. `Term::pop_on_bell()`, read by the macOS app's bell: a `BEL` while the window is not focused orders it front with `orderFrontRegardless`, "without changing either the key window or the main window" (`[[source: apple-nswindow-orderfrontregardless]]`), as xterm's `XRaiseWindow` restacks without focus (`Bell`, `[[source: xterm-misc]]`); a minimized window is left alone, "If the window is iconified, this has no effect" (`[[source: xterm-man]]`). A focused window is not raised, the owner's "when unfocused"; xterm raises whatever the focus. **Reset at power-on** (`Bres(XtNpopOnBell, ..., False)`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file), reset by `RIS` and kept by `DECSTR`, as `?1042` is. DECRQM answers `1` or `2`, as xterm's `MdBool(screen->poponbell)`; XTSAVE and XTRESTORE carry it. Before, it was counted as unsupported and DECRQM answered `0`. Since 2026-10-21 the Linux app sends the same activation `?1042` does: Wayland has no raise without focus, and the compositor decides whether the window comes forward (`BEL` above). `both_are_reset_at_power_on`, `each_mode_sets_and_resets_alone`, `ris_resets_both_and_decstr_keeps_them`, `a_minimized_window_is_not_raised_and_still_asks` |
| `CSI ? 2026 h/l` | synchronised output | done — exposed as `Term::synchronized_output()` |
| `CSI 3 J` | ED 3 erase saved lines | done — since 2026-10-09. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "Ps = 3 -> Erase Saved Lines, xterm". The history goes and the screen, the cursor and the rendition stay (`erase_saved_lines_empties_the_history_and_keeps_the_screen_and_the_cursor`); **the primary's from either screen**, as xterm's `do_erase_display` zeroes its one `savedlines` count whichever buffer is shown (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]`; `erase_saved_lines_on_the_alternate_screen_empties_the_primarys_history`); every ring row past the screen and the cut rows is erased, not only the count, for `RIS`'s reason (`erase_saved_lines_leaves_nothing_of_them_in_the_ring`). **The rows a shorter window cut below the cursor stay**: they are the bottom of the screen a growth brings back, not saved lines (`erase_saved_lines_keeps_the_rows_a_shrink_cut_below_the_window`). An image on an erased line goes. `Term::history_erasures` moves on it and on `RIS`, and the macOS app puts a window over the history on the live screen and settles a slide when it moves, however the read falls (`an_erased_history_puts_the_window_on_the_live_screen_however_the_read_falls`); since 2026-10-14 the terminal keeps that window and sends it live at the erasure's byte, from either screen (note V, V2; `an_erasure_sends_the_window_live_from_either_screen`); a selection naming an erased line is dropped or clipped to the screen by the clip every read applies. EL has no `3`: `CSI 3 K` stays counted |
| `CSI ? 3 J` | DECSED 3 selective erase saved lines | done — since 2026-10-09. "Selective Erase Saved Lines, xterm": DECSED over the history, so what DECSCA protected stays, the rest become spaces in their own rendition as DECSED's do, and the lines stay lines (`selective_erase_saved_lines_spares_what_decsca_protected`, `selective_erase_saved_lines_leaves_spaces_in_their_rendition`), from either screen. **xterm erases them whole** -- its `case 3` is one branch for both forms and ignores protection (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]`) -- where DECSCA's text says DECSED cannot erase a protected character (`[[source: vt510-decsca]]`); baud follows the text, decided 2026-10-10 by Jev at 1.00 and 0.98 under the owner's rule of that day and listed for the owner's veto (`docs/record.md`, "Jev's calls on `CSI ? 3 J`, XTSAVE and XTRESTORE, each as built"). Since 2026-10-21 each is counted, `Term::history_wipes`, so an embedder that keeps what it read of the history reads it again: the find field drops a match it wiped (`the_wipe_count_moves_on_each_selective_erase_of_the_saved_lines`, `the_list_kept_across_reads_is_the_list_read_afresh`) |
| `CSI ? s` | XTSAVE, `CSI ? Pm s`, save DEC private modes | done — since 2026-10-09. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "Ps values are the same as for DECSET ... this uses a one-level cache ... Only those modes listed as parameters are saved." **Every mode DECSET acts on keeps the value `DECRQM` answers**, one slot each (`PrivateMode::slot`), and one saved twice keeps the second (`the_cache_is_one_level_and_a_restore_keeps_it`); a mode DECSET does not act on -- unnamed, the read-only `?1020` to `?1023`, the permanent `?2027` -- is counted in `unsupported_param`, a parameter at a time, as DECSET counts it (`a_mode_decset_does_not_act_on_is_counted_by_both`). Held mode by mode against DECSET's own behaviour over `?0`-`?3000` and `?9999` (`every_mode_decset_changes_is_saved_and_restored_and_no_other_is`). The `?` keeps it from SCOSC and DECSLRM (`the_question_mark_keeps_them_apart_from_scosc_decstbm_and_decslrm`). **`RIS` empties the cache**, ECMA-48 § 8.3.105's "initial state" (`[[source: ecma48]]`), and `DECSTR` keeps it (`ris_empties_the_cache_and_decstr_keeps_it`). xterm saves a different set (`savemodes`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`): not `?2` or `?8`, and `?3` only under the `c132` resource `?40` sets, where baud saves `?3` whatever `?40` says and restores it through DECSET, so a restore acts only under `?40` -- following the text, decided 2026-10-10 by Jev at 1.00 and 1.00 and listed for the owner's veto (`docs/record.md`, "Jev's calls on `CSI ? 3 J`, XTSAVE and XTRESTORE, each as built"); one slot for the five tracking levels and one for the four encodings, the owner's call (XT-4, XT-5); and it keeps the cache across `RIS`, where baud empties it for ECMA-48's initial state, decided 2026-10-10 by Jev at 0.94 and 0.94 and listed for the owner's veto |
| `CSI ? r` | XTRESTORE, `CSI ? Pm r`, restore DEC private modes | done — since 2026-10-09. "The value of Ps previously saved is restored ...
