Only those modes listed as parameters are restored." **Through DECSET's own path**, so a restore has the effects setting or resetting the mode has -- `?1049` saves the cursor and clears, or leaves and restores it; `?6` homes; `?3` clears (`xtrestore_has_the_side_effects_of_setting_the_mode`) -- and `DECRQM` answers from the state it left (`xtrestore_brings_back_what_xtsave_saved`). **Only a mode whose value differs from the saved one is set or reset**, so restoring a tracking family puts back the level saved in either order, and `?1049` restored set on the alternate screen does not clear it (`a_restore_changes_only_what_differs_from_the_saved_value`). The text is silent on a value already in force; xterm re-applies `?6` whatever its value, and `?3` too under its `c132` resource (`restoremodes`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`), and baud's no change was decided 2026-10-10 by Jev at 0.96 and 0.94 and is listed for the owner's veto. **A mode never saved restores nothing** (`only_what_was_saved_and_listed_is_restored`): a bare `CSI ? 2 r` stays in ANSI. xterm restores `?1049` as the bare switch of `?47`, where baud follows the text, decided 2026-10-10 by Jev at 0.98 and 0.96 and listed for the owner's veto (`docs/record.md`, "Jev's calls on `CSI ? 3 J`, XTSAVE and XTRESTORE, each as built"); it restores the tracking level saved under any of the four mouse modes (XT-4) and a never-saved mode from a zeroed array -- `?25 r` hides the cursor (XT-1) (`restoremodes`, `VTInitialize`'s `memset`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`): each the owner's call. The `?` keeps it from DECSTBM. Generated programs reach both through `?25`'s atoms (`properties.rs`) |
| `CSI ? 25 h/l` | DECTCEM cursor visibility | done — state exposed via `cursor_visible()`. A hide that is exactly `ESC [ ? 2 5 l` with a complete `CUP` or `HVP` straight before it also records where the cursor is as `placed_cursor()` -- helix hides its cursor for good and moves it to the selection first on every frame, and an embedder's trail follows the placement; `?25h`, a screen switch, either reset and a change of geometry forget it (`baud-core/src/placed.rs`, `tests/placed_cursor.rs`) |
| `CSI > 4 ; 2 m` | XTMODKEYS, `CSI > Pp ; Pv m`, every key-modifier resource | done — since 2026-10-19 an application can set how the cursor, function, keypad, modifier and special keys carry their modifiers, not only the ordinary keys: `Pp` 0 `modifyKeyboard`, 1 `modifyCursorKeys`, 2 `modifyFunctionKeys`, 3 `modifyKeypadKeys`, 4 `modifyOtherKeys` (levels 0-3, 3 sending unmodified keys too, ctlseqs' "space sends CSI 2 7 ; 1 ; 3 2 ~"), 6 `modifyModifierKeys`, 7 `modifySpecialKeys` (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), each read by `Term::key` as xterm's `Input` reads it (`[[source: xterm-input-c]]`): modifyCursorKeys and modifyFunctionKeys -1 to 4 as `modifyCursorKey` gives them, the extended form at 4 for any resource -- `CSI 27 ; mod ; code ~` or its `CSI u`, the code the key's keysym through `keysym2ucs` (`[[source: xterm-keysym2ucs-c]]`). `modifyKeyboard` is stored and reported and changes no key, as on xterm's default keyboard, which never reads it (Jev's lean, STORE_AND_REPORT, 0.64/0.72 on the second ask). An omitted `Pv` resets one resource and no parameter all seven, as the text says, where xterm's loop takes 1-5 (ALL_SEVEN, 0.76/0.76 then 0.68/0.76); `CSI > 4 ; Pv : mask m` sets modifyOtherKeys' mask, as xterm parses it (CODE_FORM, 0.69/0.88 then 0.80/0.89). The mask is the parameter's bits, Shift 1, Alt 2, Control 4, Meta 8, as ctlseqs' "a mask of modifier bits to factor out of the parameter encoding" reads; xterm applies ignore_now to the X event state (Shift 1, Lock 2, Control 4, Mod1 8), so the two agree on Shift and Control only. A key whose one modifier is masked goes to the base layer at levels 1 and 2 and, at 3, is still a sequence with the modifier factored out: Shift-a under `CSI > 4 ; 3 : 1 m` is `CSI 27 ; 1 ; 97 ~`, as xterm's min_mod 0 there gives it. **A value no text documents is refused and counted**, not rounded (REFUSE_COUNT, 0.82/0.90 then 0.88/0.86). Open departure: ctlseqs' own example of the mask, `CSI > 4 : 1 m`, which puts it on `Pp`, is refused and counted; xterm's `CASE_SET_MOD_FKEYS` reads the mask only as `Pv`'s sub-parameter, and Jev leaned CODE_FORM below the bar. Open departure: a vendor keysym, such as a media key, sends nothing under modifySpecialKeys 4, where ctlseqs maps those keysyms to the private use area from U+F0000; a u16 parameter cannot carry the number, and no driver sends those keys today. Open departure: at modifyOtherKeys 3 Shift plus a letter sends the unshifted code (`CSI 27 ; 2 ; 97 ~`) where xterm sends the keysym's (65). Open departure: under `CSI > 4 ; 3 : 1 m` Shift-Tab sends `CSI 27 ; 1 ; 9 ~`, an unmodified Tab's bytes, where xterm's Shift-Tab is the keysym ISO_Left_Tab, which is not an ordinary key, so the mask never applies to it and its function-key branch sends `CSI 27 ; 2 ; 9 ~` (`[[source: xterm-input-c]]` 1354-1362); for a plain Tab keysym xterm sends what baud sends. `modify_other_keys_3_sends_every_ordinary_key`, `modify_cursor_keys_each_value`, `modify_function_keys_each_value`, `the_keypad_modifier_and_special_resources_act_at_4`, `the_ignore_mask_factors_a_modifier_out`, `an_undocumented_value_is_refused_and_counted`, `the_texts_mask_example_is_refused_and_counted`, `the_worked_examples_are_what_baud_sends` |
| `CSI > 1 n` | disable key modifier options, `CSI > Ps n` | done — since 2026-10-19. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "This control sequence corresponds to a resource value of "-1", which cannot be set with the other sequence ... If the parameter is omitted, modifyFunctionKeys is disabled. When modifyFunctionKeys is disabled, xterm uses the modifier keys to make an extended sequence of function keys rather than adding a parameter": Shift-F1 is F11, Control-F1 F21, `CSI 42 ~` as xterm numbers keys past F20 (decided by Jev, XTERM_42_REMAP_ONLY, 0.97/0.94 on the second ask); modifyCursorKeys -1 drops the modifier. The sources conflict on which modifier adds what: `[[source: xterm-man]]` says that at modifyFunctionKeys 0 "Control adds the value given by the ctrlFKeys resource. Shift adds twice", and xterm's input.c does the reverse at -1 (`mfkNone`), Shift adding ctrl_fkeys and Control twice it (`[[source: xterm-input-c]]`); baud follows input.c, so Shift-F1 is F11. `the_controls_reset_set_and_disable`, `modify_function_keys_each_value` |
| `CSI > 4 ; 1 f` | XTFMTKEYS, `CSI > Pp ; Pv f`, every resource's format | done — `Pv` picks `CSI 27 ; mod ; code ~` or `[[source: fixterms]]`'s `CSI code ; mod u`, for each of the seven resources since 2026-10-19 (`formatCursorKeys` and the rest, read at 4, `[[source: xterm-man]]`). **This is the only route to the `CSI u` form**, which defines no negotiation of its own |
| `CSI > 1 s` | XTSHIFTESCAPE, `CSI > Ps s`, Shift over mouse tracking | done — since 2026-10-19. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "Ps = 0 -> allow shift-key to override mouse protocol. Ps = 1 -> conditionally allow shift-key as modifier in mouse protocol", 2 and 3 "disallowed in the control sequence" and counted, an omitted `Ps` 0. At 0, the default, Shift takes every pointer event from a program tracking the mouse, the wheel's included, to the selection or the screen's wheel (the history, or the cursor keys under `?1007`), as xterm's `ShiftOverride` does (`[[source: xterm-button-c]]`); at 1 only the left button, the one the selection binds, and a Shift middle or right press or wheel goes to the program with its Shift bit (Jev's leans, SELECTION_BUTTONS 0.80/0.88 then 0.78/0.78, OVERRIDE 0.78/0.94 on the second ask). An explicit 0 is 0, as the text gives it, where xterm's `one_if_default` reads it as 1. Kept by DECSTR and RIS, as xterm writes `shift_escape` nowhere else; the `>` keeps it from SCOSC and DECSLRM. `Term::shift_escape`; both drivers' pointers read it. `xtshiftescape_is_the_state_the_pointer_reads`, `xtshiftescape_1_hands_shift_only_the_selection_button`, `shift_takes_the_wheel_from_tracking_only_under_xtshiftescape_0` |
| `CSI > 2 p` | XTSMPOINTER, `CSI > Ps p`, when the pointer hides as the user types | done — since 2026-10-20, **typing hides the mouse pointer until the mouse moves, unless a program is using the mouse**, and a program can choose otherwise. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "Ps = 0 -> never hide the pointer. Ps = 1 -> hide if the mouse tracking mode is not enabled. Ps = 2 -> always hide the pointer, except when leaving the window. Ps = 3 -> always hide the pointer, even if leaving/entering the window. If no parameter is given, xterm uses the default, which is 1"; the owner's call of 2026-10-20, in with xterm's default. A value past 3 is refused and counted, where xterm's `CASE_HIDE_POINTER` stores any value (Jev, IGNORE, 0.96/0.92); the first parameter alone. The core keeps the mode (`Term::pointer_mode`) and answers whether a key that sends bytes hides the pointer (`Term::hides_pointer_on_key`): "tracking" is any of `?9`, `?1000`-`?1003` and DEC's locator, xterm's `send_mouse_pos`. When it comes back is xterm's, but for a press (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`, `xtermShowPointer`, `unselectwindow`, `xevents`; `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file): a move, at every mode; crossing the window's edge or losing the keyboard, below 3; a program turning tracking on or changing it, a new `OSC 22` shape and `RIS`; a key under a mode that no longer hides; a button press, at every mode, as xterm(1) documents the resource: "It will be redisplayed if the user moves the mouse, or clicks one of its buttons" (`[[source: xterm-man]]`, `pointerMode`), where xterm's `xevents` passes `ButtonPress` by, its departure from that text (Jev gave no decision to follow the code: PRESS_SHOWS 0.26/0.50, then PRESS_KEEPS 0.24 against PRESS_SHOWS 0.10). Power-on and `RIS` are `[pointer] hide_while_typing`, `"on"` (1) by default or `"off"` (0), xterm's `pointerMode` resource (Jev, ON_OFF, 0.96/0.91); `DECSTR` keeps the mode, as xterm's man page lists it under the full reset only (`[[source: xterm-man]]`). **macOS** hides it with `NSCursor.setHiddenUntilMouseMoves(_:)` (`[[source: apple-nscursor-sethiddenuntilmousemoves]]`), which shows it on a move by itself; **Linux** with `wl_pointer.set_cursor` and no surface, "If surface is NULL, the pointer image is hidden" (`[[source: wayland]]`), and at mode 3 again at each enter. xterm hides at every key press its `Input` handles, a modifier's included; baud at a key that sends bytes, "as the user types". Counted as unsupported before (`xtsmpointer_is_acted_on_not_counted`, `each_value_sets_when_a_key_hides_the_pointer`, `a_value_past_three_is_refused_and_counted`, `ris_returns_the_embedders_pointer_mode_and_decstr_keeps_it`, `xtsmpointer_is_the_same_at_every_split`, `a_key_hides_it_and_motion_shows_it`, `a_press_shows_it_at_every_mode`, `crossing_shows_it_but_at_mode_3`, `tracking_turned_on_shows_it`, `the_pointer_hides_with_no_surface_and_a_shape_shows_it`) |
| `CSI = 1 ; 1 u`, `CSI ? u`, `CSI > 1 u`, `CSI < 1 u` | kitty progressive enhancement | **done -- all five flags, and the query is answered.** `[[source: kitty-keyboard]]` detects support by whether the query is *answered*, so a reply is the claim: `CSI ? u` now answers `CSI ? flags u`, made only after `Term::key` honours every enhancement (disambiguate, event types, alternate keys, all-keys, associated text) for text, functional, keypad and the Private Use Area keys. `CSI = / > / <` maintain the per-screen flag stack. Its own words -- "It does not make sense to implement only a subset" -- is why this was all-or-nothing and the query stayed silent through every partial increment. `the_query_is_answered_with_the_current_flags` |
| `CSI ? 4 m` / `CSI > 4 g` | XTQMODKEYS / XTQFMTKEYS | done — since 2026-10-19 an application can ask which key-modifier format is in force and send the answer back to restore it, except for a disabled resource (the open departure below). Each answers in xterm's shape, `report_mod_fkeys` and `report_fmt_fkeys` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file): `CSI > Pp ; Pv m` and `CSI > Pp ; Pv f`, the value included, so the reply is the XTMODKEYS or XTFMTKEYS that sets it, one reply a parameter as xterm loops over them (`xtqmodkeys_answers_modify_other_keys_with_its_value`, `xtqfmtkeys_answers_format_other_keys_with_its_value`). `[[source: ctlseqs]]` gives the reply's shape without the value at lines 1355-1371 for XTQMODKEYS (the shape `CSI > Pp m` is at 1371) and at lines 883-899 for XTQFMTKEYS (`CSI > Pp f` at 899); xterm's code sends it. **Every resource is answered** since 2026-10-19; a `Pp` naming none with the value xterm writes for its `DEFAULT`, -1 printed unsigned, 65535, and counted (Jev's lean, XTERM_65535, 0.45/0.30 then 0.46/0.49); a disabled one as xterm writes it, `CSI > Pp ; 65535 m` (Jev's lean, XTERM_65535 in both orders on the first ask, 0.22/0.83; the second ask split, DISABLE_FORM at 0.30 in one order and XTERM_65535 at 0.18 in the other). Open departure: that answer cannot be fed back to restore the state, because baud refuses 65535 as a value (`each_queried_resource_is_answered_or_counted`, `every_resource_answers_as_xterm_reports_it`). **The XTQFMTKEYS query is `CSI > Pp g`**, the form xterm 411's parse table reads (`[[source: xterm-vtprstbl-c]]`, `dec2_table`); ctlseqs line 883 documents `CSI ? Pp g`, which xterm ignores, and baud counts it (Jev's lean, 0.48 and 0.34, taken after a second ask) |
| `APC G a=t` / `a=T` / `a=q` | kitty graphics: transmit, transmit and display, query | done — since 2026-10-16 (note V, V19 steps 5-6; `[[source: kitty-graphics]]`). An `APC` whose body begins `G` is routed to `baud_domain::KittyStream` by its first byte, the graph unchanged. `f=24` and `f=32` are base64 decoded straight into the image's reserved arena span, `o=z` inflated into it (or into the PNG scratch) as its chunks arrive, `f=100` read whole from the scratch; chunks of any length, a continuation's keys but `m` and `q` ignored (Jev, ACCEPT_ANY_LENGTH_IGNORE_EXTRA_KEYS), a delete aborting an upload in flight, a cancelled chunk aborting and counted. Every declared size -- `s` x `v`, `S`, a PNG's `IHDR` -- is checked against the arena before anything is reserved, refused, counted and answered. A PNG and inflated pixels are placed, and the cursor moved, at the last chunk and decoded in steps by `Term::decode_step` (Jev, DEFER_IN_CORE_STEPPED), shown and answered when whole; through a file medium, decoded whole at the terminator and only then placed (see `APC G t=f`); a query is decoded at its terminator, before the next byte, into the probe below, and stores nothing. Each image is marked by its own pixels. Replies `ESC _ G i=..[,I=..][,p=..];OK` or the error, only for a command naming `i` or `I`, `q=1` silencing `OK` and `q=2` all, always the 7-bit `ESC _ G ... ESC \` the text writes, whatever S8C1T says (`replies_keep_the_seven_bit_shape_under_s8c1t`; following S8C1T is an open owner question, Jev FOLLOW_S8C1T 0.92 and 0.88, under the bar); `i` with `I` is EINVAL, an `I` alone gets a fresh id. Re-sending an id deletes the old image and its placements; a nameless image goes with its last placement and at the next transmission, as kitty does. **The quota is the text's** ("Image persistence and storage quotas"): a new image that finds no slot or no arena gap deletes the oldest kitty images with no placement, on screen or in the history, until it fits, each counted in `images_evicted`; when deleting them all would not make space none goes and it is refused ENOSPC and counted once (Jev, DELETE_NONE_IF_INSUFFICIENT 0.90 and 0.92; `a_new_image_deletes_the_oldest_unplaced_image_to_make_space` and the three after it). **A query stores nothing** -- it "will not replace an existing image with the same id, nor will it store the image" -- so since 2026-10-16 it decodes in a 64x64 probe outside the arena and its quota (Jev, QUERY_SMALL_SCRATCH 0.98 and 0.97), answered on a full arena, deleting and trimming nothing and moving no epoch; a larger query decodes in an arena gap, deleting nothing, else ENOSPC (`a_query_on_a_full_arena_is_answered_and_deletes_nothing` and the five after it). **The app's default arena holds "at least a few full screen images"**: 95 MiB, three 3840x2160 screens, since 2026-10-16, at no idle cost, the arena coming from zeroed memory (`host::IMAGE_BUDGET`, `the_default_budget_holds_three_3840x2160_screens`). A reply the stream's outbox cannot hold is dropped whole and counted in `KittyCounts::replies_dropped`. Open departure: an image with a placement is never deleted, where the text's "preferentially" allows it. Chunk-invariant at every read size and two-read split across the body, base64 quanta, zlib blocks and `m=1` chunks (`tests/kitty_graphics.rs`) |
| `APC G a=p` | kitty graphics: put a stored image | **partial** — since 2026-10-16. By `i` or the newest `I`, ENOENT when none; `(i, p)` replaces the placement with that pair; the cursor moves right by the columns and down by the rows the placement is drawn in, `C=1` not at all; a count not given is the cells the drawn image reaches, where kitty adds `X` to the box (`[[source: kitty-graphics-c]]` `update_dest_rect`). Kitty moves down by the rows less one, its practice and not followed (Jev, asked uncached on 2026-10-16, 0.86 and 0.76 for the text). **Drawn as the text lays it out** since 2026-10-16 ("Controlling displayed image layout"), on the CPU and both Metal paths from one description (`KittyPlace::draw`) and one set of nearest-neighbour tables (`Renderer::image_map`): the source rectangle `x y w h` intersected with the image; `c` alone or `r` alone scaled with the other from the aspect, to the nearest pixel, the `X`/`Y` offset not added to the box; both letterboxed or pillarboxed and centred (Jev, CENTRED, 0.94 and 0.97), where kitty stretches; `z` negative under the text and over the backgrounds, and the order by `z`, then the lower client id under, then placement order, where kitty orders by its internal id. A margin scroll, a `DECSLRM` rectangle's included, moves a kitty placement only when its shown rows, and under side margins its columns, are all between the margins and clips the rows it takes past them, "Interaction with other terminal actions"; `IL` and `DL` the same, baud's choice; under a top-anchored region, which moves the window, the rows under it are put back with their images and one across its bottom margin stays (`a_top_anchored_region_leaves_the_images_it_does_not_scroll`). Each pixel is the source texel under its centre (nearest; Jev picked it on four asks, 0.88/0.52 and 0.98/0.72, under the bar on one of each, so interim and open; kitty and ghostty sample linearly). `baud-render/tests/kitty_draw.rs`, the `kitty_draw_gate` of `baud-present-macos/tests/cells.rs` holding all three paths to the text's pixels, `a_margin_scroll_moves_only_an_image_inside_the_margins_and_clips_it` and `a_rectangle_scroll_moves_only_an_image_inside_its_margins_and_clips_it`. A `z` strictly below `INT32_MIN/2` is drawn under the text and under every cell whose background is not the default one, whose background is composited over it at its own alpha with nothing but the window under the image there, so on the glass the image shows through a dimmed coloured cell as the desktop does and an opaque one hides it (the owner's pick, "Picture shows dimmed", 2026-10-16, kitty's blend, `[[source: kitty-cell-slang]]`); a cell is default when the colour its background is drawn in, after `SGR 7` and `?5` exchange the pair, equals the default background's colour as stored, the theme's or `OSC 11`'s, so an explicit colour of that value is default and the comparison follows `OSC 11`, a default cell under one of `SGR 7` and `?5` is not and under both is, and a selected cell or one under a block cursor is not (the value the owner's pick, "Compare the colour", 2026-10-16, as kitty's `cell_has_default_bg`, kitty's `is_reversed` keeping an `SGR 7` cell special where baud decides it by the value, `[[source: kitty-background-slang]]`; SGR 7, `?5`, the selection and the cursor: Jev 1.00 and 0.98, 1.00 and 1.00; a placeholder's `z` the owner's pick, as built); a placeholder's image never goes below its own cells' backgrounds ("visible if the image is transparent"). `a_z_below_half_int32_min_draws_under_non_default_backgrounds`, `a_z_of_half_int32_min_exactly_stays_over_every_background`, `an_explicit_background_of_the_default_colour_shows_a_deep_image`, `on_the_glass_a_coloured_cell_is_blended_over_a_deep_image` and the `kitty_below_gate` and `kitty_picks_gate` of `cells.rs`. `P` is refused with EINVAL, placing nothing (Jev, REFUSE_WITH_EINVAL, 1.00 and 0.98), a virtual placement made relative among them, which the text answers EINVAL. **A picture placed one cell at a time is drawn whole**, since 2026-10-18: the table holds a placement for every cell of the largest screen the storage displays (64 at least, `NPLACEMENT`), where it held 64 and yazi 26.9.1's preview, an `a=p` per cell, showed one row and part of the next; The placement a put replaces, and the one a delete naming `i` and `p` takes, is found by the pair through an index beside the table, a step a lookup where it was a walk of every placement before it, since 2026-10-18 (`a_put_and_a_delete_find_their_placement_by_its_key`, `puts_and_deletes_in_any_order_leave_each_pair_found`); so the lower rows of a tall picture stay over the text the history still holds, "images must be scrolled along with text", kitty's `scroll_filter_func` (`[[source: kitty-graphics-c]]`), since 2026-10-18, Jev at 1.00 both ways: it went with its anchor row before (`a_tall_picture_goes_when_its_last_row_leaves_the_history`, `a_tall_picture_on_the_alternate_screen_goes_with_its_last_row`, `a_cut_picture_goes_when_its_last_shown_row_leaves_the_history`, `a_reflow_keeps_a_picture_past_the_far_end_and_drops_it_with_its_rows`, `erasing_the_history_keeps_a_picture_with_rows_on_screen`, and `kitty_history_gate` in `baud-present-macos/tests/cells.rs` on all three paths);
