through a file medium, the reply is the single EBADF (see `APC G t=f`); an `a=T` answers for the placement it asked for in the same way, at its terminator or, deferred, when its decode completes, and through a file medium the reply is the single EBADF (see `APC G t=f`), where kitty builds the `a=T` reply before it places (`[[source: kitty-graphics-c]]`) and so answers `OK` (Jev, PLACEMENT_OUTCOME, 1.00 and 1.00; `a_placement_a_full_table_refuses_is_answered_enospc`, `a_transmission_whose_placement_is_refused_is_answered_enospc`, at every read size and two-read split, `q` absent, 1 and 2). `U=1` is the row below |
| `APC G a=p,U=1` / `a=T,U=1`, `U+10EEEE` | kitty graphics: Unicode placeholders | done — since 2026-10-16 (note V, V19; `[[source: kitty-graphics]]`, "Unicode placeholders"). **A virtual placement** is kept in the kitty stream's own table of 256 (`KITTY_VIRTUALS`), apart from the placements on screen, holds a reference on its image so the quota spares it, moves no cursor whatever `C` says (Jev, STAY, 1.00 and 1.00), is answered as a put is, and is replaced by the same image and non-zero placement id; a full table refuses it, counts it (`KittyCounts::virtuals_refused`) and, since 2026-10-16, answers `ENOSPC:No room for another virtual placement`, never `OK` (`a_virtual_placement_a_full_table_refuses_is_answered_enospc`; the text names no code for it, kitty having no such table, and ENOSPC is the code it gives for storage run out; Jev, ENOSPC, 1.00 and 1.00); an image no id names -- `a=T,U=1` with neither `i` nor `I` -- is refused and counted and answered nothing, the text replying only to a command that named an image. **A U+10EEEE cell** is one cell wide (private use, narrow whatever the ambiguous width); a listed diacritic after it, up to three, goes into the cell's own content word, not the cluster store (`Content::placeholder`; Jev CONTENT_WORD 0.72 and 0.68, under the bar, then the owner's pick, 2026-10-16: the store dropped 2366 of an 80x24 kitten picture's 5760 marks), any other mark sends it to the store, and a copy reads back what was written. **Resolved as drawn** (`Term::placeholder_runs`, `PlaceholderRuns`): the image id from the foreground (24-bit value or palette index) with the third diacritic's top byte, the placement id from the underline colour, the row and column from the diacritics in `[[source: kitty-rowcolumn-diacritics]]` (a table generated by `scripts/gen-placeholder-table.py`, `placeholder_table_matches_the_pinned_list`); a missing value inherited from the cell immediately to the left (Jev, ADJACENT, 1.00 and 1.00) when the two ids match (Jev, IDS, 1.00 and 1.00, where the text's words compare "colors") and the rest follows, else row 0, column 0, byte 0, kitty's practice (`[[source: kitty-screen-c]]`). **Drawn** on the software compositor and both Metal paths as one `ImagePlacement` a run: the virtual placement's whole image fitted into its `c` x `r` cells, aspect kept and centred, a count not given the image's size in cells, `x y w h X Y` unused (Jev, FIT_WHOLE, 1.00 and 1.00), each run its strip, clipped to its cells; the placeholder and its diacritics are never drawn as glyphs, and with no image to show the cell is its background (Jev BLANK 0.80 and 0.84, under the bar, then the owner's pick, 2026-10-16; TUS 5.3's `.notdef` the other option). `tests/kitty_placeholder.rs` in baud-core and baud-render, the `kitty_placeholder_gate` of `baud-present-macos/tests/cells.rs` holding the three arms to a placement of the same box, and `kittens_placeholders_place_the_picture`, kitten 0.49.2's own output replayed. Chunk-invariant at every read size to 8 and every two-read split. **In a smooth scroll's leaving row** too, since 2026-10-16: the line above the window resolves its runs from its own cells, `view(back + 1).row(0)`, the row the cell path slides in, so a slide shows the strip that row showed on screen, and a settled frame clips it above the top; with no line above the window, an empty history or the alternate screen, nothing is placed there (`the_leaving_row_places_the_strip_it_placed_on_screen` in baud-render; `kitty_placeholder_leaving_gate`, the cell arm over the ring and over the window's copy at a whole and a half row's slide, and all four arms settled, at `z` 0, -1 and below `INT32_MIN/2`). A placeholder image's `z` is the virtual placement's, never below its own cells' backgrounds (the owner's pick, 2026-10-16). Horizontal scrolling and overlapping images are the text's "will not work" |
| `APC G a=d` | kitty graphics: delete | done — since 2026-10-16: every specifier of the "Deleting images" table but the frames, `a i n c p q r x y z`, lowercase removing placements and keeping named images, uppercase freeing what it leaves unplaced, `I`, `N` and `R` freeing an image only stored, as kitty's `handle_delete_command` does (`[[source: kitty-graphics-c]]`); no reply. Text does not erase a kitty image and `ED 2` and `RIS` clear them ("Interaction with other terminal actions"); a Sixel keeps the text-erases rule. **A virtual placement** goes only by `d=i`, `I`, `n`, `N`, `r` and `R`, uppercase freeing an image it alone held; "a, c, p, q, x, y, z and their capital variants never affect virtual placements", nor do `ED 2` and the alternate screen; re-sending its image's id takes it, `RIS` everything (`only_the_id_number_and_range_deletes_reach_a_virtual_placement`, `a_virtual_placement_outlives_the_screen_and_not_its_image`) |
| `APC G t=f` / `t=t` / `t=s` | kitty graphics: file, temporary file and shared-memory mediums | done — since 2026-10-16 through an embedder hook (Jev, TD_FIRST_FILES_LATER; `[[source: kitty-graphics]]`, "The transmission medium"). The core never opens a file: at the command's terminator, inside the read, it hands `baud_domain::KittyFiles` the decoded path or shared-memory name, `O`, `S` and the most bytes the transmission can use, and decodes what comes back as a `t=d` payload, so a query is answered before the next byte and a placement moves the cursor before the text after it (`a_served_query_per_medium_is_answered_before_the_primary_da`). Raw pixels are read to `s * v` and no further, a PNG bounded by the scratch and zlib data by its bound, refused before reading past them; an object "smaller than the client claimed" by `S` is the one error, the text's plain reading, where kitty reads `S` as a bound and kitten's detection sends `S` as the path's length (Jev, uncached, 0.69 and 0.72 for the text, under the bar for a departure; so kitten's detection, refused its `t=t` probe, sends `t=d`); a path past 2048 bytes is EINVAL, kitty's; a file medium is one chunk. Once the reader is asked, every refusal -- a failed read, and a PNG that does not decode, a size past the store or a placement refused after it, which would each tell the client the file was there -- is `EBADF:Failed to read image file` (`every_file_failure_answers_one_identical_reply`, `every_refusal_after_the_read_is_the_one_reply`; kitty answers a decode failure `EBADPNG`, not followed), and keeps nothing, as a missing file keeps nothing: the image freed, so an `a=p` after it answers ENOENT, and the cursor unmoved, the image being decoded whole at the terminator and placed only then, so the reply comes before the next byte (`a_refusal_after_the_read_keeps_nothing`, raw, raw through zlib and PNG, a relative placement and either full table; `a_file_png_that_fails_late_moves_nothing_and_answers_in_order`, at every read size and two-read split); a refusal decided before, from the control data and the path alone, keeps its code and reads nothing (`a_refusal_before_the_read_keeps_its_code_and_reads_nothing`). baud-app's `LocalFiles` resolves the path, following links, refuses `/proc`, `/sys` and `/dev` (the text's "may refuse") but a file under `/dev/shm`, which the text names a temporary directory (Jev 0.86 and 0.90), and a relative path (Jev 0.68 and 0.70, interim and open), refuses anything not regular before opening, walks the resolved path from `/` a directory at a time with `openat`, following no link, requires the last directory's device and inode to be the one resolved, and only then opens the file in it, following no link, with `O_NONBLOCK`, the same order on Darwin and Linux (`an_ancestor_swapped_for_a_link_before_the_checks_is_refused`, `a_directory_renamed_in_after_resolving_is_refused`, `a_directory_swapped_for_a_link_after_resolving_is_refused`), and requires the descriptor's `fstat` to be the regular file checked, device and inode (the swap test); deletes a `t=t` file only in a resolved temporary directory, by component, with `tty-graphics-protocol` in its path, from the directory it was read from (`unlinkat` on the walk's descriptor, `a_directory_swapped_before_the_unlink_does_not_move_it`); reads `t=s` through `shm_open` (mapped on Darwin), then unlinks it (`baud-app/src/kitty_files.rs`'s tests). With no reader lent the answer is the error, nothing reserved (`with_no_reader_a_file_medium_reserves_nothing`). `kitten icat` 0.49.2's `file` (`t=f`), `memory` (`t=s`) and `detect` (`t=d`, above) decode through it live (`kitten_icat_reads_through_the_reader_live`, ignored, `BAUD_KITTEN`) |

---

## Out of scope, with reasons

Recorded so that "not implemented" is a decision rather than an omission.

| seq | name | why not |
|---|---|---|
| `OSC 9 ; 1` to `OSC 9 ; 12`, but `9 ; 4` | ConEmu's other `OSC 9` forms: sleep, message box, tab title, wait for a key, GuiMacro, run a process, an environment variable, the directory, xterm emulation, a comment, the prompt | **out** — refused and counted, never posted as a notification (`conemus_other_forms_post_nothing`). Five are unsafe from a remote program: `9;7` "Run some process", `9;6` "Execute GuiMacro", `9;8` "Output value of environment variable", `9;2` a modal box and `9;1`/`9;5` block the console (`[[source: conemu-ansi-escape-codes]]`); `9;3` and `9;9` are the title and the directory, which `OSC 0`-`2` and `OSC 7` carry; `9;10` switches ConEmu's own emulation; `9;11` is "Just a 'comment', skip it"; `9;12` marks a prompt, which `OSC 133` does |
| `OSC 777 ; Pt` but `notify` | rxvt-unicode's perl extensions | **out** — "Call the perl extension with the given string, which should be of the form extension;parameters" (`[[source: rxvt-7-pod]]`): a call into urxvt's perl, which baud does not have. Counted |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetMark` | iTerm2: a mark to jump back to | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (Jev, 0.97 and 0.92: `RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; StealFocus` | iTerm2: bring the terminal to the foreground | **out** — refused and counted under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`, among its refusals): a program bringing the window in front of whatever the user is in is focus stealing, which iTerm2 itself puts behind a prompt ("A control sequence attempted to activate a session. Allow it?", `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-ptysession]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetProfile=name` | iTerm2: switch to another profile | **out** — refused and counted under its key, among its refusals: a program switching the user's settings for another set of them rewrites what the user chose (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetProfileProperty=key=base64-JSON` | iTerm2: change one profile setting | **out** — refused and counted under its key, among its refusals: a program changing the user's settings one at a time rewrites what the user chose (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; CopyToClipboard=name` | iTerm2: with `EndCopy`, copy the text between them | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (Jev, 0.97 and 0.92: `RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; AddAnnotation=..., AddHiddenAnnotation=...` | iTerm2: attach a note to a range of text | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (Jev, 0.97 and 0.92: `RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; HighlightCursorLine=yes|no` | iTerm2: the cursor guide | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (Jev, 0.97 and 0.92: `RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetBackgroundImageFile=base64` | iTerm2: a background image, after the user confirms | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (Jev, 0.97 and 0.92: `RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; RequestUpload=format=tgz` | iTerm2: ask the user for files to upload | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (Jev, 0.97 and 0.92: `RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetKeyLabel=key=value, PushKeyLabels, PopKeyLabels` | iTerm2: Touch Bar labels | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (Jev, 0.97 and 0.92: `RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; UnicodeVersion=n` | iTerm2: which Unicode version widths follow | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (Jev, 0.97 and 0.92: `RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; File=args, MultipartFile, FilePart, FileEnd` | iTerm2: inline images and file downloads (`[[source: iterm2-images]]`) | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (Jev, 0.97 and 0.92: `RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`). An inline image does not reach baud's image store yet: Sixel and kitty's graphics do |
| `OSC 1337 ; Custom=id=secret:pattern` | iTerm2: a custom control sequence for a script | **out** — no counterpart: a sequence handed to a script through iTerm2's Python API, which baud does not have. Counted under its key |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetUserVar=name=base64` | iTerm2: a user variable | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (Jev, 0.97 and 0.92: `RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; ShellIntegrationVersion=n` | iTerm2: the shell integration version | **out** — no counterpart: "if it sees a lower number the user will be prompted to upgrade" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), iTerm2's own shell-integration scripts, which baud does not ship. Counted under its key |
| `OSC 1337 ; ClearCapturedOutput` | iTerm2: clear the captured output | **out** — no counterpart: the output iTerm2's triggers capture into its toolbelt, which baud does not have. Counted under its key |
| `OSC 1337 ; OpenURL=:url` | iTerm2: open a URL | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (Jev, 0.97 and 0.92: `RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; Disinter` | iTerm2: the inverse of burying a session | **out** — no counterpart: iTerm2 buries sessions, baud does not. Counted under its key |
| `OSC 1337 ; Block=id=...;attr=start|end, UpdateBlock, Button` | iTerm2: foldable blocks of output and their buttons | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (Jev, 0.97 and 0.92: `RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetBadgeFormat=base64` | iTerm2: the session badge (`[[source: iterm2-badges]]`) | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (Jev, 0.97 and 0.92: `RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; Capabilities` | iTerm2: report the terminal's features as a feature string (`[[source: iterm2-feature-reporting]]`) | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (Jev, 0.97 and 0.92: `RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetWindowFrame`, `ReportWindowFrame`, `ReportScreenFrames`, `Notification`, `AddNote`, `AddHiddenNote` | iTerm2: keys its source compares and its pages do not name (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-vt100terminal]]`) | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built.** Recognised and counted under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen. `SetWindowFrame`, the two frame reports and `Notification` have a meaning in baud; `AddNote` and `AddHiddenNote` are the old names of the annotations, unbuilt with them. `every_key_not_acted_on_is_counted` |
| `OSC 1337 ; BeginFile`, `EndFile`, `Env`, `it2ssh`, `SendConductor`, `EndSSH`, `wrap`, `ExecFailed`, `Debug` | iTerm2: keys its source compares and its pages do not name (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-vt100terminal]]`) | **out** — each counted under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`). `BeginFile` and `EndFile` iTerm2 itself ignores ("Deprecated and unsupported"); the rest drive iTerm2's ssh integration and its debug build, which baud has no counterpart of. `every_key_not_acted_on_is_counted`, and `every_iterm2_key_has_its_row` holds the table to the pinned pages' keys and the source's |
| `OSC 21 ; transparent_background_color1` to `7` | kitty: cells whose background is that colour drawn at an opacity of its own | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (Jev, T_UNKNOWN_ROW, 0.98 both orders: per-colour background opacity is its own work, on every path). "A background color that is rendered with the specified opacity in cells that have the specified background color" (`[[source: kitty-6d5d0c4-color-stack]]`). Until then each is a key baud does not know: a query answers `?`, as kitty answers transparent_background_color9, and is not counted, as DECRQM's "not recognized" is not; a set or a reset is counted (`a_transparent_background_colour_is_an_unknown_key`) |
| kitty graphics `a=f` / `a=a` / `a=c`, `d=f`, `P` | animation, relative placements | **In scope and unbuilt; they stay in this table until they are built.** The protocol is built in Tier 3 since 2026-10-16 (note V, V19 steps 5-6), Unicode placeholders with it. An animation command and a frame delete are ignored and counted (`Term::kitty().ignored`): the text gives no reply for a terminal without them. A relative placement is refused with EINVAL and places nothing. And **not to be confused with `[[source: kitty-keyboard]]`**, which shares a vendor and nothing else |
| `DCS Ps $ t D...D ST` | DECRSPS restore presentation state | done — since 2026-10-19. A DECCIR's data (`Ps` 1) or a DECTABSR's (`Ps` 2) restores what it reports (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-205; `[[source: vt510-decrsps]]`), so a report fed back puts the state back (`a_report_fed_back_restores_what_it_reported`). The cursor goes to the position, confined to the margins under a restored origin mode as DECRC confines it; the four renditions are set or cleared and the rest of the rendition kept (Jev's lean FOUR_ONLY); a pending wrap comes back on the column it is pending on; a single-shift bit leaves the shift pending after the string (Jev's lean RESTORE_PENDING); `Scss` says which series each final of `Sdesig` is from, so `A` is the UK set or ISO Latin-1 Supplemental. The tab stops become the listed columns at the terminator, each kept as a bit as it arrives, so a body of any length restores (`decrsps_restores_a_stop_at_every_column`) and one a `CAN`, an `ESC` or a C1 cancels changes nothing (`a_cancelled_tab_restore_leaves_the_stops`). **An invalid value ends the restore there**, the fields before it kept: `[[source: vt510-decrsps]]`, "the terminal ignores the rest of the sequence", where STD 070 has the terminal "ignore the error, if possible" first (Jev decided STOP_AT_FIRST, 0.90 and 0.94 after research; `decrsps_stops_at_an_invalid_value`); a tab stop past the screen is one too (Jev's lean STOP), and `Pgr` 0, G0 in GR, which vt510's DECCIR lists and ECMA-35 8.3.3 rules out, is accepted (Jev's lean ACCEPT). The position is the screen's, under DECOM too (Jev, SCREEN, 0.96 and 0.96). `Ps` 0 and other values are consumed and dropped |
| `DCS Ps $ p D...D ST` | DECRSTS restore terminal state | done — since 2026-10-22. `Ps` 1 restores a DECTSR (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-208): the body is kept whole and checksummed at the terminator, and a byte outside `4/0..4/15`, a failed checksum, a version not baud's or a cut body restore nothing, "If the checksum fails, the entire sequence is ignored" (`a_report_that_does_not_check_restores_nothing`). **Each field goes through the control that sets it, and only where it differs from the state now**, XTRESTORE's rule (Jev's lean, R_DIFFER, 0.48/0.62 after research): the conformance level first, since DECSCL soft-resets, and the C1 transmission alone through `S7C1T`/`S8C1T` when the level is the same (`the_c1_transmission_is_restored_without_a_soft_reset`); the input encoding as `ESC % @` and `ESC % G` set it, the bytes after the string decoded under it (`the_input_encoding_is_restored`); each mode through SM or DECSET, so `?3` resizes and clears as it does, and the alternate screen is switched to or from through `?47`, the first of its three modes in slot order, so `?1049`'s cursor save is not replayed, with `?40` and `?1046` first, since `?3` moves only while `?40` is set and the alternate screen is reached only while `?1046` is (`a_gated_mode_is_restored_after_its_gate`); the status line's type and display (`the_status_line_type_and_display_are_restored`); DECSTBM and DECSLRM; DECSCUSR, DECSWBV, DECSMBV, DECSACE, DECSCPP and DECSLPP, the last two as window requests under `[window] operations` (`the_page_size_is_restored_as_a_window_request`); XTSMTITLE and the key-modifier controls; the rendition as its SGR; the cursor information through DECRSPS's restore; then the tab stops and the shown screen's `DECSC` save, written directly since no control restores either, the save into the slot of the screen the modes left shown, or emptied when the report had none (`the_saved_cursor_is_restored`, `decrsts_restores_what_every_tsi_control_reports`, `the_level_is_restored_before_what_it_resets`). A field with a value baud cannot take is stepped over and the rest restored, STD 070's "the terminal will ignore the error if possible", where the VT340, VT420 and VT510 manuals stop the restore (`[[source: vt3xx-tp-002]]` p. 209, `[[source: vt420-rm]]` p. 247, `[[source: vt510-rm-b]]` p. 5-113; Jev's lean, I_SKIP, 0.72/0.36 after research; `an_invalid_field_is_stepped_over`). `Ps` 2 restores a DECCTR: each group is set as its `/` arrives, as `OSC 4` sets a colour, "only the index entries specified are changed" (p. 5-109), and an invalid group -- `Pc` past 255, `Pu` not 1 or 2, a value past its range, a parameter missing -- stops the restore there, the manuals' "ignores the rest of the sequence" (Jev's lean, G_STOP, 0.18/0.30 after research; `an_invalid_colour_group_stops_the_restore`); a `CAN` keeps the groups set before it. 0 and any other `Ps` are ignored. `DCS 1 $ p` and `DCS 2 $ p` are not ReGIS, whose `p` is bare (`a_dollar_p_string_is_no_regis`), and the `$` kept to tell them apart does not reach an escape that cancels the string (`a_cancelled_dollar_string_leaves_no_intermediate`). **A restore that asks for a page width holds the read** for an embedder that resizes, as DECSCPP and `?3` do, and a single shift pending in the report's DECCIR flags waits for the held rest, whose first character it takes at the width asked for, as SS2 "causes the next character in the received data stream" to be G2's (070-3 p. 3-80; `a_restore_that_asks_a_width_holds_with_a_single_shift_pending`); until 2026-10-22 the shift replaced the hold and the rest was parsed at the old width. baud's own report never carries a pending shift; a report a host made can. **Open**: the hold takes effect only when the string ends, so every field of the restore lands at the width before the request, and a 132-column report restored on an 80-column page loses its cursor information, whose restore stops at a column past the screen's last, and its side margins, even where the embedder then takes 132 |
| `DCS ... | DECUDK` | user-defined keys | **The stated reason expired and the row survives on a new one.** It read "Input-side; baud has no input path", and baud has one -- `encode_key` and `Term::key`. What keeps DECUDK out is that it redefines what a *key* sends, which needs a table of user-programmable strings and a policy for who may set them; the resource above it needs neither. Out on scope, not on absence |
| `CSI 20 t`, `CSI 21 t` | XTWINOPS icon label and title reports | **Refused because answering is unsafe**: the reply, `OSC L label ST` or `OSC l label ST`, puts text a program set back into the terminal's input, so a remote program that can set the title can make the terminal type a command for it. Nothing is written, and each is counted in `Term::title_reports_refused`, not in `unsupported_param`, so an embedder can see the requests arrive (`the_title_reports_are_refused_and_counted`). xterm refuses both by default too, its `disallowedWindowOps` (`[[source: xterm-main-h]]`). |
| `OSC 7 ; Pt` | the shell's working directory, a `file:` URI | done — since 2026-10-21, **a shell can tell baud which folder it is in**, as Apple's own shell files do at every prompt, "using a "file:" scheme URL, including the host name to disambiguate local vs. remote paths" (`[[source: macos-zshrc-apple-terminal]]` 17-20). No standard defines the sequence ("to my knowledge there is no documentation on the behavior of this escape", `[[source: terminal-wg-issue-20]]`); the payload is read on RFC 8089's grammar (`[[source: rfc8089]]` § 2) with RFC 3986's `host` and `path-absolute` (`[[source: rfc3986]]`): `file://host/path` and the authority-less `file:/path`, the scheme and `localhost` in any case, the path percent-decoded (RFC 3986 § 2.1), the bytes classed by a table built at compile time. The core keeps the host it named and the decoded path (`Term::working_directory`): no host and `localhost` are this machine, as RFC 8089 § 2 says of both, and any other name is the embedder's to compare with its own, since § 3's "resolves to the machine from which the URI is being interpreted" needs the machine. **Every report replaces the last**, and one the core cannot read leaves none, counted in `Term::directories_refused` and as unsupported: off the grammar (a raw space or UTF-8 byte, a query, a fragment, a broken escape, no path), of another scheme (`kitty-shell-cwd://`, which kitty's and ghostty's shell integrations send unencoded), decoding to a NUL, or cut at `OSC_MAX`, whose surviving path names another directory. `RIS` and `DECSTR` keep it. **The app shows it in the window's title** when a program sets none (the `OSC 0/1/2` row), and only when the host is this machine: no host, `localhost`, or one of its names without case -- the name `gethostname` gives, which shells send (zsh's `$HOST`, bash's `$HOSTNAME`), and on macOS also `NSProcessInfo`'s `hostName`, which there can be a different name, such as `<name>.local`; on a Mac with `HostName` set the two can be the same, and baud accepts either (`an_osc_7_naming_the_hostname_is_this_machine`); § 3's "resolves to the machine" is narrowed to those names, since a lookup would wait. A report naming another host is refused there and counted, once a report however often the title is built, and the program's own folder shown (Jev's lean, R1, 0.76/0.61, "Refuse a remote host for every use and count it"; § 5: "Treating a non-local file URI as local ... can result in security problems"; `a_local_osc_7_names_the_folder_and_a_remote_one_does_not`, `a_remote_osc_7_is_counted_once_a_report`). Under `ESC % @` a GR byte is its 7-bit byte, as in every `OSC` body (`apples_report_keeps_its_host_and_decodes_its_path`, `no_host_and_localhost_are_local`, `a_refused_report_leaves_no_directory_and_is_counted`, `a_report_cut_at_osc_max_is_refused`, `a_report_is_chunk_invariant`, `the_resets_keep_the_directory`, `under_latin1_a_gr_byte_is_its_seven_bit_byte`). Counted as unsupported before |
| `OSC 3 ; prop=value` | set an X property on the window | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can name the window and its icon through their X properties**, the owner's call of 2026-10-20 ("3 46 and 50 all yes", note AV): "Set X property on top-level window. Pt should be in the form \"prop=value\", or just \"prop\" to delete the property" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`). baud has no X window, so the properties with a counterpart are mapped and every other one is counted in `Term::properties_dropped` and as unsupported: ICCCM's `WM_NAME` and EWMH's `_NET_WM_NAME`, "the title of the window", set the title, as `OSC 2` does, and `WM_ICON_NAME` and `_NET_WM_ICON_NAME` the icon name, as `OSC 1` does, the last write winning (`[[source: icccm-2-0]]` chapter 4, "WM_NAME Property"; `[[source: ewmh-1-5-app]]` 5.1 and 5.3; Jev, ICCCM_AND_EWMH, 0.96/0.98). Pt is split at the first `=` and a name is matched exactly, as xterm's `ChangeXprop` interns an atom (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`); a bare name deletes the property and leaves the window or its icon unnamed, which the app shows as its own name. The bytes are read by the property's type: ICCCM's two as ISO 8859-1, the `STRING` xterm writes them as ("STRING as a type ... specifies the ISO Latin-1 character set", ICCCM chapter 2), and EWMH's two as UTF-8, their `UTF8_STRING`, a value that is not UTF-8 dropped and counted (Jev, BY_PROPERTY_TYPE, 0.97/1.00); never in hexadecimal, as `ChangeXprop` goes past `ChangeGroup`, whose XTSMTITLE modes it does not read. xterm gates it under `allowWindowOps`, subcategory `SetXprop`, so `[window] operations = "off"` refuses it and counts it in `Term::window_ops_refused`, and `OSC 61 ; allowWindowOps` names `SetXprop` only then (Jev's lean, FOLLOWS_WINDOW_OPS, 0.38/0.52 after a second ask) (`wm_name_names_the_window_in_iso_8859_1`, `net_wm_name_names_the_window_in_utf8`, `the_icon_name_properties_set_the_icon_name`, `a_bare_name_deletes_the_name`, `every_other_property_is_counted_and_dropped`, `the_hexadecimal_title_mode_does_not_apply`, `the_window_operations_setting_refuses_it`). Counted as unsupported before |
| `OSC 46 ; Pt` | change the log file | done — since 2026-10-20, **the file a program's log goes to** (the `?46` row): "Change Log File to Pt. The parameter Pt sets the logFile resource" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`). As xterm's: it only renames, so a log already started keeps its file until the next start; `?`, at which xterm rings, and an empty `Pt`, which its `do_osc` drops for want of data, change nothing and are counted, as is a name `OSC_MAX` may have cut, which would be another file (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`). A GR byte under ISO 8859-1 is its 7-bit byte here as in every `OSC` (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 3.5.4.5), as xterm's parser stores it too, so a Latin-1 `é` in a path is `i`; under UTF-8 the path's characters are kept whole (Jev, FOLD, 1.00/0.98, and 1.00/1.00 with the owner's "log to the file the program names" quoted, over reading the path as ISO 8859-1; `an_osc_46_path_in_gr_is_its_seven_bit_path_under_latin1`). Refused and counted while logging is off (`osc_46_names_the_file_the_next_start_opens`, `off_both_are_refused_and_counted`). Counted as unsupported before |
| `OSC 50 ; Pt` | set or query the font | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can ask which font is in use, switch to another font baud has loaded, or make the text bigger or smaller**, the owner's call of that day (option D, "set and query, on by default, a setting turns changes off"). "Set Font to Pt ... If Pt begins with a \"#\", index in the font menu, relative (if the next character is a plus or minus sign) or absolute. A number is expected but not required after the sign (the default is the current entry for relative, zero for absolute indexing). The same rule ... is used when querying the font" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`); the text's defaults are followed where xterm's `ParseShiftedFont` steps one for a bare sign and keeps the current entry for a bare `#` (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3759-3796). **The query** answers `OSC 50 ; name` in xterm's `QueryFontRequest` shape (3798-3846), with the query's terminator: since 2026-10-22 the name a program last set for the face that leads, as it sent it, the text's own reading, since under renderFont the control "sets/queries the faceName resource" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), and as xterm answers the face name its `setFaceName` stored (`getFaceName`, `[[source: xterm-fontutils]]` 3453-3489) and mintty the name `win_change_font` stored (`[[source: mintty-wintext-0aea388]]` 1120-1154, answered by `[[source: mintty-termout-0aea388]]` 4984-5001), so a program's name sent back finds the same face (Jev, Q_AS_SET, 0.94/0.94 after a re-ask; `the_query_answers_the_name_a_program_set`); the answer is only ever a name baud took, one that matches a loaded face's own name by full case folding and nothing else, 64 bytes at most, so it carries no text a program chose beyond a spelling of that name; for a face no program has named, the family of the face that leads the chain, from its `name` table (nameID 16, else 1, `[[source: ms-opentype-name]]`), so the default stack answers "Proto Hebrew Mono C", the built font's name while its family name is open; `#0 ` before it when anything follows the `?` or the sign (xterm's `*buf != 0`), and `OSC 50` with no `;` for an entry baud has not. A core no embedder told is silent. **What the index means**, baud having no font menu: entry 0 is the configured size, `#+n` and `#-n` step the size `n` points within 6 to 72, a step past them refused where the View menu's Bigger and Smaller clamp, and on Linux, which has no size keys, `n` of the configured pixel size, and entries 1-7 are none (Jev's lean, SIZE_STEPS, 0.22/0.18 after a re-ask; xterm's TrueType path never resizes through `OSC 50`); a query of a relative step names no numbered entry and is answered empty (Jev, EMPTY, 0.91/0.91). **A name resolves to a face baud has loaded** (Jev's lean, OWN_FACES, 0.79/0.85 after a re-ask), the embedded ones and the configured ones (the system faces the default stack names too), through the lookup a config's `fonts` names take, since 2026-10-22: a face's PostScript name (nameID 6), else its full name (nameID 4) where that is none of its own family names, else a family name of a face in the fallback chain, any of its nameID 16 and 1 records, each by the default caseless match CSS Fonts gives a family name (`[[source: css-fonts-3]]` 5.1), so `OSC 50 ; ProtoHebrewMonoC-Bold` finds the embedded Bold (`a_program_names_a_face_by_its_postscript_or_full_name`); until then only a family name did. The face named leads the chain as the regular face, the rest follow in order, and its family's other styled faces come with it, a styled face named alone having no style beside itself. Entries 1-7, an empty `Pt`, the current entry with no name and a name past 64 bytes change nothing and are counted in the core (`Term::fonts_refused`); a family baud has not loaded and a step past the bounds change nothing and are counted too, by the app through `Term::refuse_font_request`, and said at info (mintty and xterm hand the name to the system's lookup, `[[source: mintty-termout-0aea388]]`). Both apps restack the renderer (`Renderer::restack`) and re-lay the grid as a zoom does. `[font] changes = "off"` refuses every change and keeps the query answered, where xterm's `allowFontOps` off refuses both by default; `OSC 60` names `allowFontOps` and `OSC 61` says `SetFont` while changes are off. **Open departure**: a query after a change in the same read names the font from before it, since the embedder performs the change after the read (`a_query_after_a_change_in_one_read_names_the_font_before_it`). Counted as unsupported before (`osc_50_is_acted_on_not_counted`, `the_query_names_the_font_in_use_in_xterms_shape`, `a_stepped_font_has_no_numbered_entry`, `a_change_is_queued_for_the_embedder`, `a_change_that_cannot_be_made_is_refused_and_counted`, `a_change_the_embedder_refuses_is_counted`, `a_change_the_app_cannot_make_is_counted_in_the_core`, `naming_the_face_in_force_replaces_a_waiting_one`, `with_changes_off_a_change_is_refused_and_the_query_answered`, `the_feature_queries_say_what_osc_50_does`, `osc_50_is_the_same_at_every_split`, `each_form_parses_as_the_text_gives_it`, `a_change_is_planned_whole`, `a_restack_takes_the_new_faces_metrics_and_shapes_afresh`, `jetbrains_mono_names_its_family`) |
| `OSC 13`, `OSC 14`, `OSC 113`, `OSC 114` | pointer foreground and background colours | **out** — the system draws the pointer: macOS's cursors take no colour, and neither do the Linux driver's, named shapes the compositor draws (`cursor-shape-v1`), so a colour set would be drawn nowhere and a query would name a colour nobody sees. A pointer baud draws itself is open. Counted, and an `OSC 12 ; c ; c` walks past them (`a_colour_baud_does_not_keep_is_counted_and_the_walk_goes_on`). Decided by Jev, 0.98 and 0.97, over drawing baud's own pointer and storing without drawing |
| `OSC 15`, `OSC 16`, `OSC 18`, `OSC 115`, `OSC 116`, `OSC 118` | Tektronix foreground, background and cursor colours | **out** — they colour the Tektronix window, which baud does not have (Tektronix 4014, below, out). Counted, and walked past in an `OSC 10`-family list |
| `OSC 21 ; Pt` | DECSWT, set window title (VT520) | **out** — DECSWT is the VT520's ("Ps = 2 1 -> Change Window Title to Pt (DECSWT), VT520", `[[source: ctlseqs]]`; williams: "present on the multisession VT520 and VT525 terminals. Earlier terminals treat OSC in the same way as PM and APC"), baud identifies as a VT102, and xterm ignores it below a VT520 (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 4172-4177). **Since 2026-10-22 the number is kitty's colour control** (the `OSC 21 ; key=value` row), as kitty takes it whatever the body (`[[source: kitty-6d5d0c4-vt-parser]]` 609-611) and ghostty does (`[[source: ghostty-f54340a-osc-kitty-color]]`), so a title sent as DECSWT is read as a record of a key baud does not know, sets no title and is counted, as it was counted before (`a_title_under_osc_21_sets_no_title_and_is_counted`; Jev's lean, N_KITTY, 0.46/0.55 and 0.27/0.34 after research, under the bar). Note AK, section 1.4 |
| `OSC 51 ; Pt` | reserved for Emacs | **out** — "reserved for Emacs shell" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), with no function defined; xterm's `do_osc` does nothing with it. Counted |
| `CSI 2 i`, `CSI ? 2 i`, `CSI ? 8 i` / `CSI ? 9 i`, `CSI 10 i` / `CSI 11 i` | MC's screen to host, graphics to host, printer-to-host session, xterm's HTML and SVG dumps | **Refused because answering is unsafe**, part of the option the owner took (TODO.md, 2026-10-20: "CSI 2 i, printer-to-host and xterm's dumps stay refused"): `CSI 2 i`, "Sends screen data through host port" (`[[source: vt510-rm-b]]` p. 5-178), would type the screen back into the program's input, the reason the title reports are refused; `CSI ? 2 i`, "Graphics to Host" (`[[source: vt3xx-gp-002]]` ch. 16), would send a ReGIS hard copy's image of the screen to the program instead of the printer, refused for the same reason since 2026-10-21 (Jev's lean, PRINTER_ONLY, 0.61/0.72); a printer-to-host session relays a printer's input to the host and baud's print panel sends nothing back; the dumps write files a program names (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`). Each counted in `Term::print_refusals`, apart from the unknown (`the_refused_kinds_are_counted_apart_from_the_unknown`). **Out and counted as unsupported**, each decided by Jev on 2026-10-20: `CSI ? 18 i` and `CSI ? 19 i`, which assign and release the printer between two sessions, and baud has one (COUNT, 0.86/0.88 then 0.94/0.98); 070-7 § 7.8's graphics printing was out with them until 2026-10-21 (OUT_RECORDED, 1.00/0.98), and is built now under the standing rule (the ReGIS row); the VT510's printer-type and printed-data-type selections (DECSPRTT, DECSDPT and the ProPrinter forms, `[[source: vt510-rm-b]]` Table 4-9), which configure a serial printer's protocol where baud's printer takes UTF-8 (OUT, 1.00/1.00); `CSI 6 i` and `CSI 7 i`, the locator row's (070-13's locator controller mode); the VT510's MC page reads them as printer-to-host, "functionally equivalent to CSI ? 8 i and CSI ? 9 i", and under either reading they stay refused and counted, in `unsupported`. |
| Tektronix 4014 | vector graphics in xterm's second terminal personality | **out** — taken lean, 2026-10-20 (TODO.md; note AW, section 7): no DEC text defines it, and xterm runs it as "standard Tektronix 4014 control sequences" in a second window with its own emulator and graphics-input mode (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 3245-3300; `[[source: xterm-man]]`, `tekInhibit`), a second terminal rather than a protocol of this one. ReGIS, the DEC vector language, is in, in Tier 3 |
| `DCS Pc $ w ... ST` | DECLKD locator key definitions | done — since 2026-10-21 a program can choose what each pointer button sends in ReGIS graphics input: `Ky/Std/Stu` hex pairs for a key's press and release, six bytes each, `Pc` 0 emptying every key first ("All button definition strings are empty (not the default)") and 1 only the keys redefined (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-13 § 5.4; `[[source: vt3xx-locator]]`'s DECLBD). The press comes first, as 070-13 and a real VT340's script load it (`[[source: vt340test-locatorprog]]`); the VT330/VT340 manual's own example and its separator sentence put the release first, a contradiction inside that manual, not followed. An empty string reports nothing and does not end a one-shot input (DECLKD note 2); the null button's `CSI 240 ~` is no key and stays (Jev's lean, KEEP_NULL, 0.78 and 0.92, a real VT340's test leaving the question open). Read as DECUDK's loading reads one (070-11 `FILL_KEYS`; decided by Jev): a key past 4 skips its definition (SKIP_DEFINITION, 0.96 and 0.94), a broken hex pair stops the rest (STOP, 0.96 and 0.98), a string past six bytes is not stored and stops the rest (UDK_OVERFLOW, 0.92 and 0.94), a `Pc` past 1 refuses the string, each counted (`Term::locator_keys_refused`). `RIS` restores the defaults and `DECSTR` keeps the keys, as the text says (`a_declkd_programs_a_keys_press_and_release`, `pc_0_empties_every_key_and_an_empty_key_does_not_end_one_shot`, `pc_1_clears_only_the_keys_redefined`, `ris_restores_the_keys_and_decstr_keeps_them`, `a_bad_declkd_is_refused_as_decudk_refuses_one`). Until then it was counted and ignored, "waiting on the ReGIS row" |
| `CSI 6 i` / `CSI 7 i` | locator controller mode | **out** — the Locator Port's other control (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-13 § 1.2, § 7). Controller mode passes the host's bytes "directly to the locator port", a "guideline-documented exception" for a serial mouse or tablet on a terminal's own port; baud's pointer is the window system's, with no port to talk to. Counted (`locator_controller_mode_is_counted`) |
| National replacement character set mode | DECNRCM, `CSI ? 42 h` and `l` | Not recognised (`PrivateMode::Other`; DECRQM answers 0), and the national sets above are translated in every mode. **Not built because DEC's texts disagree on what it does**, the gap on the "Translating the national replacement sets" row: in the VT220 and VT420 manuals it makes the national sets available; in `[[source: dec-std-070]]` (pp. 5-126-5-127) it restricts the terminal to 7 bits and re-initialises G0-G3, to the keyboard dialect's NRC set under typewriter keys and to ASCII under data processing keys, and it "is ignored when the North American or Dutch Keyboard Dialect is selected" -- and baud has no keyboard dialect. xterm gates designation on it (`[[source: xterm-reset]]` `charproc.c:1929`, `need_nrc`), practice. Under (b) the bit would gate the sets, a change to what applications see today; under (c) it would be ignored if no dialect is read as North American; recorded under (a), as built, nothing would read it. Note AF's Q-DECNRCM, for Jev. This row said the sets were "Superseded by UTF-8" while the rows above translated fourteen of them, and until 2026-10-12 that ungated translation "departs from DEC's gate", citing STD 070 §5.8.1 note 4, which points at DECNRCM ("See DECNRCM") and states no gate of its own. |
| `CSI ? 1040 h/l` | keepSelection, "Keep selection even if not highlighted" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`) | done — since 2026-10-22 **a selection's text survives output that writes over it**. Set at power-on, xterm's default ("The default is \"true\"", `[[source: xterm-man]]`; Jev, SET_AT_POWER_ON, 0.95/0.98): output that writes or erases on a selected row takes the highlight away, as before, and the text the selection held stays for Cmd-C and Ctrl+Shift+C until another selection is made, as xterm's `ScrnDisownSelection` keeps it while the resource is set (`[[source: xterm-screen]]`); the text is taken before the first read after the selection is made, where xterm's `SaltTextAway` takes it as the selection is made (`[[source: xterm-button]]`), and a selection made between two reads replaces what was kept (`output_over_a_selection_leaves_its_text_while_1040_is_set`, `the_kept_text_is_the_last_selections`, `a_selection_dismissed_between_reads_replaces_the_kept_text`). **Since 2026-10-22 the text kept is the one the selection was made with**: a scroll that carries or trims the selection, or the end of the history that clips it, moves only its ends, as xterm's truncating `adjustHiliteOnFwdScroll` and `adjustHiliteOnBakScroll` move only `startH` and `endH` (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]` 460-525), and Cmd-C and Ctrl+Shift+C copy that text while the selection is up (`a_trimmed_selection_copies_the_text_it_was_made_with`); a selection the end of the history clips away leaves its text to copy, as xterm disowns a selection scrolled off its margins (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]` 482-490 and 502-506) and its disowning under keepSelection takes the highlight alone (`a_selection_the_history_clips_away_copies_the_text_it_was_made_with`); and a click that makes no selection takes the highlight and leaves the text to copy, as xterm's press takes the highlight (`StartSelect`) and its release disowns nothing (`SelectSet` on a null selection, whose `ScrnDisownSelection` finds no highlight). Until then a trimmed selection's text was taken again, trimmed, and a selection clipped away or a click left nothing to copy. Reset, the text goes with the highlight, and a scroll that would trim the selection drops it, as xterm's reset disowns it where its set truncates it (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]`, `adjustHiliteOnFwdScroll` and `adjustHiliteOnBakScroll`): across a region's edge, pushed out of a region or under the screen, across a top region's bottom, off the alternate screen's top, and off the primary's top into the history; `IL` and `DL` trim in either mode, as `InsertLine` and `DeleteLine` read no keepSelection (`while_keep_selection_is_reset_a_trimming_scroll_drops_the_selection`, `while_1040_is_reset_a_selection_scrolled_off_the_top_goes`). DECRQM answers it, XTSAVE keeps it in slot 60 and DECTSR carries it, the report's version 2, `DECSTR` keeps it and `RIS` sets it (`keep_selection_is_set_at_power_on_and_answers_decrqm`, `each_switch_sets_resets_saves_and_restores`). Reset, output that takes the selection gives up what SELECT offered on Linux, the primary selection or under `?1041` the clipboard, as xterm's `DisownSelection` gives up each selection it owns (`XtDisownSelection`, `[[source: xterm-button]]`), and only while the source the selection's copy set still offers it (`output_that_takes_the_selection_while_1040_is_reset_gives_up_select`, `a_selection_is_given_up_only_while_it_is_offered`); since 2026-10-22, where it kept offering the text. Open: the macOS pasteboard a copy-on-select wrote keeps the text, since a pasteboard holds a copy where X offers one and emptying it would lose what was copied; and a switch of screens, `RIS` or a resize dropping the selection gives nothing up, xterm's handling of those not read; and a selection the history's end clips away gives nothing up, where xterm's disowning there, `ScrnDisownSelection` with the resource reset, gives up SELECT (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]` 482-490 and 502-506). Two departures, open: set, `ICH`, `DCH`, `DECIC` and `DECDC` on a selected row take the highlight away where xterm's `InsertChar`, `DeleteChar` and `xtermColScroll` shift it -- one column for `ICH` and `DCH` whatever their count, and by the count for every end whose row is in the top and bottom margins for `DECIC` and `DECDC` (Jev, XTERM_ONE 0.94/0.98 and XTERM_ROWS 1.00/1.00) -- which waits on the owner, since a shift keeps a highlight the owner's answer A to B23 has go (Jev leaned that a shift is within that answer, 0.66/0.90 then 0.82/0.90, under the bar); reset, xterm disowns the selection on any scroll that is not full-line (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]` 451-456 and 553-559), so a scroll under side margins drops it wherever it lies, where baud drops it only when the rectangle's rows hold it, in either mode. Until 2026-10-22 `?1040` was not recognised and no text was kept. The highlight goes on a write or an erase on a row of the selection, the owner's answer A to B23, 2026-10-17 (`every_writer_reaches_the_row`, `a_write_or_an_erase_on_a_selected_row_drops_it`). For text, `EL`, `ED`, `ECH`, `ICH`, `DCH`, `DECIC` and `DECDC` that is what xterm does with the resource reset: `WriteText`, and `ClearInLine` under `ClearLeft`, `ClearRight`, `ClearLine`, `ClearAbove` and `ClearBelow`, disown always, and `InsertChar`, `DeleteChar` and `xtermColScroll` only while it is reset, shifting the selection while it is set (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]`). A joined mark, `DECALN`, `DECSEL` and `DECSED` drop it as xterm disowns it, by the rows they touch: `ScrnUpdate` on the mark's row and over the rows `ScrnFillRectangle` fills (`[[source: xterm-screen]]`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`), `ClearInLine` row by row (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]`). xterm's `ClearScreen` -- `ED 2`, `DECSED 2` with nothing protected, `ED 0` at home, `ED 1` at the last cell, and the clear for `?3` -- disowns with no row test, a selection wholly in the history too; baud keeps that one, whose text the clear leaves, as kitty does (`[[source: kitty-screen-c]]`, its erase in display): Jev's lean, 0.72 and 0.86, and re-asked with kitty's and alacritty's practice 0.58 and 0.78 (the record, "A selection is numbered by the lines the surface moved"). A redraw mark's erase and `CSI ? 3 J` on the saved lines drop a selection on the lines they erase (`a_redraw_mark_reaches_the_rows_of_a_block_on_the_screen`, `a_watch_on_saved_lines_is_reached_where_their_text_goes`). **A scroll carries the selection with its text**, since 2026-10-20, as xterm's `adjustHiliteOnFwdScroll` and `adjustHiliteOnBakScroll` do with the resource set (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]`; Jev's decisions of that day): a full-width region's scroll moves it and trims the lines it takes out of the region, keeping the part inside of one across the region's edge; a scroll down trims what it pushes under the screen; the alternate screen's top trims what leaves it at that scroll; the rows under a top region's scroll keep theirs; `IL` and `DL` move it below the cursor and drop it where the cursor's row or a deleted row is in it, as xterm's `InsertLine` and `DeleteLine` disown it (`a_scroll_carries_a_selection_with_its_text_at_every_read_size`, `a_copying_scroll_up_carries_the_regions_rows_and_only_those`, `il_carries_the_rows_from_the_cursor_down`). A scroll under side margins still drops it, as xterm disowns it there. Until then each of these dropped it. |

---

## A source conflict, recorded

`[[source: vt510-decawm]]` gives DECAWM's default as **"No autowrap"**.
`[[source: ctlseqs]]` documents xterm, where autowrap is on, and every modern
terminal wraps. baud follows xterm: an application emitting a long line expects
it to wrap, and the DEC default would break essentially all of them. The older
and stricter reading is the one baud does **not** implement, and that is a
choice rather than an oversight.

**DECPEX's default** (`?19`) is "Scrolling region" in `[[source: vt420-rm]]`
p. 210 and `[[source: vt510-rm-b]]` p. 5-81, and xterm's `printerExtent` is
false; the VT420's own Table 11-5 marks the page "(D)", and
`[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s `host_state_init` sets `PRINT_DISPLAY`, from NVM.
baud resets it, Jev's lean (SCROLLING_REGION, after WHOLE_PAGE 0.40/0.04 and
SCROLLING_REGION 0.20/0.54 on a second ask). And **the underline sent to a
printer** is SGR 4 in STD 070 § 7.4.2 and SGR 2 in the VT420's p. 215, which
ECMA-48 makes faint; baud sends 4.

## Why these decisions went the way they did

The reasoning — the refusals, the source conflicts, the things that look like
gaps and are not — is in [protocol-decisions.md](protocol-decisions.md). It was
in this file, 180 lines below the last table, where nobody scanning for an
operation's status was ever going to find it.

---

## How this file stays true

`baud-core/tests/coverage.rs` reads this file and fails if a row marked **done**
names an operation no test mentions. Run by `devenv test`, so it gates CI.

Be precise about what that proves, because this paragraph was itself the
failure it warns about for most of the project's life: it named
`tests/coverage.rs` while **no such file existed**, so the guarantee was
enforced by nothing.

* The test **does** prove every `done` row is mentioned by some test. Marking a
  row done with no test at all fails.
* It **cannot** prove the test would fail if the operation regressed. Nothing
  mechanical can. That stays a review obligation, and it is stated here as one
  rather than dressed up as a guarantee.

A weaker check that is real beats a stronger claim that is not. Documentation
claiming coverage it does not have is the failure mode this file exists to
prevent — the README claimed "~22 operations" while three of them were parsed
and dropped.

### An operation is done on *both screens*, or it is not done

`EL` and `ED` sat at **done**, with tests, and did **nothing at all** on the
alternate screen in `baud-core2`'s `OpMode::Runs`. That core's `erase_line`
had a span-truncation fast path that read the `promoted` flag raw; that flag belonged to
the primary screen, so on
the alternate one it was false for every row, the erase took the span path, and
it returned without touching a cell. `HELLO WORLD` survived an erase-to-end
from column 6.

Every erase test in `baud-core2` ran on the primary. So did every scrolling
test — full-screen scrolling in that core's alternate screen was copying rows
for the same reason, at 26x the cost.

**The enumeration was complete in the wrong dimension.** This file lists the
operations and `coverage.rs` checks each is mentioned; neither asks *in which
state* it was exercised. The alternate screen is not an operation and so has no
row here, but it changes the behaviour of the ones that do — in `baud-core2`
it was where the run table stopped applying — and every full-screen
application spends its whole life there.

Two gates came from that in `baud-core2`, and neither survives as it was
written: the run table and the op modes went with that core (bcefaae), and
this one has neither. Here each screen is a surface of its own -- cells,
extents, ring and rotation -- and `set_alt_screen` swaps the view onto the
other (`View::swap_surface`), so an operation reaches whichever screen is up
through the one view. What stands now:

* `erases_on_the_alternate_screen_are_not_swallowed_by_the_span_path`
  (`baud-core/tests/vt100/scrolling.rs`) runs six erases on the alternate
  screen, once each: there is one mode, and what it pins is that the surface
  erased is the surface shown. core2 ran each in `Computed` as well as `Runs`.
* core2's `screen_isolation.rs` required every function touching its run
  table to be screen-aware, structurally, because a wrongly zeroed run count
  showed through no public accessor. It was deleted with that core, and this
  file then named `baud-core/tests/xterm/alt_screen.rs` in its place, which
  holds no structural check. A write through to the parked primary, or a
  primary back under a wrong rotation, shows in its rows, so the checks read
  rows. A wrong extent does not, until something trusts it: with
  `View::swap_surface` swapping no extents, every row of
  `a_scrolled_primary_comes_back_in_the_order_it_left`'s trip compared
  equal, and the test caught it through what trusts the extents -- the
  history read back, the erase, and an exit that indexed past the screen.
  `a_round_trip_through_the_alternate_screen_preserves_the_primary` and
  `index_on_the_alternate_screen_leaves_the_primary_intact` scroll the
  alternate screen over a primary that never scrolled, so they fail when the
  primary comes back under the alternate's rotation and pass when it comes
  back reset to zero, the rotation it started from.
  `a_scrolled_primary_comes_back_in_the_order_it_left` scrolls the primary
  first, to rotations of 24, 33, 11, 111 and 57 (the last three over
  history), then crosses `?1049` and `?47` and holds every row, the history
  and the cursor to a twin that never switched, at six ways of cutting the
  reads; then it erases rows 42-50 on both and compares again, for the
  extents.

The reviewer's question for a new operation is therefore not only "is there a
test" but "does it run where applications will run it": on the alternate
screen as well as the primary, and over a primary that has scrolled as well
as one that has not.
